# Blackmagic eGPU under Linux on iMac19,1 — findings (2026-11-05)

For the next person (possibly us, later, with fresh eyes). Everything below was measured on the machine, not theorized.

## Setup
- iMac19,1 (2019 27"), Kubuntu 26.04, kernel 7.0.0-14-generic
- Blackmagic eGPU, Radeon Pro 580 (Ellesmere, rev c0), subsystem `Blackmagic Design Device a14c`
- Thunderbolt device authorized permanently (enrolled) — connects itself at boot

## What works
- Enumeration: card appears at `44:00.0` on the Thunderbolt bus (internal Ellesmere GPU at `01:00.0`)
- amdgpu probe gets deep: IP blocks discovered (sdma, smu, dce, uvd, vce), **VBIOS fetched from ROM BAR** (`113-C94002A1XTA-004`), firmwares load, UVD/VCE enable
- Under **macOS** the enclosure works perfectly — verified empirically 2026-11-05 evening: posts and drives a second monitor over its HDMI output with no fuss (its designed pairing)

## The wall (conclusive on this host)
- Probe dies at memory init: `Problem resizing BAR0 (-22)` → `sw_init of IP block <gmc_v8_0> failed -19` → `Fatal error during GPU init`
- `/sys/bus/pci/devices/0000:44:00.0/resource`: **BAR0–BAR4 all `0x0` (never assigned)**. Only BAR5 (0x88b00000, 256 KB) and the ROM BAR (0x88b60000, 128 KB) received addresses.
- Mechanism: Apple's firmware/ACPI gives the Thunderbolt bridge chain a prefetch window with no room for a ≥256 MB BAR0. The card, the tunnel, and the driver are all healthy; the address space simply isn't there.
- Side evidence: `ACPI VFCT table present but broken (too short #2), skipping`

## Tried and ruled out (all end at the same -22)
1. Hot-plug: authorize mid-session, manual bind via `/sys/bus/pci/drivers/amdgpu/bind`
2. Presence at cold boot (enrolled, attached from power-on)
3. `pci=realloc` in the GRUB cmdline — **verified live** via `/proc/cmdline`; BAR0 still 0x0. (Also caused a Kubuntu-logo boot stall once; since removed. `reboot=pci` remains in the GRUB line for the unrelated warm-reboot hang.)

## Untested levers (future research — not attempted 2026-11-05)
1. **`pci=hpmemsize=` / `pci=hpiosize=`** — kernel hotplug bridge window sizing; the TB bridges are hotplug-managed, so this family is untested, not exhausted. Long shot.
2. **DSDT override enlarging the Thunderbolt host bridge `_CRS` prefetch window** — has real precedent: the Windows Boot Camp eGPU community's "large memory" DSDT mod fixes Error 12, which is this same starvation under a different OS. Linux can inject a patched AML via initrd/GRUB. High-effort, machine-specific; keep a known-good boot entry before attempting.
3. **Runtime register surgery with `setpci`** — skirt the firmware entirely: from the running system, hand-program the Thunderbolt chain's bridge prefetch windows into genuinely free address space (recon first: `/proc/iomem`), write BAR0's address, enable memory decode, bind amdgpu. Runtime-only — a reboot erases it, so the trial itself carries zero persistent risk; worst case is a hard hang and a power cycle. The chain is only as wide as its narrowest window, so the root port's window may have to move too.
4. **Pre-boot window programming (rEFInd / EFI-stage)** — the old DIY-eGPU playbook (nando4's Setup era): reallocate/compact PCI resources before the OS loads. Heavier machinery, persistent config, real boot-risk — last resort.
5. **VFIO/QEMU passthrough (added 2026-11-06, the author's find)** — a Framework-community guide (NixOS host, kernel 6.12) runs THIS EXACT CARD (Ellesmere rev c0, subsystem a14c, IDs 1002:67df + 1002:aaf0) passed through via vfio-pci to an Ubuntu QEMU guest, where guest amdgpu drives it with working HDMI output. On their (normal PC) firmware the host assigned BARs fine — amdgpu bound on the host too; only display output misbehaved. Guide nuggets worth banking: disable ROM BAR, attach ONLY the VGA function (not USB/HDMI-audio), auto-authorize the TB device via udev. Transfer caveat for the iMac: VFIO changes who drives the card and who PICKS the BAR addresses (guest OVMF), but guest-chosen addresses still have to route across the host's Thunderbolt bridge window — Apple's cramped window is one floor BELOW where VFIO operates. This door lives only if the recon finds the TB bridge window is actually ≥256 MB and merely unallocated, so QEMU can place the guest BAR inside it. Guide: https://community.frame.work/t/guide-nixos-blackmagic-rx-580-580x-egpu-inside-qemu/66829
6. A future kernel/BIOS-side change in how TB windows are claimed — nothing on the horizon as of this writing.

## Verdict as of 2026-11-05
eGPU = macOS-side only on the 19,1. Wednesday's Linux GPU remains the internal card (4 GB). A macOS llama.cpp/Metal bench using the eGPU is still an open plan item (checklist Phase 1 addendum).
The author's call: publish this map so the next iMac19,1 owner starts here instead of at zero.

## Update 2026-11-08 — internet scan + EFI shim spec
- A deep-research scan (report: `~/workspace/research_notes/efi-preboot-pci-bar-egpu-20261108-2150/report.md`) found **no public precedent for the exact EFI-shim idea** (pre-boot bridge-window/BAR reallocation for a TB eGPU, any machine, any OS). Closest analogues: **t2-gmux-uefi** (chainloaded UEFI loader rewriting BARs + bridge windows on T2 MacBook Pros, internal GPU, Windows only, single-author/uncorroborated), **t2-egpu-linux** (Mac mini 2018 + TB eGPU under Linux reportedly working via runtime setpci **plus a kernel resource-tree patch module** — the tree, not the registers, is what drivers check; single-author/uncorroborated), and a **Mar 2026 LKML thread** documenting this identical failure on a Mac mini 2018, unresolved, with a maintainer pointing at new in-kernel PCI resource patches (incl. a v7.1 fix for Linux shrinking TB bridge windows on rescan).
- **Platform correction (the author, 2026-11-08, reaffirming 2026-11-05): the iMac19,1 has NO T2 chip** (nor T1; the regular iMac line had no Apple security chip until 2020). All donors above are T2-generation machines — their numbers do NOT transfer; the 19,1's windows must be measured on the 19,1. Upside: no T2 Startup Security gate stands in a chainloaded shim's way.
- Lever 4 (pre-boot) now has a full spec: `blackmagic-egpu-efi-shim-spec.md` in this folder — v0.1 is a **read-only EFI logger** (zero config-space writes; dumps PCI tree, bridge windows, EFI memory map, and ACPI tables from a USB boot, then chainloads GRUB), designed to answer: is the card visible at EFI stage, what is the exact bridge chain, how big is the root-bridge window, and does a free ≥256 MB-aligned gap exist. Sequencing unchanged: Linux-side recon sheet first (still unrun), logger second, placement plan third, write attempts only after a runtime cross-check.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (evening) — egpushim logger v0.1 FIRST HARDWARE RUN: Q1–Q4 answered

Log: `egpushim-20261108-224243.log` (uploaded by the author; run 22:42:43 MST off the 128 GB SD card in the built-in SDXC slot; logger ran blind — no console on the 5K panel — wrote logs, chainloaded GRUB, Kubuntu booted normally).

- **Q1 — Card visible at EFI stage: YES.** `EGPU_AT_EFI_STAGE: YES`, audio YES. Genuine Blackmagic unit = `0B:00.0 1002:67DF subsys A14C:BDBD`, link Gen3 x4, behind chain 00:1C.4 → 05:00.0 (1578) → 06:01.0 (15EA) → 09:00.0 (15EF) → 0A:01.0 (15EF). At EFI stage firmware bus numbers are compact; Linux renumbers (card = 44:00.0 there).
- **Q2 — Chain mapped completely** (above). Internal Ellesmere twin at 01:00.0 (subsys 106B Apple) is what confused the logger's marker; real eGPU identified by A14C subsystem.
- **Q3 — The wall, measured at firmware stage:** eGPU command register = 0000 (decode + bus-master fully OFF), BAR0/BAR2 address bits = 0 (never assigned); only BAR5 (0x81C00000) + ROM BAR (0x81C40000) placed, inside the last bridge's non-prefetch window. Root bridge apertures: mem 0x80000000–0x8FAFFFFF (251 MiB), pref 0x90000000–0xBE2FFFFF (739 MiB), all 32-bit (chain prefUpper = 0 everywhere). The zeroing happens in firmware, before any OS — Linux inherits it.
- **Q4 — No free ≥256 MB aligned hole below 4 GB.** Pref space is fully carved: iGPU 0x90000000–0x9FFFFFFF (256 MiB), internal GPU window 0xA0000000–0xB01FFFFF (258 MiB), TB branches from 0xB0300000 up (1 MiB sliver at 0xB0200000 is the largest gap). The only room big enough is ABOVE 4 GB (RAM map extends to ~0x900000000), which means a write-version shim must manufacture an above-4 GB prefetch window down the whole chain (prefUpper registers exist on every bridge) — exactly the t2-egpu-linux placement strategy, done at EFI time instead of runtime.
- **Logger v0.1.1 fix list (cosmetic, no data lost):** (1) eGPU marker should require subsystem A14C so it stops flagging the internal 67DF; (2) SSDT dumps overwrote a single `acpi-SSDT.aml` filename (12 SSDTs logged by address+length; only the last file survives) — number them; (3) DSDT was not dumped on real hardware — grab it (and the SSDTs) from Linux `/sys/firmware/acpi/tables/` instead, 10-second job, already on the recon sheet.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (later) — DSDT decompiled: the above-4 GB window exists on paper, is firmware-gated at runtime

Tables pulled from Kubuntu `/sys/firmware/acpi/tables/` (DSDT 30,355 B + all 12 SSDTs; SSDT sizes match the EFI logger's log exactly — same tables at both stages). Decompiled with iasl; tables + .dsl kept in the goal's `hidden_files/acpi-from-linux-20261108/`.

- `\_SB.PCI0._CRS` (BUF0) is DYNAMIC. Its big 32-bit producer window (_Y0D) is computed at evaluation time: `_MIN = TLUD << 20` (TLUD = top of low usable DRAM). With TLUD = 0x800 that yields 0x80000000 — exactly the root-bridge mem base the EFI logger read. DSDT and logger corroborate each other.
- The template ALSO contains a genuine 64-bit QWordMemory slot (_Y0E). Its values are NOT static: `_CRS` fills `_MIN = M64B`, `_LEN = M64L`, `_MAX = M64B+M64L-1`, and if `M64L == 0` it zeroes the length (window vanishes). M64B/M64L are 64-bit fields in an OperationRegion defined in SSDT6 — firmware-written at boot. So Apple firmware decides per-boot whether a >4 GB host window is advertised at all.
- Read on the evidence so far: the logger's root-bridge resources included a zero-length prefetch slot — consistent with M64L = 0 on that boot (no >4 GB window advertised). Not yet kernel-confirmed: the deciding check is Kubuntu's own record (`dmesg` root-bus-resource lines for a `[mem ... 64bit pref]` window ≥ 0x100000000, eGPU attached). Requested from the author 2026-11-08.
- Design consequence if M64L = 0 confirmed: an EFI shim placing BAR0 above 4 GB would ALSO need Linux to accept resources outside the advertised _CRS (the t2-egpu-linux kernel-tree problem), or a companion ACPI override advertising the window (findings lever 2). If M64L != 0 with a roomy window, the pure-shim route stays clean. This is the fork the v0.2 plan must be written around.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (16:01) — KERNEL-CONFIRMED: the 256 GB above-4 GB window IS advertised (M64L != 0)

Kubuntu `dmesg` (same boot chain as the logger run, eGPU attached):

```
pci_bus 0000:00: root bus resource [mem 0x80000000-0xfeafffff window]
pci_bus 0000:00: root bus resource [mem 0x4000000000-0x7fffffffff window]
```

- The QWord slot (_Y0E) evaluates LIVE: **M64B = 0x4000000000, M64L = 0x4000000000 (256 GiB window at the 256 GiB mark)**. Identical numbers to the t2-egpu-linux Mac mini 2018 host window — Apple's M64 template is shared across these Coffee Lake Macs.
- CORRECTION to the previous addendum: the inference "logger's zero-length prefetch slot suggests M64L = 0" was WRONG. Apple's EFI root-bridge protocol decode simply does not surface the 64-bit _CRS window; the kernel's ACPI evaluation is the authoritative record. Good thing the check was run before the plan forked on it.
- **Design consequence: the clean pure-shim route is ON.** Linux already believes the root bridge owns 0x4000000000-0x7fffffffff. A shim that (a) opens prefetch windows (incl. upper-32 registers) down the chain 00:1C.4 → 05:00.0 → 06:01.0 → 09:00.0 → 0A:01.0 into that range and (b) assigns the GPU's BAR0/BAR2 inside it places resources the kernel's tree already has a parent for — no kernel patching, no DSDT override required by anything known so far. Reference placement from the runtime precedent: 0x4010000000. Plan detail to settle in v0.2: BAR0 sizing vs amdgpu's resize behaviour (the original -22 was a resize failure on an unassigned BAR; the Mac mini runtime fix paired placement with rebar off).

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (16:05) — Placement plan drafted on paper (spec §7 satisfied)

`files/egpu-shim-v0.2-placement-plan.md` — the hand-derived baseline the v0.2 planner must reproduce. Headline decisions: **WIDE geometry** — bridge prefetch window `0x4000000000–0x42001FFFFF` on all five chain bridges; BAR0 at the root-window base `0x4000000000` (native 256 MiB; an amdgpu resize to full 8 GiB still fits inside the window, turning the original -22 failure mode into a success path); BAR2 at `0x4200000000` (expected 2 MiB, probe-verified later). Blast radius: v0.3 = 20 window-register writes, v0.4 = 4 BAR writes + sizing probes; GPU command register never written; non-prefetch space untouched. Narrow precedent-shaped variant kept as documented fallback. Nothing built or authorized by the plan itself.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (16:26) — v0.2 GATE PASSED on real hardware

Planner log `egpushim-20261108-232142.log` (run 16:21 MST, card in built-in slot, blind run, chainload into Kubuntu clean). Verified by scripted diff:
- Header `0.2.0-planner`, `EGPU_AT_EFI_STAGE: YES` (audio YES), marker fired ONLY on the Blackmagic functions; internal twin got the "(control sample)" line. v0.1 marker flaw confirmed fixed on hardware.
- `PLAN chain leaf->root: 0A:01.0 09:00.0 06:01.0 05:00.0 00:1C.4` — exact.
- **All 24 PLAN register entries: new values match paper plan §3 with ZERO mismatches; old values also match the v0.1 log's recorded state** (incl. the disabled-window FFF1/0001 + FFFFFFFF-upper pattern on 09:00.0/0A:01.0 and the GPU's zero BAR addresses). Window summary line: base 0x4000000000, end 0x42001FFFFF, BAR2 base 0x4200000000.
- SSDT dumps: 12 logged (numbering in filenames — the card now holds acpi-SSDT-01..12.aml separately; count matches).
- NO ReBAR line anywhere in the log — the extended-capability walk found no 0x0015 capability on the eGPU (nor on any device), consistent with v0.1. Reading: this Ellesmere does not expose a ReBAR capability to the EFI-stage walk, so amdgpu's original "resize BAR0" failure was the driver attempting resize of an UNASSIGNED BAR (resize machinery failing on address 0), not evidence of a resizeable BAR waiting at 8 GiB. Plan impact: LOW — WIDE geometry is indifferent: if a resize is ever attempted and supported it fits; if unsupported, BAR0 simply stays 256 MiB. R2 in the plan doc downgraded accordingly (paper plan to be annotated).

Ladder state: v0.2 gate (spec §8) PASSED. Next per spec: Gate A runtime cross-check (optional-informative) and/or v0.3 spec/build — both need the author's explicit go; v0.3 is the first WRITE build.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (16:34) — GATE A RUN: hardware accepts the plan; the kernel's cached tree rejects it (as predicted)

Linux chain (sysfs readlink): `00:1c.4 → 05:00.0 → 06:04.0 → 42:00.0 → 43:01.0 → 44:00.0` — Linux renumbers the TB domain vs EFI (06:01.0/09:00.0/0A:01.0/0B:00.0 there); same 5 bridges, same endpoints. No driver was bound to the GPU at baseline; resource file matched all prior evidence; dmesg baseline includes `Probably bad vram size: 0xff801000` (driver reading garbage from the unassigned BAR).

- **Phase 2 (setpci writes + readback): COMPLETE PASS.** Originals saved to `~/gateA-originals.txt` on the iMac. After writes, all five bridges read back `0001/0011/00000040/00000042` and the GPU BARs read back `0000000c/00000040/0000000c/00000042`. First attempt's GPU write appeared not to stick and the D3cold theory was tested and KILLED (power_state D0, runtime_status active); with power/control pinned `on`, the same writes verified immediately. Cause of the first non-stick unproven; current-state verification is what counts.
- **Phase 3 (remove + rescan): the predicted rejection, verbatim from dmesg:** `BAR 0 [mem size 0x10000000 64bit pref]: can't assign; no space`, same for BAR 2 — the kernel re-probed, sized both BARs correctly, and tried to allocate them from its **boot-time cached** parent-bus windows (which contain no prefetch space), discarding the register values we'd set. amdgpu then re-probed and died identically (`bad vram size`, resize -22, gmc -19, Fatal). Side effect: ROM resource released and not reassigned in-tree (VBIOS came from the VFCT ACPI table instead); reboot restores the stock baseline.
- **Bonus confirmations:** the kernel's own sizing probe reports BAR0 = 256 MiB and **BAR2 = 2 MiB** — the paper plan's inferred BAR2 size is now kernel-measured fact.
- **Conclusion:** Gate A isolated the variable. Registers right + tree stale = fail. The runtime route would need the t2-egpu-linux kernel-tree patch; the EFI route puts the values in place BEFORE the tree is built, so the tree is born containing them. Gate A therefore strengthens, not weakens, the v0.3 case. Per the plan, Gate A was informative-only and held no veto.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (16:43) — v0.3 WRITER built and verified offline; hardware gate run pending

Kit: `files/egpu-shim-writer-v0.3.zip` (62,659 B); BOOTX64.EFI 108,150 B, SHA-256 `a6af10b8201bfb9f4f375d819090af55f69cd8bc304951a46d8144dbf696b9a9` (extraction-verified). The project's **first write-capable build**, authorized by the author after the v0.2 gate passed and Gate A proved the values live.

- **What it writes:** ONLY the 20 prefetch-window registers of the 5 chain bridges (plan §3a), leaf→root, under the plan §5 protocol: live originals logged (WSAVE) and flushed before the first write; read-back after every write (WWRITE); first mismatch restores ALL originals root→leaf (WRESTORE) and logs `WRITER_RESULT: ABORT_RESTORED`, chainloading anyway; success logs `WRITER_RESULT: APPLIED entries=20`. GPU BARs stay plan-only (v0.4); command register never written.
- **Arming:** `plan_write_gate()` in plan.c — ARMED only when `WRITE_ENABLE=1` in shim.cfg AND a full plan computed; even then the writer refuses unless the filtered write list has exactly 20 entries. The kit's shim.cfg ships ARMED (WRITE_ENABLE=1), flagged in its first comment block and the run sheet's first lines; deleting the line returns the binary to exact v0.2 planner behaviour. Source audit: exactly ONE `Pci.Write` call exists (in `pci_write()`), reachable only via `pci_write_entry()` ← `do_write()`; the write list filter keeps only offsets 24/26/28/2C on non-GPU devices.
- **Tests:** host suite **25/25** (13 v0.2 checks unchanged + 12 new: write list exactly 20, leaf→root order/groups, values == §3a, no GPU entries, offsets-only, full gate truth table, card-removed never ARMED) — re-run green from a clean extraction of the shipped zip, extracted binary hash == build tree. QEMU/OVMF: A (default cfg) → `CFG: WRITE_ENABLE=0`, planner NOT-FOUND, `WRITER: SKIPPED - WRITE_ENABLE is not 1`, HELLOCHAIN; B (armed cfg, no card) → `CFG: WRITE_ENABLE=1`, `WRITER: SKIPPED - no valid plan was computed`, HELLOCHAIN; zero WSAVE/WWRITE lines in either log.
- **Honest limits:** the ARMED path (SAVE/WRITE/RESTORE) has never executed anywhere — QEMU cannot present a Blackmagic-subsystem device; it is covered by host-tested pure logic + the single-call-site audit. First execution = the author's hardware run, which is the v0.3 gate: 3 clean boots, Linux-side setpci readback showing the wide windows on all five bridges (Linux BDFs 00:1c.4/05:00.0/06:04.0/42:00.0/43:01.0), card otherwise unchanged.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (16:53) — v0.3 FIRST HARDWARE RUN: WRITER APPLIED 20/20; root port keeps the window in Linux, TB bridges reverted by something in the Linux boot

Log `egpushim-20261108-234918.log` (he saw console text this run — panel initialized): `WRITER: ARMED`, SAVE phase recorded **pure firmware values** for all 20 registers (root port old = B031/BE21/0/0 — so Gate A's runtime writes did NOT survive the reboot; the wide root-port value seen in Linux below is the writer's own work from this boot). All 20 WWRITE lines read back OK. **`WRITER_RESULT: APPLIED entries=20`.** Chainload clean.

Post-boot Linux check (setpci reads, Linux numbering): root port `00:1c.4` = `0001/0011/00000040/00000042` — **the wide window survived into a running Linux**. The four Thunderbolt bridges (`05:00.0`, `06:04.0`, `42:00.0`, `43:01.0`) read `fff1/0001/00000000/00000000` — the window-disabled encoding. Conclusion so far: the writes crossed the EFI→OS boundary; something inside the Linux boot (kernel bridge fitting and/or the Thunderbolt stack) re-disabled the TB bridges' prefetch windows while leaving the Intel root port untouched. v0.3's gate question therefore narrows to kernel behaviour, answerable from this boot's dmesg (bus resource lines at scan vs assignment/teardown later) — requested, pending. Whether amdgpu fared any differently this boot is also still unchecked.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (16:55) — the kernel's first scan REFUSED the wide windows (tree was never born wide)

This boot's dmesg, at scan time (t≈0.28 s): `pci_bus 0000:42: resource 2 [mem size 0x00200000 64bit pref]` — a SIZE-ONLY placeholder (the 2 MiB is BAR2's requirement showing through), with NO address range, while the same bus's io and mem resources carry real ranges. So despite all five bridges holding `0x4000000000–0x42001FFFFF` in hardware when GRUB handed over, the kernel did not import the prefetch window into the TB buses' resource lists at scan; the later register state (TB bridges disabled, root port — imported via a different path — still wide) follows from that. The refusal happens at import/read time in the kernel's bridge-window handling for these TB buses, not as a later teardown. amdgpu remained unbound on 44:00.0 this boot. (His "saw text" was the EFI console on the iMac panel, which initialized this run.) Full bus-by-bus resource picture + /proc/iomem still being pulled to find WHICH bridge in the chain breaks the import first.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (16:56) — BREAK LOCALIZED: host-side windows imported wide; the hop into the enclosure failed on containment + a port the shim never wrote

Full scan picture from the v0.3 boot's dmesg (t≈0.283 s): root bus resource 8 = `[mem 0x4000000000-0x7fffffffff window]`; **bus 05 resource 2 = `[mem 0x4000000000-0x42001fffff 64bit pref]` and bus 06 resource 2 identical — the shim's wide windows WERE imported for the host-side bridges (00:1c.4's and 05:00.0's writes).** Bus 42 resource 2 stayed size-only. The GPU subtree (buses 43/44, device 44:00.0) only enumerated at **t≈29.1 s** (Thunderbolt authorization), where three assignment passes failed BAR 0/2 against bus 42's resourceless pref slot, and amdgpu died as usual.

Mechanism that fits every number (kernel containment rule + connection-manager port divergence):
1. Apple's EFI routes the PCIe tunnel through host-switch downstream port **06:01.0**; Linux's ICM routes it through **06:04.0** (Gate A's sysfs chain vs the EFI chain). The shim wrote the EFI chain — including 06:01.0, verified — and never touched 06:04.0, whose registers kept firmware's below-4 GB pref window `0xB7310000–0xBE21FFFF`.
2. The kernel imports a bridge's window into the child bus ONLY if contained in the parent bus's resource. Bus 06's pref resource was now the WIDE window, which does not contain 06:04.0's below-4 GB window → bus 42's pref import was dropped entirely (size-only placeholder). In a stock boot the same window IS contained (parent = firmware's B031–BE21) and imports normally — the shim's widening of the parent is precisely what orphaned the unwritten port's window.
Fix direction (v0.3.1, windows-only, one variable): the writer additionally applies the same wide pref window to any sibling downstream port of the same TB switch (same parent 05:00.0, 8086:15EA) that is not in the GPU chain and has no endpoints in its subtree — at EFI stage that is 06:04.0, head of the empty reserved tree (buses 66–122). Confirming probes queued: `lspci -tv` topology in Linux, and a stock-boot dmesg comparison (prediction: bus 42 resource 2 = [mem 0xb7310000-0xbe21ffff pref] there).

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (17:06) — STOCK-BOOT COMPARISON: bus-42 prediction WRONG; true gatekeeper found (kernel hotplug bridge sizing)

Stock boot (no shim): buses 05/06 resource 2 = firmware `[mem 0xb0300000-0xbe2fffff pref]` as predicted, **but bus 42 resource 2 = `[mem size 0x00200000 64bit pref]` — size-only AGAIN.** The 16:56 containment explanation for bus 42 is therefore DEAD: its pref window fails to materialize in every boot, shim or not. Post-boot registers in stock: root port keeps firmware values; **all four TB bridges read the disabled pattern — the kernel rewrites them post-fit in stock boots too** (so the shim boot's identical register state was never a teardown of our values; it's the stock end-state whenever GPU assignment fails).

Revised mechanism, fitting all data: the TB downstream ports are **hotplug bridges**, and the kernel sizes their downstream windows itself (children's needs + hotplug reserves) instead of importing firmware windows. The 2 MiB size-only placeholder matches the kernel's default hotplug memory reserve (`pci=hpmemsize` default 2 MiB). At the ~29 s authorization hot-add, assignment walks from bus 42's placeholder and fails — in stock because ancestors offer only ~226 MiB total, under the shim DESPITE buses 05/06 holding the wide window, because re-opening an already-sized hotplug bridge window requires reallocation permission. **Untested combination:** `pci=realloc` (tried alone on 2026-11-05, useless with no headroom above) TOGETHER with the shim's wide windows (headroom now exists). Next experiment: v0.3 shim boot + one-time `pci=realloc` on the GRUB linux line.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (17:15) — shim + pci=realloc: bus 42's placeholder became a REAL window — but only 2 MiB

Kernel 7.0.0-42 (photo-verified GRUB edit: `… quiet splash pci=realloc`). Scan line changed for the first time all day: `pci_bus 0000:42: resource 2 [mem 0x4000200000-0x40003fffff 64bit pref]` — realloc let the kernel place bus 42's hotplug reserve as a real 2 MiB window **inside the shim's wide space** (offset +2 MiB from the window base). Proof the combination works mechanically. But at the 28.6 s hot-add, BAR 0/2 still failed: bus 42's window was sized at boot (GPU absent) = child needs (0) + 2 MiB reserve, and the hot-add path does not grow an already-sized ancestor window. The choke point is now bus 42's window SIZE at boot time. Next lever, same run shape: `pci=realloc,hpmemsize=512M` — force every hotplug bridge's boot-time window ≥512 MiB (the wide parent has 8 GiB; nesting costs alignment only), so BAR0 (256 MiB) + BAR2 (2 MiB) fit when the GPU arrives.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (17:21) — **BAR 0 AND BAR 2 ASSIGNED** (first time ever on this machine); amdgpu's BAR-resize is the last blocker

Shim + `pci=realloc,hpmemsize=512M` (GRUB edit photo-verified): bus 42 resource 2 = `[mem 0x4358c00000-0x44b13fffff 64bit pref]` (~5.6 GiB real window inside the wide space). At the 29.5 s hot-add: **`BAR 0 [mem 0x4360000000-0x436fffffff 64bit pref]: assigned`** and **`BAR 2 [mem 0x4370000000-0x43701fffff 64bit pref]: assigned`**, BAR 5 assigned (BAR 4 IO failed — normal/irrelevant). Then amdgpu probed and did its day-one move with a new twist: it RELEASED both BARs and tried to resize BAR 0 to **4 GiB** (`size 0x100000000`, full-VRAM aperture) — allocation failed because bus 44's window was sized for 258 MiB at assignment time and pci_resize_resource cannot re-open ancestor sizing; kernel restored the old BAR values ("old value restored"), but the driver still ended unbound (the original `Problem resizing BAR0` failure, now occurring AFTER a successful assignment). Next lever, one variable: same combo + **`amdgpu.rebar=0`** — the 256 MiB BAR is the standard pre-ReBAR Polaris configuration; skip the resize, keep the assignment.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (17:27) — rebar=0: resize skipped, VRAM fully detected — init now dies ~10 s in, at a later IP stage

Shim + `pci=realloc,hpmemsize=512M amdgpu.rebar=0`: no resize lines (guard works). amdgpu on 44:00.0 progressed further than ever: `VRAM: 4096M … (4096M used)`, `Detected VRAM RAM=4096M, BAR=256M`, VRAM + 15984M GTT ready, PCIe GART enabled, Chained IB enabled, UVD firmware found (the cosmetic `Probably bad vram size: 0xff801000` still prints but no longer stops anything). Then a ~10.5 s silence (29.307 → 39.796) and `amdgpu_device_ip_init failed` / `Fatal error during GPU init` / `probe … failed with error -22`. The failing IP stage sits inside that gap (SMU/VCE/display/firmware-load territory; a timeout-shaped hole). Full untruncated 44:00.0 sequence requested to identify the exact block.

This boot's assignments: BAR 0 `0x4020000000–0x402fffffff`, BAR 2 `0x4030000000–0x40301fffff`, BAR 5 + ROM + audio BAR assigned; only BAR 4 (IO) failed. ## Addendum 2026-11-08 (17:31) — CORRECTION (the author's): the Blackmagic card is 8 GB; the driver's "4096M" is a misdetection tied to the garbage size read

The author flagged that the eGPU still shows 4096M VRAM although the external card is an **8 GB** card. He is right on the product: the Blackmagic eGPU is a Radeon Pro 580 **8 GB**; the internal iMac Ellesmere is the 4 GB card (its identical `VRAM: 4096M` line is correct there). On the eGPU the figure is wrong — exactly half. The driver's own warning is the fingerprint: `Probably bad vram size: 0xff801000`, whose low field encodes 4096 under garbage high bits (0xff80…). The driver distrusted the read, fell back to 4096M, and proceeded — so the mis-size alone doesn't explain the later silent hang (several stages completed at 4096M), but it puts the register-read/POST path and the SMU/memory side at the top of the suspect list alongside Display Core. Earlier docs that implied the external card's size from the log are corrected by this note: external = 8 GB, detected = 4 GB, cause unknown (register-read integrity through the tunnel vs card POST state under this boot flow). Next bisect boot adds both disjoint guards: `amdgpu.dc=0 amdgpu.dpm=0`.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (17:44) — dc=0 + dpm=0 boot: journal proves the eGPU failed identically (-22); dc/dpm EXONERATED; the boot also wedged the console

The `amdgpu.dc=0 amdgpu.dpm=0` boot hung the graphical console (main screen stuck on splash with a garbled strip, VT switch dead; power-hold recovery; stock boot normal after — nothing persisted). Journal of that boot (`journalctl -b -1`) survived: the eGPU's ending is the standard one — `finishing device`, `probe with driver amdgpu failed with error -22`. So with Display Core bypassed AND dynamic power management off, the probe still fails the same way: **the silent waiter is in neither subsystem.** Side lesson, recorded: `amdgpu.dc=0` applies to ALL amdgpu devices and broke the healthy internal card's console handoff — dc=0 is banned from future combos on this dual-Ellesmere machine. Remaining unified suspect for BOTH symptoms (garbage CONFIG_MEMSIZE read 0xff801000; ~10 s silent wait): PCIe link power state over the tunnel (note the link printed at 2.5 GT/s in Linux vs Gen3 at EFI stage). Next test: working trio + `pcie_aspm=off`, no dc/dpm.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (17:54) — ASPM off: identical failure; the wait is a fixed ~10.4 s constant across boots

Shim + trio + `pcie_aspm=off`: UVD firmware found 26.360 → ip_init failed 36.775, Δ = 10.42 s, probe -22, same as before. ASPM exonerated (with DC and DPM). Notable: the silent interval is reproducible to ±0.1 s across independent boots (10.49 s, 10.42 s) — a named timeout constant, not a race. Prime candidate: amdgpu's default 10 s lockup/fence timeout. Discriminating test next: `amdgpu.lockup_timeout=60000` — if the hole stretches toward 60 s (or the operation completes inside it), the wait is a fence path; if it stays 10.4 s, the wait is a different fixed poll (SMU/firmware handshake family).

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (18:00) — lockup_timeout boot failed DIFFERENTLY: instant, named — `sw_init of IP block <gmc_v8_0> failed -22`

With `amdgpu.lockup_timeout=60000` added, the probe died at 28.016 — ~4 ms after the VRAM/GART prints, with the block named: gmc_v8_0 sw_init, -22, right where the driver consumes the (garbage) VRAM-size read and the memory-config registers; the "Detected VRAM / RAM width" lines never printed. No 10 s wait occurred, so the lockup-timeout question is moot for this boot (the parameter doesn't touch sw_init logic; this looks like per-boot variance in the card's POST/register state — the same recipe family produced the 10.4 s unnamed failure three times and this instant named one once). Two failure modes now on record: (1) pass gmc, die silently at +10.4 s in a later unnamed stage; (2) die instantly in gmc sw_init -22. Next experiment targets POST determinism: full power-cycle of the ENCLOSURE (its PSU, 10 s unplugged), then the proven trio (realloc + hpmemsize + rebar=0), and observe which mode appears.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (18:07) — cold-cycle run INVALID as an experiment: the boot carried no kernel parameters (`Problem resizing BAR0` = stock signature)

The cold-enclosure boot failed with the day-one stock signature — `Problem resizing BAR0 (-22)` then `sw_init of IP block <gmc_v8_0> failed -19` — which only occurs when the ReBAR attempt is active, i.e. `amdgpu.rebar=0` (and by implication the whole trio) was NOT on this boot's command line; every verified rebar=0 boot lacks that line. The cold-cycle variable therefore remains untested with the trio. Process fix adopted: verify each experimental boot afterwards with `cat /proc/cmdline` so a missed GRUB edit can never again masquerade as a result.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (18:17) — cold card + verified-clean trio: Mode 1 again (Δ 10.42 s). POST hygiene exonerated; the wait is deterministic

Full enclosure cold-cycle, cmdline verified byte-clean (`pci=realloc,hpmemsize=512M amdgpu.rebar=0`): UVD firmware 34.2036 → ip_init failed 44.6198, Δ = 10.42 s, probe -22. Fourth occurrence of the unnamed 10.4 s mode; warm/cold POST makes no difference. The wait is a deterministic stage with a 10 s-class timeout that prints no IP-block name. Next step is instrumentation, not more parameters: boot the trio with dynamic debug for the amdgpu module (`dyndbg="module amdgpu +p"`) and read what the driver logs inside the silent window after "Found UVD firmware".

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (18:24) — THE WAITER IS NAMED: "SMU load firmware failed" — the 10.4 s hole is the SMU firmware-load timeout

The author's wider grep (the -A 60 window swept past the twin's UVD line into the eGPU's timeline) caught what every earlier capture missed, at 36.8547, Δ = 10.419 s after the eGPU's "Found UVD firmware" (26.4358): `amdgpu: SMU load firmware failed` / `fw load failed` / `smu firmware loading failed` → ip_init failed → -22. The driver spends the whole silent interval trying to upload the SMC firmware blob (polaris10_smc) into the card's System Management Unit and the SMU never acknowledges. Why it stayed "unnamed" all night: these lines carry NO device prefix (plain `amdgpu:`), so our `grep "0000:44:00"` filter structurally hid them — they were almost certainly present in every Mode-1 boot. Also visible in this capture: the internal twin's full successful init for contrast (UVD/VCE initialized, KFD node added, fbcon primary), and the eGPU subtree enumerating at 26.39 with the link again reported limited by a 2.5 GT/s x4 link at 06:04.0. Next: compare both cards' SMU/SMC lines (`dmesg | grep -iE "smu|smc"`) and search the exact error string against Thunderbolt/eGPU cases.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (18:26) — LKML precedent found: Apple VI ASICs need a reset-on-init quirk, else "SMU load firmware failed … -22" — same disease; our card isn't in the table

Search hit (amd-gfx, Oct 2026): "[PATCH] drm/amdgpu: reset VI ASIC on MacBookPro14,3 with Radeon Pro 560" and the earlier commit a22e5e4f2ebb for MacBookPro15,1. `vi.c` has a `vi_reset_quirks[]` table keyed by {device, PCI_VENDOR_ID_APPLE subvendor, subsystem, revision}: Apple firmware leaves these VI cards in a state (framebuffer at MC address 0) inconsistent with the driver's re-POST, and without an ASIC reset on init the init derails — the thread names our exact corpse: "the SMU firmware load fails ('SMU load firmware failed', then 'GPU reset end with ret = -22')". With the quirk, the driver does a PCI config reset on load and init completes. Table entries cover Apple-subvendor (0x106B) Polaris11 parts; the Blackmagic card (67DF, subvendor BDBD, subsystem A14C, rev E7) is not in the table — but the mechanism suggests an equivalent by hand: since the failed probe leaves the device unbound with BARs still assigned, do a function-level reset from sysfs, then re-bind amdgpu so its POST runs on a freshly reset ASIC. No reboot needed. Also noted from a gfx803 field guide: wrong-vendor VBIOS memory straps produce the same SMU failure string — not our case (genuine Blackmagic VBIOS, correct card).

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (18:27) — sysfs FLR refused (ENOTTY); rebind probe ran anyway, SMU struggled ~78 s (message retries) and still failed

`echo 1 > …/44:00.0/reset` → "Inappropriate ioctl for device (os error 25)": no function-level reset occurred. A probe nevertheless ran (likely re-triggered by the reset attempt/udev; the bind write itself returned EINVAL, probably racing that auto-probe). Its SMU phase behaved DIFFERENTLY: five two-line `[powerplay]` message attempts at ~14.5 s intervals (continuation lines lost to the grep filter) spanning 302→379 s before "SMU load firmware failed" — the SMU was in a different state than the clean-boot case (one 10.4 s wait), but the load still failed. Next escalation: secondary bus reset via the parent downstream bridge 43:01.0's sysfs `reset` (bridges do SBR), then re-bind. Config/BARs are kernel-restored across SBR; the audio function rides along.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (18:31) — bridge sysfs reset also ENOTTY; manual probes consistently take the ~78 s retry path; escalating to raw setpci secondary-bus reset + remove/rescan

`echo 1 > …/43:01.0/reset` (the downstream bridge) also returned ENOTTY — both kernel-mediated reset methods are unavailable on this Thunderbolt topology. The auto-probe that followed repeated the ~78 s SMU pattern exactly (five [powerplay] attempts at ~14.5 s spacing, 459.77→537.64). Pattern note: boot-time probes fail the SMU load after a single ~10.4 s wait; every manually re-triggered probe takes the five-retry ~78 s path — same ending. Escalation chosen: raw secondary-bus reset via setpci (BRIDGE_CONTROL bit 6, value:mask form so other bits survive), with both GPU functions removed first and a bridge rescan after, letting the kernel rebuild the subtree (BAR assignment state from this boot's realloc+hpmemsize still applies) and amdgpu auto-probe a genuinely reset ASIC.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (18:34) — raw secondary-bus reset delivered; SMU load STILL fails: reset family fully exonerated; the fault is in the upload path itself

Remove ×2, BRIDGE_CONTROL read `0002`, SBR asserted (`40:40`) 1 s, released (`00:40`), rescan: subtree rebuilt, GPU re-enumerated (lspci shows it, still unbound), auto-probe ran 676.86→754.54 with the same five-retry SMU pattern → "SMU load firmware failed". Reset state is now exonerated from every direction: stone-cold enclosure power, warm boots, FLR attempt (refused), and a genuine bus reset all end at the same SMU refusal — so the failure lives in the SMC firmware UPLOAD path itself (SMC indirect-register writes/polls through the tunnel, or this VBIOS's SMU soft-register layout), not in the ASIC's starting state. Next datum needed: the unfiltered log slice of a failing probe to read the actual PPSMC message numbers the [powerplay] retries are failing on.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (18:36) — the five retries decoded: "last message was failed ret is 0" ×5 — the SMC answers NO message, ever

Unfiltered slice of the post-SBR probe: after "Found UVD firmware" (676.861), five attempts at 686.6 / 701.2 / 715.7 / 730.2 / 744.8 (~14.5 s apart) each print `last message was failed ret is 0`, then "SMU load firmware failed" (754.538). Meaning: `smu7_send_msg_to_smc` polls the SMC response register and reads a flat 0 every time — the System Management Unit does not answer a single message from the Linux driver, in any card state tried (cold power, warm boot, post-FLR-attempt, post-bus-reset, boot-time or manual probe). Everything the ASIC demonstrably does (config, POST/ASIC_Init, register writes, GART setup) is host-driven MMIO; no log line in any boot proves the card completed an operation autonomously, consistent with the SMC — which brings up the card's real power/clock tree — never coming up. Remaining untested hypotheses are no longer parameter-class: (a) the Thunderbolt tunnel's handling of the MP/SMC register block, (b) Blackmagic's Apple-channel SMC firmware expecting the macOS driver's handshake, (c) the enclosure's own controller gating the SMC domain. The MacBook reset-quirk route is ruled out by direct experiment (a genuine bus reset changes nothing). Cheap residual test whenever desired: different TB cable/port (30 s, long shot). Any further progress likely requires comparing the macOS driver's SMC conversation against Linux's — a reverse-engineering project, not a boot-parameter search.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (18:46) — REGISTER PROBE RESULTS: the Blackmagic's SMC is ALIVE and executing, parked in a loop, and never posts a response; FB location is the CORRECT value (MBP theory dead)

Read-only probe (BAR5 mmap; twin = baseline). Twin 01:00.0: SMC_RESP_0=0x1 (last response: success), FB_LOCATION=0xf4fff400, PC samples 0x2057c/0x23fd4/0x205f0 (roaming = working main loop). Blackmagic 44:00.0: SMC_RESP_0=0x00000000 (never responded — the driver's failure confirmed at register level), SMC_MESSAGE_0=0x254 / ARG_0=0x47e (residue of the driver's own five attempts — the writes DO land), FB_LOCATION=0xf4fff400 (identical to twin — the MacBook 0x00ff0000 fingerprint is ABSENT; that mechanism is ruled out), SYSCON_CLOCK_CNTL_0=0x01000000 (identical), SMC_PC_C=0x2df64/0x2df5c/0x2df5c — in the valid running range (≥0x20100, clock fine: passes the driver's own is_smc_ram_running test) but essentially PARKED at one address vs the twin's roaming PC. Conclusions: (1) the tunnel's MP/SMC register path reads and writes cleanly — tunnel-corruption theory dead; (2) the SMC core is powered, clocked, and executing firmware, stuck in a tight wait loop; (3) it never services the mailbox. The divergence is firmware state/behaviour on the card, not transport, not POST state.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (18:47) — THE MECHANISM FOUND: the driver SKIPS its own SMU start sequence because its "already running" test passes spuriously

Source (`polaris10_smumgr.c:polaris10_start_smu`): the upload+start dance runs ONLY `if (!smu7_is_smc_ram_running(hwmgr))`. The test is `ck_disable == 0 && SMC_PC_C >= 0x20100`. The probe measured the Blackmagic at ck_disable=0 (SYSCON 0x01000000, bit0=0) and PC=0x2DF5C — the test PASSES, so the driver concludes a proper SMC firmware is already running, skips the upload entirely, and proceeds to `smu7_request_smu_load_fw`, whose five mailbox messages the card's resident boot-stage code never services (RESP=0 forever). On reference cards the SMC's wait state sits below the PC threshold (or clock-disabled), so the dance runs. The author's EFI-impersonation proposal reframes accordingly: the missing half is probably not an Apple-secret handshake but the STANDARD start dance (reset → upload polaris10_smc.bin → jump → clock → de-reset → wait for interrupts-enabled) that Linux knows but never performs here. Fix forms, cheapest first: (1) perform the dance from userspace, then rebind; (2) patched amdgpu that ignores the heuristic for this device; (3) the elegant permanent form — EFI shim v0.4 performs the dance pre-boot so stock drivers find a genuinely running SMU. Next: probe round 2 (SMU_FIRMWARE mode/sel, RCU_UC_EVENTS boot_seq_done, FIRMWARE_FLAGS, SMU_STATUS) to determine which dance variant the card needs.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (18:49) — probe round 2: the Blackmagic's SMC state is IDENTICAL to the twin's on every status register — it fully self-started; only the response register and PC behaviour differ

Blackmagic vs twin, indirect reads — SMU_FIRMWARE 0x0003001e (protected=1, hard key=1), RCU_UC_EVENTS 0x000e0084 (boot_seq_done=1), FIRMWARE_FLAGS 0x1 (interrupts enabled), SMU_STATUS 0x3 (DONE=1, PASS=1): IDENTICAL on both cards. The Blackmagic's SMU firmware was loaded and passed its self-check without any host OS help (the card self-starts its SMC at power-up). Remaining deltas: SMC_RESP_0 (1 twin / 0 Blackmagic) and PC behaviour (twin roams 0x205xx↔0x23fxx; Blackmagic oscillates 0x2df5c↔0x2df64, a two-instruction wait loop). Hypothesis now targeted: the card's self-boot completes the protected-mode start through DONE/PASS but parks before operational service, awaiting the host-ownership tail of the dance (write FIRMWARE_FLAGS=0 → pulse SMC reset → wait FIRMWARE_FLAGS interrupts-enabled) that the macOS-side flow performs and Linux never does (Linux skips the whole start because its already-running test passes). Next experiment performs exactly that tail from userspace, then rebinds.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (18:51) — THE KICK WORKED at the SMC level: after the ownership tail, the Blackmagic's PC ROAMS like the twin's

`smckick.py` (clear FIRMWARE_FLAGS → pulse SMC_SYSCON_RESET_CNTL rst_reg 1→0 → wait for flags) changed the SMC's behaviour immediately and visibly: PC samples went from the parked 0x2df5c↔0x2df64 oscillation to roaming 0x2e000 / 0x2dea0 / 0x20198 / 0x202a0 / 0x2de98 / 0x2de9c — multiple code regions including the same low loop the twin idles in. SMU_STATUS remained 0x3 (DONE+PASS); RESP still 0, expected — no message has been sent since the kick; RESP only means something once the driver speaks. The host-ownership-tail hypothesis is confirmed at the register level: the SMC was waiting for exactly those steps. Next: rebind amdgpu into the awakened SMC and see whether the five load messages are finally answered.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (18:57) — manual Test message also unanswered (state B deaf); POST count = 2; escalating to the FULL protected dance with Linux's own SMC image

Manual `PPSMC_MSG_Test` (0x100, ARG 0x20000) sent from userspace in the post-kick state: writes landed (ARG reads back 0x20000), RESP stayed 0 through 5 s of polling, PC still parked at the second loop (0x2df24–2c). Separately, `grep -c "GPU posting now"` = 2 — the rebind did re-POST. Deafness is now established independent of POST and of which parked state the SMC sits in, so the resident Blackmagic SMC image itself is the suspect: it completes its self-check but never services the host mailbox in any state reachable by resets alone. Escalation: perform the complete protected-mode start from userspace with LINUX's `polaris10_smc.bin` (the image the twin runs), parsed and uploaded exactly as the driver does (header fields at 0x10/0x14/0x18/0x1C; SRAM base 0x20000; SMU7_SMC_SIZE limit), followed by the full trigger/status/flags sequence. If the SMC answers under Linux's image, the resident image was the wall; if not, the obstacle is below firmware.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (19:01) — full dance with polaris10_smc.bin FAILED at the bootloader: DONE never set; parse audit clears the script — image variant + upload readback are the open questions

Dance output: image parsed as version 0x171a00, size 129960 (fits, %4=0; the "start 0x177bb0b8" print was the header CRC at 0x1C, a script cosmetic bug — ucode_start_addr lives at 0x20; version/size/array-offset at 0x10/0x14/0x18 are confirmed correct against `amdgpu_ucode.h`, so the uploaded byte range was right). Result: RCU-interrupts wait TIMEOUT, Test RESP 0 (15 s), SMU_STATUS stayed 0x0 (DONE never set — the bootloader never validated/started the image), PASS 0, flags TIMEOUT, PC parked 0x2df24/2c. Two discriminators next: (1) the card's true PCI revision byte (sysfs `revision`) — if it matches ASICID_IS_P20, the driver would have chosen the KICKER image `polaris10_k_smc.bin`, not the plain one; (2) read back SMC SRAM at 0x20000 and compare with the uploaded payload head — the upload loop is the first step all night that relied on SMC_IND_ACCESS_CNTL auto-increment, and whether those 32k writes actually landed has never been verified.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (19:03) — revision is 0xc0 (plain image confirmed correct); SRAM readback MATCHES the payload — upload path verified; both images stall at the same park, pointing at boot ENVIRONMENT, not the image

Sysfs `revision` = 0xc0 (launch Ellesmere stepping; ASICID_IS_P20/P30 do not match, so the driver's own choice for this card is plain `polaris10_smc.bin` — the file used). SRAM verify: first 8 dwords at SMC 0x20000 are byte-identical to the uploaded payload head (`a6e46fc4 … 00171a00 …`) — the auto-increment upload landed perfectly. Key inference: the uploaded Linux image stalls at the SAME park (0x2df24/2c) as the resident Blackmagic image — two different images, identical stall, so the missing ingredient is environmental (state the firmware boots into), not the image. Prime suspect: choreography position — by the time userspace can act, the driver's own POST (ASIC_Init) and failed-probe teardown have already disturbed the ASIC/VRAM state the SMC boots against; the twin gets its dance in-kernel before any teardown. Best userspace approximation next: remove both functions → secondary-bus reset (virgin ASIC) → dance → rescan/rebind.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (19:10) — virgin-ASIC dance FAILED IDENTICALLY; choreography theory dead — twin control experiment is the fork

Remove → secondary-bus reset → rescan (driver held off via driver_override=none; verified unbound) → dance: same signature (RCU timeout, Test RESP 0, DONE never set, PC parked 0x2df24/2c). ASIC POST state is therefore NOT the missing ingredient — dances have now failed from three starting states (post-probe, post-kick, virgin). Side bug in the test sequence: `printf ''` (zero-byte write) did not clear driver_override, so the follow-up manual bind returned EINVAL and `dmesg` needs sudo on this system (dmesg_restrict). The decisive remaining discriminator is the control experiment: run the identical userspace dance on the known-good internal Ellesmere twin (scripted unbind → dance → rebind, logged to file). If it succeeds there, the procedure is validated and the Blackmagic's SMC boot environment is uniquely at fault; if it fails there too, the userspace dance itself is missing a step the in-kernel path performs.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (19:15) — correction: bind EINVAL is the probe's own -22 surfacing through the sysfs write, not driver_override

With the override verified cleared (`cat driver_override` → `(null)`), the manual bind STILL returned EINVAL — and the dmesg tail shows why: the bind write ran the probe synchronously in the writer's context (`tee` appears in the amdgpu_pci_probe → load_kms → unload_kms → fini WARNING traces, ending `probe with driver amdgpu failed with error -22`), and sysfs propagates the probe's error code back to the write. So every "Invalid argument" on bind tonight was the -22 itself, not a stale override; the earlier override theory is retracted. The irq_put WARNINGs are the failed probe's cleanup path, cosmetic. Probe outcome unchanged (-22), as expected given the preceding dance failed.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (19:46) — TWIN CONTROL VERDICT: the userspace dance fails on the known-good twin too — the PROCEDURE is defective; all dance-based Blackmagic inferences retracted

Timeline (~100 s from run start) matches the dance burning its full timeout stack on the twin (10 s RCU + 15 s Test + DONE/flags waits), i.e. the identical dance that "fails on the Blackmagic" also fails on a card whose in-kernel SMU start works, and leaves that card's SMC deaf to the stock probe until a cold reboot (hence the black screen; the twin recovered fully on the stock boot, as expected — all volatile). CONSEQUENCES: (1) every inference drawn from userspace dances (post-kick, virgin-ASIC) is RETRACTED as evidence about the Blackmagic — the instrument was never valid; (2) the SRAM-upload verification and revision/image facts stand (they are reads, not dance outcomes); (3) the one untouched fact remains the original one: the Blackmagic's SMC does not answer the completely stock driver (first failure 17:27, before any userspace tooling existed).
