## Addendum 2026-11-08 (19:50) — reset-hold test INVALID in a stock boot: every read returned 0xFFFFFFFF (dead bus), script false-positived "FROZEN"

In the straight stock boot (no shim, no realloc trio), the reset test read 0xFFFFFFFF for PC, RESET_CNTL, RESP, SMU_STATUS and FIRMWARE_FLAGS alike — the all-ones signature of PCIe transactions not being answered at all, i.e. BAR5 is unreachable because the bridge windows were never widened in this boot (the original BAR-assignment problem), despite sysfs reporting D0. The script's "FROZEN — reset reaches the core" verdict is VOID: it compared identical values without sanity-checking for the dead-bus pattern. The reset-hold test must run in a shim+trio boot where BARs are verifiably placed; the script needs an explicit 0xFFFFFFFF guard. Nothing about the reset question was decided by this run.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (19:58) — reset-hold test (valid run, trio boot): the reset DOES gate the core — PC freezes at 0x00000000 while asserted, roams on release

Baseline PC parked at loop A (0x2df5c/64). RESET_CNTL read 0x40000000 (rst_reg=0); the assert write landed (read back 0x40000001). While held, SMC_PC_C read 0x00000000 on all ten samples — the core halts in reset. On release the PC immediately roams the image (0x20180–0x2dff0) — the firmware reboots and executes. So the reset segment of the userspace dance is FUNCTIONAL; the dance's failure lies after release, in the bootloader/firmware acceptance of the uploaded image (DONE never re-set when we clear SMU_STATUS first). Caveat: this run did not clear SMU_STATUS, so its post-release 0x3 may be stale rather than a fresh bootloader verdict — the follow-up micro-test clears SMU_STATUS while held, releases, and watches DONE/RCU with the RESIDENT image, which decides whether warm-reboot validation happens at all outside a cold power-on.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (20:00) — warm reboot NEVER re-sets DONE (resident image); the PPSMC mailbox has never been observed live under Linux in ANY state

smcdone.py: cleared SMU_STATUS to 0 while the core was held (clear landed, PC=0), released with the untouched RESIDENT image. Over 5 s: SMU_STATUS stayed 0 (DONE never re-set), RCU_UC_EVENTS never raised INTERRUPTS_ENABLED, FIRMWARE_FLAGS never rose; the firmware rebooted and re-parked at loop B (0x2df24/2c). So DONE/PASS is a cold-power-on-only artifact on this card, and every warm-start gate the Linux dance watches is permanently dark after any reset. Synthesis of the hard facts: stock probe messages unanswered (17:27, pristine), RESP=0 in every state ever measured, zero RESP!=0 events across kicks, dances and bus resets — the mailbox has never been live under Linux. One universe remains untested: every observation so far happened AFTER the stock driver's auto-probe had already spoken its five load messages into the mailbox (residue 0x254/0x47e). A boot where amdgpu never speaks first (modprobe.blacklist=amdgpu, console on EFI framebuffer) allows a first-speaker test against the pristine self-booted firmware.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (20:14) — blacklist boot: BARs assigned (cmdline byte-perfect) but BAR5 reads dead because no driver ever called pci_enable_device — endpoint MEM decode is off

In the pristine boot (pci=realloc,hpmemsize=512M + modprobe.blacklist=amdgpu, verified byte-exact in /proc/cmdline), BAR0/BAR2 are assigned above 4 GB and BAR5 at 0x88b00000, yet every BAR5 read returns 0xFFFFFFFF. Difference vs the working trio boots: those had amdgpu loaded, and its (failed) probe still called pci_enable_device, setting the endpoint Command register's Memory Space bit; with the driver blacklisted nothing ever enables MEM decode, so the device does not answer BAR transactions. Userspace remedy: setpci COMMAND=0002 on 44:00.0 (config space — does not touch the SMC mailbox, so the first-speaker experiment stays pristine).

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (20:16) — BREAKTHROUGH FRAMING: in the pristine boot the SMC sits in its ROM download-wait state (RESP=1, RCU interrupts up, PC in ROM range) — every previous boot was poisoned by the driver's own POST starting the VBIOS-resident Apple firmware

With COMMAND=0002 set (MEM decode theory confirmed: was 0000), smcfirst.py found a card-state never seen before: mailbox pristine (MESSAGE=0, ARG=0) with **RESP=0x1 — the mailbox-ready signal the driver's send waits for and never once saw in driver boots**; RCU_UC_EVENTS=0x000f0080 (INTERRUPTS_ENABLED set); SMU_STATUS=0x0; PC executing in the low ROM range (0x2a3c–0x2b54), not the 0x2dfxx parks. Interpretation: with amdgpu blacklisted, nobody ran the ASIC POST, so the VBIOS's SMC image was never loaded/started — the SMC boot ROM is simply waiting for a host download, exactly like a normal card before its first probe. In every driver boot, the probe's POST starts the VBIOS-resident (Apple-channel) firmware, which then parks and never services the Linux mailbox; that, not tunnel/reset/image issues, is the wall. A bare Test message was the wrong first word for a download-waiting ROM (it trap-parked at PC 0x88 chasing an empty SRAM start); the correct first conversation is the full upload dance, whose leading reset recovers the ROM. FIRMWARE_FLAGS reads garbage (0x426328ba/0xaaaa5555) in this state — uninitialized pre-firmware space, disregard.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (20:21) — pristine-boot dance: RCU wait PASSED for the first time (0.1 s) — the ROM accepted the upload path; DONE/Test still dark; PC roams the ROM range

Full dance against the download-waiting ROM: after release, "RCU interrupts enabled" succeeded in 0.1 s (every poisoned-boot dance timed out at 10 s) — proof the ROM took the upload branch this time. But Test stayed unanswered (15 s), SMU DONE never set, PASS=0, and the final PC samples roam 0x28dc–0x2c8c (ROM space, below SRAM) — the ROM is looping in its own state machine, neither trap-parked (0x88) nor running the uploaded image. The tail's "flags ok after 0.0s" is the known garbage FLAGS pattern (bit 0 of 0xaaaa5555) — disregarded. Two live suspects: (1) upload integrity in the pre-POST state (indirect reads are visibly flaky there — the earlier SRAM MATCH was proven only in a POSTed boot), discriminated by a head/tail SRAM readback in this state; (2) image variant — this ROM may only validate a kicker image (polaris10_k_smc.bin), discriminated by re-dancing with it.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (20:24) — verify readback returns 0xAAAA5555 for every SRAM address — the indirect window itself is now answering with a fixed pattern, not data

smcverify2.py in the pristine boot (post-dance, post-session-reload): HEAD and TAIL SRAM reads alike return 0xaaaa5555 — the same pattern FIRMWARE_FLAGS showed in this boot. This is not upload corruption; the read path is degraded (SMC indirect engine unresponsive / block unclocked / device power state), in contrast to the live reads during smcfirst and the dance minutes earlier (real PC/RCU values, RCU wait passing). Next diagnostic separates direct BAR5 registers (MESSAGE/RESP/ARG — no indirect engine) from indirect ones, after re-asserting power/control=on: pattern confined to indirect reads indicts the SMC's indirect interface state; pattern everywhere indicts BAR5 power/link.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (20:27) — peek: register space alive (dance's Test residue intact), core trap-parked at PC 0x84, SRAM-space reads were the pattern — timing theory for the pristine dance's Test failure

smcpeek (after re-asserting power/control=on; power_state D0): DIRECT registers real (MESSAGE=0x100, ARG=0x20000 — the dance's Test send landed and was never consumed; RESP=0), INDIRECT registers real and stable (PC=0x84 both passes, RCU=0x000f0084, SMU_STATUS=0, SYSCON_CLK sane). So the 0xaaaa5555 episode is specific to SRAM-space reads while the SMC core sits trapped (0x84 now; 0x88 after smcfirst's Test) — the ASIC-hosted register interface stays up, the core-served SRAM window does not. New lead theory for why the pristine dance's Test went unanswered: the script wrote MESSAGE immediately after the RCU wait passed, while the driver's send first WAITS for RESP!=0 (mailbox armed) — if the ROM was still mid-validation, the early write was lost (residue: MESSAGE still sitting unconsumed). Dance v3 therefore mimics the driver's patience: poll RESP up to 30 s before writing, wait up to 30 s for the answer, verify the upload mid-dance while the core is held, and skip the reset-pulse tail when PASS fails (the tail only re-traps the core).

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (20:33) — dance v3: mailbox NEVER arms after a reset-release (30 s); RESP=1 has existed exactly once — at untouched power-on. New theory: in the pristine state the reset itself is the poison; the untested order is upload-without-reset, then Test

Dance v3 (patient protocol): reset held correctly (PC=0), upload completed, SRAM verify while held returned the 0xaaaa5555 pattern at both ends (the SRAM window is dark whenever the core is held/trapped — integrity neither proven nor refuted in this state). After release the RCU interrupts bit read set instantly (it is a static property of the ROM-wait state, not a validation signal — downgrade earlier "RCU ok" evidence accordingly). The mailbox then NEVER armed: RESP stayed 0 for the full 30 s, so the Test was never even sent. Final PC roams the ROM loop. Pattern across all experiments: RESP=1 existed only in the virgin power-on state before any host action; every reset spends it permanently, like DONE. Source check closed another door: smu7_request_smu_load_fw's DRAM-address messages load the AUX ucodes through an already-running SMC — not an alternate SMC start path. Next: kicker image under the reset protocol (this boot), then a re-armed boot (reboot restores the ROM's armed state — it did so for the 20:16 boot) running dance v4 "cold hands": no reset at all, upload while the ROM loops, then Test.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (20:39) — kicker under reset protocol: identical failure (variant eliminated). Reboot alone did NOT re-arm the ROM — SMC registers persist across warm host reboots (old Test residue intact, RESP still 0); re-arming requires an enclosure power-cycle

Kicker dance v3: same signature (mailbox never armed, DONE/PASS dark) — image variant eliminated under the reset protocol. The follow-up reboot into the blacklist line did not restore the armed state: smcpeek in the "new" boot shows the previous Test residue (MESSAGE=0x100, ARG=0x20000) still in the mailbox and RESP=0 — the enclosure holds its own power, so the SMC rides through warm host reboots with state intact. Dance v4 therefore ran un-pristine (its warning fired) and replicated the dark result; upload-without-reset changes nothing when the ROM is not armed. Also settled: SRAM-space reads return the 0xaaaa5555 pattern whenever the core executes ROM code (looping, held, or trapped) — that window is only served while real firmware runs, so in-ROM readbacks are meaningless by design. The deterministic re-arm is an enclosure power-cycle (SMC cold-boots from its own rail), then the blacklist boot, peek (gate: RESP=1 AND MESSAGE=0), then v4 immediately.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (20:49) — VIRGIN-STATE V4: TEST ANSWERED (RESP=1 in 0.1 s) AND DONE SET — first time ever; PASS=0 because the cold-hands upload itself is corrupted (verify MISMATCH with real values)

In the manufactured virgin state (enclosure power-cycle + blacklist boot; gate RESP=1/MESSAGE=0 confirmed), dance v4 (no reset): upload completed with RESP still armed; SRAM verify read REAL values but MISMATCHED the payload at head and tail; the Test was then answered — RESP=0x1 in 0.1 s, the first answered SMC message of the entire investigation — and SMU_STATUS showed DONE=1 (also a first) with PASS=0: the ROM ran its start validation and the image failed the check, consistent with the corrupted SRAM contents. The PC never left ROM space. So the virgin conversation works end-to-end (armed → upload → Test → DONE), and the remaining fault is upload integrity while the ROM core is live (reset-mode uploads are clean but the reset permanently disarms the ROM). Next: dance v5 in the still-armed state — dump the current SRAM shape, then upload with a full verify-and-repair loop until byte-clean, clear SMU_STATUS, send Test.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (20:51) — *** PASS=1: THE BLACKMAGIC'S SMC IS RUNNING LINUX'S STANDARD FIRMWARE *** (virgin-state v5: blast + explicit-rewrite storm, clear status, Test → SMU_STATUS=0x3, PC roaming SRAM)

Dance v5 in the armed state: every SRAM readback returned the 0xaaaa5555 pattern on all six passes (32,490/32,490 "mismatched" — the SRAM window is simply never host-readable while the core runs ROM; all pre-start readbacks in this investigation are void, including v4's "corruption"). The repair loop consequently rewrote EVERY dword via explicit index writes — and that is the plausible winning difference over v4's lone auto-increment blast (blast loses dwords against the live core; explicit writes land). After clearing SMU_STATUS and sending Test: **RESP=0x1 in 0.2 s, SMU_STATUS=0x3 — DONE and PASS both set** — and post-tail PC samples roam 0x20554–0x23fd0, the SRAM execution range, matching the healthy twin's PC neighbourhood (0x2057c/0x23fd4/0x205f0). The standard polaris10 SMC firmware is up on the Blackmagic for the first time. Recipe for the record: (1) enclosure power-cycle + no-POST boot (driver blacklisted) → virgin armed ROM (RESP=1); (2) COMMAND=0002 so BARs answer; (3) upload blast; (4) rewrite every dword explicitly; (5) clear SMU_STATUS; (6) Test (0x100, arg 0x20000) → DONE+PASS; (7) ownership tail. Remaining question: whether a manual `modprobe amdgpu` probe binds without its POST trampling the now-running firmware.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (20:54) — First probe with a live SMC: sails past VBIOS fetch + UVD/VCE firmware loads, dies at "PCI CONFIG reset → asic reset on init failed" — NO SMU timeout this time

With v5's standard SMC firmware running, a manual `modprobe amdgpu` probe of 44:00.0 progressed far beyond every previous attempt: IP blocks detected, the VFCT bus-number-mismatch workaround matched the card by device identity, VBIOS fetched from VFCT (ATOM BIOS 113-D0008A14GL-003), UVD enabled in VM mode, VCE firmware 53.26 found and enabled. Then: "PCI CONFIG reset" (442.165) → 0.57 s gap → "asic reset on init failed" → "Fatal error during GPU init" → -22. Crucially, no SMU firmware-load timeout appears — the probe never reached the step that used to kill it. New blocker: the driver decided the ASIC needs a reset on init (never-POSTed card: this boot had the driver blacklisted, so nothing ran ASIC_Init) and its config-reset success check failed. Next: read vi.c/amdgpu_device.c for need_reset_on_init's predicate (CONFIG_MEMSIZE is the prime suspect) and the reset flow, to find the lever (candidate: pre-write CONFIG_MEMSIZE via BAR5 before modprobe).

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (21:00, source-verified) — WHY the probe reset: vi_need_reset_on_init returns TRUE *because* the SMC is running (ck_disable==0 && PC >= 0x20100); the reset it performs is the config-0x7C magic write, which cannot complete over the TB path

vi.c (torvalds/linux master): `vi_need_reset_on_init` — after the Apple-subvendor FB_LOCATION branch (not us: subvendor is BDBD) and the reset-quirks table (three Apple 67EF entries only), the decisive check reads SYSCON_CLOCK_CNTL_0 and SMC_PC_C via the SMC index window: if the SMC clock is enabled AND PC >= 0x20100 (firmware executing from SRAM), it returns TRUE — a running SMC is precisely what makes the driver demand a reset-on-init so it can start from a known state. The reset (reset_method forced to default) is vi_asic_reset's "PCI CONFIG reset": `pci_write_config_dword(pdev, 0x7c, AMDGPU_ASIC_RESET_DATA)` plus a recovery poll — the poll failed after ~0.57 s in the 20:54 probe (over a Thunderbolt tunnel the config-reset either drops the device or its post-reset signature never appears on a never-POSTed card). So on normal machines the lifecycle is: POST leaves a cooperative VBIOS SMC running -> probe resets -> driver uploads its own SMC. Here BOTH nonstandard links are ours alone: the reset cannot complete over TB, and the resident VBIOS image ignores the mailbox. Counter-strategy (E1): present the SMC as NOT running at the reset check (gate its clock after v5, PC frozen in SRAM), then thaw ~1.5 s into the bind so the later SMU init sees a running, cooperative firmware and takes the already-running branch.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (21:01) — The config reset DID land: ASIC sits reset-and-unPOSTed, MMIO aperture dead until enclosure power-cycle; the SMC (and our firmware) survive it — fans still run

Post-probe diagnostics: config space fully alive (lspci shows 1002:67df rev c0), power_state D0, BAR5 still assigned at 0x88b00000, COMMAND=0002 set — yet every BAR5 read returns 0xFFFFFFFF. So the driver's "failed" config reset actually executed: it reset the ASIC, the driver's recovery poll then failed (its expected post-reset signature requires a POSTed card), the probe bailed before POST, and the ASIC now sits in its reset state with the internal register aperture down. The fans still spin: the SMC is a semi-independent block and keeps running our firmware through an ASIC config reset — unreachable only because the MMIO path to it is down. At enclosure power-on the aperture is alive by default (all pre-probe dances prove it), so the recovery is: enclosure power-cycle -> COMMAND=0002 -> peek (RESP=1 gate) -> dance v5 -> smcbind freeze/thaw. Standing lesson: after any probe that reaches the reset-on-init step, the card needs an enclosure power-cycle before userspace can talk to it again.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (21:03) — Power-cycle WITH a live TB link boots the VBIOS image (state A); the virgin download-wait requires the link DOWN during the SMC's boot window

After the reset-wedged probe, an enclosure power-cycle with the iMac fully booted (TB link up) produced classic state A, not the virgin state: PC parked at 0x2df5c/64, SMU_STATUS=0x3 (the SPI/VBIOS boot's own DONE+PASS), mailbox residue MESSAGE=0x254/ARG=0x47e, RESP=0. Contrast 20:48, where the cycle straddled an iMac reboot (link down while the SMC booted) and produced the virgin armed state (RESP=1, MESSAGE=0, PC in ROM loop). Rule: the ROM chooses download-wait vs SPI-boot by host-link presence during its boot window. Deterministic virgin recipe therefore = TB cable unplugged, enclosure power-cycle, wait, replug — with a driver_override set beforehand so the loaded amdgpu cannot auto-probe the hot-replugged device and spend the armed state.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (21:06) — Cable-out power-cycle did NOT reproduce the virgin state (state A again, bit-identical residue); both virgin sightings followed an iMac reboot into a blacklist boot — reverting to that proven recipe

The link-down hypothesis failed its test: TB unplugged, enclosure cycled, replugged (with driver_override=dummy set) — peek shows state A again with identical residue (MESSAGE=0x254, ARG=0x47e, PC 0x2df5c, SMU_STATUS=0x3). Reviewing the record, BOTH virgin sightings (20:12 and 20:48) share a different common factor: an iMac reboot into a driver-blacklisted boot with the enclosure left powered and untouched — plausibly the iMac's EFI/TB firmware resets or power-sequences the TB device during reboot in a way that leaves the ROM armed. The enclosure-cycle-with-OS-up path has now produced state A three times. Plan: replicate the proven recipe exactly (reboot, shim, trio+blacklist, no enclosure games), set driver_override before the dance so the manual modprobe cannot auto-probe the card, and use smcbind v2, which freezes the SMC clock, clears the override, and only then writes the bind — keeping the freeze under script control with no race.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (21:24) — Freeze/thaw bind: freeze verified (PC pinned at 0x20564) but the config reset fired ANYWAY; reset+POST completed this time, VRAM mis-sized (4096M, POST ran against the frozen SMC), probe died at the original SMU load timeout (~11 s hole in ip_init)

smcbind2 run: pre PC=0x23fd4 running; clock gate froze the PC solid; override cleared; bind -> -22. dmesg: "PCI CONFIG reset" printed anyway at probe start (freeze did not suppress it — his kernel 7.0's predicate may differ from master's, or another path ungates first), then unlike 20:54 the reset did NOT fail: "GPU posting now..." followed, GART/UVD/VCE all initialized. Two defects: (1) POST ran entirely while the SMC clock was gated and mis-sized the memory ("Probably bad vram size: 0xff801000" -> fell back to VRAM 4096M on an 8 GB card) — POST wants a live SMC; (2) after POST, a 10.97 s hole then amdgpu_device_ip_init failed — the classic SMU load timeout: by ip-init time the running core did not answer PPSMC, i.e. reset+POST replaced or disturbed the v5 firmware before the driver could use it (a mid-hole userspace PC read showed 0x23fd0, but that range cannot distinguish the images). Conclusion: userspace cannot sneak a prepared SMC past the stock probe's reset+POST. Next bisect: feed the stock SMU dance the one initial condition it has never had — the SMC held in reset (PC=0) at bind time, which also makes every variant of the reset predicate false; the dance's own reset handling then owns the core from a clean held start.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (21:28) — Hold-bind also fails (-22): the core problem is now fully cornered — the driver's SMU dance *begins with a reset*, and ANY reset permanently disarms this card's mailbox

Hold-bind: RESET_CNTL asserted from userspace, PC verified pinned at 0x0 across samples, bind still returned EINVAL. That closes the bisect matrix: virgin-running firmware (reset-on-init eats it), frozen firmware (reset fires anyway, POST disturbs it), held core (the dance's own reset-release leaves the ROM disarmed — the mailbox never re-arms after any reset, per the standing finding). No userspace-preparable state survives the stock probe, because smu7's start sequence itself opens with the one operation this card never forgives. Verdict: the stock driver cannot be negotiated with; the fix is a patched amdgpu: (1) skip vi_need_reset_on_init's config reset for subsystem BDBD:A14C, (2) gate the SMC clock across POST inside the driver (POST demonstrably tolerates a gated SMC), (3) replace the SMU start for this device with the v5 recipe — explicit-write upload, no reset anywhere, clear SMU_STATUS, Test trigger — valid only from the virgin armed state, which the boot choreography (enclosure cycle + reboot, module probing first) guarantees.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (21:32) — Build prerequisites all green; minimal patch locked (one guard in vi_need_reset_on_init)

Hold-bind dmesg tail confirms the same SMU timeout death (11.0 s hole in ip_init). Build environment on the iMac: kernel headers for 7.0.0-42 present, gcc 15.2.0, NO Secure Boot enforcement (unsigned module will load), 485 GB free, and linux-source-7.0.0 version 7.0.0-42.42 — the exact source of the running kernel — already installed. Patch design (source-verified against smu7_smumgr.c / polaris10_smumgr.c): `polaris10_start_smu` skips its reset-dance entirely when `smu7_is_smc_ram_running()` (ck_disable==0 && PC >= 0x20100) is true, and `smu7_request_smu_load_fw` then services the live firmware with plain PPSMC messages — so the ONLY change needed is a guard at the top of `vi_need_reset_on_init` returning false for subsystem vendor 0xBDBD / device 0xA14C. Install via /lib/modules/$(uname -r)/updates/ + depmod (reversible). Test choreography: virgin-recipe boot with amdgpu blacklisted, v5 dance from userspace, then modprobe of the patched module — POST will run against a live cooperative SMC for the first time (watch the VRAM size: expect the true 8192M).

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (22:01) — PATCHED amdgpu.ko BUILT (vermagic 7.0.0-42-generic SMP preempt mod_unload modversions); build-route lessons recorded

Build saga resolved: (1) headers-tree (KDIR) builds fail on amdgpu_trace.h's root-relative trace include — in-tree build is the way; (2) Ubuntu's source carries SUBLEVEL=14 while their shipped release is 7.0.0-42 — fixed by setting SUBLEVEL=0 in the top Makefile (their packaging does the equivalent; kernel.release then reads exactly 7.0.0-42-generic); (3) scripts/gendwarfksyms needs libdw-dev + zlib1g-dev; (4) never pipe a first `make modules_prepare` to tail — syncconfig can block on an invisible kconfig prompt (fix: make olddefconfig first); (5) modpost needs the running kernel's Module.symvers copied from the headers tree into the build root. Result: amdgpu.ko linked with the one-guard vi.c patch and the exact vermagic of the running kernel. Installs to /lib/modules/$(uname -r)/updates/ so depmod prefers it over stock (reversible).

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (22:10) — First patched-module load refused: "Invalid relocation target, existing value is nonzero" (arch/x86 module loader) — mixed objects from the two build venues; clean rebuild required

The patched amdgpu.ko (correct vermagic, uname confirms 7.0.0-42-generic) fails insmod with ENOEXEC and the kernel log gives the real reason: `module: x86/modules: Invalid relocation target, existing value is nonzero for sec 92, idx 1, type 1`. Cause: the amdgpu subtree contains objects from BOTH build attempts — the aborted headers-tree (KDIR) build compiled some .o files into the source dirs before dying on the trace header, and the in-tree build + modpost rerun linked a mixture, producing a composite with already-applied relocations. Remedy: `make M=drivers/gpu/drm/amd/amdgpu clean` and a single-venue in-tree rebuild, reinstall to updates/, and modprobe in the same boot (the v5 SMC state survives — a failed insmod never touches the card; PASS=1 was re-established at 22:09 in this boot).

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (22:27) — Control test isolates it: EVERY module from the hand-prepared source tree is refused (hello.ko too); the same hello built against the headers tree LOADS (insmod exit 0)

hello.ko built in-tree fails with the same loader complaint (its sec 42), and readelf shows the trigger family: R_X86_64_64 relocations in __patchable_function_entries/__mcount_loc whose slots come out pre-filled from our prepared tree — the strict x86 loader check (existing value nonzero) refuses every module the tree produces. The identical hello.c built with KDIR=/lib/modules/$(uname -r)/build loads cleanly. So the hand-rolled prepare (olddefconfig + locally generated build state) diverges from Ubuntu's shipped build state in ftrace/patchable-entry handling; the toolchain is innocent. Decision: abandon the in-tree build; build amdgpu in the headers venue (the DKMS-standard path), fixing its one known blocker — define_trace.h's root-relative include of amdgpu_trace.h — with a single symlink of the amd subtree into the headers root. The vi.c patch lives in our source copy and is picked up via M= as before.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (22:37) — First patched-venue probe: no config reset, POST ran, deep init, died at ip_init SMU timeout; POST identified as the SMC-custody thief

Probe (t=1782): ATOM BIOS read, UVD/VCE firmware found and enabled, "GPU posting now...", GART 256M enabled, Chained IB on — then an 11.3 s hole and "amdgpu_device_ip_init failed" / -22: the classic SMU-load timeout, now in ip_init with NO preceding "PCI CONFIG reset" and no "asic reset on init failed". VRAM mis-sized again (0xff801000 → 4096M on the 8 GB card), the same bogus value as the frozen-SMC probe — the mis-size tracks POST, not SMC state. Interpretation: with the reset patched out, the driver's POST still runs (amdgpu_need_post is a separate decision), and POST's ASIC_Init restarts the VBIOS-resident Apple SMC image, evicting the userspace-loaded standard firmware. polaris10_start_smu then sees "a running SMC" (the Apple image passes ck_disable==0 && PC>=0x20100) and skips its dance, so smu7_request_smu_load_fw's PPSMC messages go to the Apple image and are ignored → timeout. The enemy is singular: any POST after v5 swaps SMC custody. Patch #2 (pre-designed): also gate amdgpu_need_post to false for subsystem BDBD/A14C, making the probe follow the driver's own resume path (no POST, no reset, SMC already running). Open risk: with POST skipped the memory controller is never host-initialized in a virgin boot; VRAM sizing may be the next wall.

## CORRECTION 2026-11-08 (22:39) — The 22:37 probe was the STOCK module; the patch is still untested

modinfo -n amdgpu resolves to /lib/modules/7.0.0-42-generic/kernel/drivers/gpu/drm/amd/amdgpu/amdgpu.ko.zst — the stock module. The 22:37 install failed with "No such file or directory" because the shell's working directory was not the build tree, so every relative path in that block missed; modprobe therefore loaded stock. The probe record stands (stock + v5-running firmware: reset+POST proceeded, deep init, ip_init SMU timeout at 11.3 s — the eviction story replicated), but the earlier reading "the patch held, no config reset" is retracted: no patched module has probed the card yet. Process fix: absolute paths everywhere, and modinfo -n must show the updates/ path before any future modprobe counts as a patch test. Design consequence already drawn: patch #1 (reset guard) alone cannot win because POST evicts the userspace SMC image; patch #2 (need_post guard) joins it before the next virgin-recipe cycle.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (22:52) — DOUBLY-PATCHED PROBE: no POST, no reset, no timeout — new wall is VRAM sizing (the pre-flagged no-POST cost)

Virgin recipe + v5 PASS=1, modinfo gate confirmed updates/amdgpu.ko, modprobe exit 0. Probe log (t=159.96, all within ~7 ms): IP blocks detected, VFCT VBIOS fetched, UVD/VCE firmware found — NO "GPU posting now", NO reset, NO 11 s SMU hole. Failure: VRAM: 0M (base 0x0), RAM width 32bits UNKNOWN, "Failed initializing VRAM heap", gmc_v8_0 sw_init -22, ip_init failed. With POST skipped, the memory controller was never initialized: FB_LOCATION/CONFIG_MEMSIZE hold power-on values, so GMC finds no VRAM. Note the SMU rendezvous is still untested — gmc sw_init precedes smu sw_init in ip order. Next: plant POST's GMC state from userspace in the virgin state (FB_LOCATION was measured POSTed at 0xf4fff400 on this card; CONFIG_MEMSIZE encoding to be decoded from the twin's live register), then re-probe via sysfs bind in the same boot. Remaining unknown after that: whether GDDR5 training (a POST duty) is needed for real VRAM traffic.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (23:02) — Planted-register bind probe HARD-FROZE the machine; its log lines never reached disk

With FB_LOCATION/CONFIG_MEMSIZE planted, the sysfs-bind probe froze the iMac completely (no mouse; force power-off required). journalctl -b -1 shows only the first (modprobe) probe's lines — the bind probe's output was lost unflushed, so the hang point is unlogged. The planted values moved the probe past the clean GMC failure into a hard hang somewhere deeper (GMC programming polls, firmware loads into untrained VRAM, or the SMU rendezvous). Strategy upgrade: stop guessing POST's register state piecemeal — capture the FULL POSTed BAR5 aperture (the stock boot probe in a normal boot leaves the card POSTed and readable), diff it against a virgin dump, and replay the complete differing register set from userspace before v5 + bind. Next bind attempt also gets a 1-second dmesg snapshot loop (with sync) so a hang leaves a last-position record.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (23:10) — In a normal boot the stock probe leaves the eGPU aperture WEDGED (all-0xFF); twin MC panel captured

Targeted reads of the MC sequencer panel on the eGPU in a normal (non-blacklist) boot returned 0xFFFFFFFF on every register — after the stock boot probe fails, its config reset leaves BAR5 dead (the 21:01 wedge), so the eGPU's own POSTed MC values cannot be harvested in a normal boot. The twin's panel read fine (CHMAP 0x00433210, MISC0 0x506066b2, RD_CTL 0x00c0327a, WR_CTL 0x2035b1bb, LP WR 0x20353133, LP RD 0x0100327a, WR_CTL_2_LP 0x0a2f2800) and is the fallback plant set (same GDDR5 class). Better plan: harvest in a blacklist boot by parking the patched updates/ module, depmod, and modprobing STOCK — after a modprobe-driven stock probe the aperture has historically survived (21:24→21:27 SMC reads worked), gated on FB_LOCATION reading 0xf4fff400 before trusting the panel.

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (23:19) — eGPU's own POSTed MC panel harvested; POST leaves CONFIG_MEMSIZE garbage (0xff801000) in the register itself

Harvest boot (blacklist + stock modprobe): FB_LOCATION=0xf4fff400 (aperture survived, gate passed), CONFIG_MEMSIZE=0xff801000 — POST itself leaves the "bad vram size" value in the register; the stock driver's 4096M fallback is its response to that garbage. The computed plant 0x2000 (8192 MB, twin's encoding) stands. eGPU POSTed MC panel (byte offsets): CHMAP 0x2004=0x00433210; MISC0 0x2a00=0x506000b2 (twin 0x506066b2 — population bits differ); RD_CTL_D0/D1 0x28b4/0x28b8=0x00c0327a; WR_CTL_D0/D1 0x28bc/0x28c0=0x20351111 (twin 0x2035b1bb); WR_CTL_2 0x2b54=0x0000003f (twin 0); WR_CTL_D0/D1_LP 0x2a7c/0x2a80=0x20353133; RD_CTL_D0/D1_LP 0x2b1c/0x2b20=0x00b0327a (twin 0x0100327a); WR_CTL_2_LP 0x2b58=0x0a2f2800. Full plant set for the next virgin attempt = FB_LOCATION + CONFIG_MEMSIZE(0x2000) + these 12. Open risk: POST programs more registers than this panel, and some MC_SEQ registers may be locked pre-POST (the poke readback will say).

## Addendum 2026-11-08 (23:37) — BREAKTHROUGH: with the full plant, the patched probe BOUND AND RAN; death came later, via job timeout → GPU recovery → reset

The attempt boot's flushed journal (00:29 local) shows amdgpu_job_timedout → amdgpu_device_gpu_recover → asic_reset → fini cascade with TTM warnings in switcheroo-cont context, on a kernel that kept logging (00:29:07 and 00:29:21) — the machine never hard-froze; the display stack died. A job timeout is only possible on a fully probed device with live rings and allocated VRAM/GTT managers: the 14-register plant + v5 + doubly-patched module completed ip_init and reached job submission — the first successful bind of the investigation. The killer chain is the card's known nemesis arriving through the back door: any job timeout triggers GPU recovery, recovery does an ASIC reset, and reset is fatal to this card's state (and took the shared display session with it). Next questions: which ring/job timed out (journal head), and whether recovery/reset can be patched out for BDBD:A14C (amdgpu_gpu_recovery=0 / lockup_timeout tuning as a stopgap; a third guard in the reset path as the real fix). Instrumentation lesson: run future attempt tooling under tmux/systemd-run so a display-session bounce cannot kill the snapshot loop.

## CORRECTION 2026-11-08 (23:38) — The eGPU did NOT bind; the plant never reached the registers, and the timeout cascade was a separate event

The attempt boot's journal head (00:29:02) shows the eGPU probe failing with VRAM: 0M / RAM width UNKNOWN / gmc sw_init -22 — identical to the no-plant probe. The patched module did load and POST was skipped, but FB_LOCATION/CONFIG_MEMSIZE read as zero at probe time: the 14-register plant was absent (the attempt's dance/poke terminal output was never captured and died with the session, so whether the poke ran, mismatched, or didn't stick is unknown). The 00:29:07 job-timeout/recovery cascade carries no device prefix in its traces and began 5 s after the failed eGPU probe, in switcheroo-cont context — most consistent with the TWIN timing out a job during the live-session display handoff after its bind, its recovery reset then killing the display stack on a live kernel. The previous addendum's "bound and ran" claim is retracted. Tomorrow's protocol fixes: (1) redirect dance+poke output to a log file and VERIFY the plant with a regpeek re-read as a hard gate before modprobe; (2) run the module load from a TTY or tmux so the graphical session bounce cannot kill the operator or instrumentation; (3) read the full -1 journal segment to name the cascade's device. The journal persists across boots, so none of this is urgent tonight.

## Addendum 2026-11-09 (09:16) — STEP 0 VERDICT: the 00:29 cascade was the TWIN (DRM client init -EINTR in a live session); mid-session modprobe is racy by nature; revised protocol loads the module pre-session via multi-user target

Attempt-boot journal (now -b -2) settles it. Twin probe (00:29:01): full hardware init succeeded — VRAM 4096M, width 256bits GDDR5, GART, UVD/VCE, Display Core, planes registered — then "Failed to init DRM client: -4" and "probe with driver amdgpu failed with error -4" (-EINTR). The twin never bound. The 00:29:07 job_timedout → gpu_recover → asic_reset → fini-warning cascade (switcheroo-cont context) is the twin's probe unwind/recovery: it was the only device with live VRAM/GTT managers and jobs. The eGPU's VRAM-0M failure is an independent line in the same modprobe and contributed nothing to the blackout. Root cause family: registering a DRM device mid-graphical-session requires a console/switcheroo handoff that races the running compositor — the harvest boot won the race (login bounce, twin bound), the attempt boot lost it (-EINTR + recovery cascade). PROTOCOL REVISION: attempt boots add systemd.unit=multi-user.target to the GRUB line — text console, no compositor, no SDDM; dance/plant/verify/modprobe all run there (tee-logging per the resume sheet), and only on probe success does `systemctl isolate graphical.target` start the display stack with both GPUs already bound. This is also the final product's boot shape (service loads the module pre-session). Side findings: VT switching (Ctrl+Alt+F3, with or without Fn) does not work on this system in a graphical session, and the Nov 9 morning sleep-wake failure (backlight on, no image, stock driver, normal boot) is the same display-stack fragility — the graphical stack does not survive GPU state transitions on this machine.

## Addendum 2026-11-09 morning (chat clock) — VRAM 8192M; the plant works; new failure deeper in gmc sw_init

- Typing-free protocol built and verified: GRUB params baked via /etc/default/grub (single-quoted line GRUB_CMDLINE_LINUX_DEFAULT='reboot=pci quiet splash' + ' pci=realloc hpmemsize=512M'; sed paste mangled, a python regex edit landed it; update-grub; cmdline verified live). Blacklist+rebar moved to /etc/modprobe.d/amdgpu-egpu.conf (2 lines; a tee echo made cat output LOOK duplicated — file was always 2 lines, wc -l settled it). Boot target: `systemctl set-default` SILENTLY DID NOT PERSIST (no /etc/systemd/system/default.target existed, get-default still graphical) — direct symlink `ln -sf /usr/lib/systemd/system/multi-user.target /etc/systemd/system/default.target` + ls verification is the reliable route. Attempt wrapped in ~/egpu/run.sh (tee to attempt.log, modinfo gate inside), go.sh (modprobe+settle+report), gfx.sh (isolate graphical). Initramfs contains no amdgpu (lsinitramfs grep empty) so the modprobe.d blacklist governs the whole boot.
- run.sh executed the dance PERFECTLY from the tty (virgin start PC 0x2a40 RESP=1; blast 30490 mismatches pass1, byte-clean pass2; Test RESP=1 in 0.2s; SMU_STATUS=0x3; ownership tail; PC in SRAM) — then the plant step failed: **regpoke.py was NOT on the iMac (Errno 2)**. Only regpeek.py had been copied the night before. THIS is the root cause of the 2026-11-08 23:28 plant silently never taking effect (its error died with the graphical session). The run.sh hard gate (verify read all zeros) correctly stopped the sequence before modprobe.
- Recovery in the same boot (no re-cycle): gfx.sh to desktop, pasted regpoke.py into existence (ls-verified), planted from the desktop. ALL 14 pokes readback OK — no locked MC registers pre-POST. Independent gate: FB_LOCATION=f4fff400, CONFIG_MEMSIZE=00002000, CHMAP=00433210, MISC0=506000b2. Bonus: CHMAP already read 00433210 BEFORE the plant (strap-derived, self-populating).
- Console route remains cursed: `systemctl isolate multi-user.target` from the desktop killed the panel (backlight on, no image — same display-handoff quirk as the morning wake failure). Blind `bash ~/egpu/gfx.sh` from tty1 restored the desktop. Modprobe therefore launched from Konsole (harvest-boot style).
- **PROBE RESULT (the milestone): `amdgpu 0000:44:00.0: VRAM: 8192M 0x000000F400000000 - 0x000000F5FFFFFFFF (8192M used)`, GART 256M allocated.** The plant is read and believed; the VRAM-0M heap failure is dead. Probe then failed ~5 ms later: `sw_init of IP block <gmc_v8_0> failed -22` (silent, no message of its own), amdgpu_device_ip_init failed, probe -22. Twin bound under the patched module in the same modprobe (desktop returned full resolution).
- **Post-failure survival (measured minutes later, same boot): SMC untouched — MESSAGE=0x100 RESP=0x1, PC samples in SRAM (0x205bc), SMU_STATUS=0x3 DONE+PASS, RCU boot_seq_done set. Plant registers ALL survived (f4fff400/00002000/00433210/506000b2).** gmc hw_init (mc_program) never ran, consistent with plant survival. Consequence: same-boot re-probe after a module fix is plausible — iteration may no longer need enclosure cycles for this failure class.
- Source analysis (mainline gmc_v8_0.c read 2026-11-09): sw_init order = vram_type from MISC0 -> irq_add_id x2 -> vm_adjust_size (the "vm size is 128 GB" print) -> dma_set_mask (prints on failure; absent) -> gmc_v8_0_init_microcode (prints pr_err + DRM_ERROR on failure) -> gmc_v8_0_mc_init -> amdgpu_bo_init -> gmc_v8_0_gart_init -> vm_manager_init -> kmalloc (-ENOMEM, not -22). mc_init body: vram_width, CONFIG_MEMSIZE, amdgpu_device_resize_fb_bar, gart size, vram_gtt_location — and resize_fb_bar returns 0 immediately when amdgpu_rebar==0 (amdgpu_device.c: `if (!amdgpu_rebar) return 0;`), so the resize path is excluded under our rebar=0. Live suspects: init_microcode (polaris10_k_mc.bin for rev c0 if ASICID_IS_P30 matches; file presence + full-log check pending) or a later silent return in bo_init/gart_init. Next: firmware ls + untruncated probe window; fallback = breadcrumb-instrumented sw_init rebuild and same-boot re-probe.

## Addendum 2026-11-09 ~10:07 (chat clock) — the gmc -22 root-caused: BAR0/BAR2 were never assigned this boot

- Firmware check excluded the microcode suspect: /lib/firmware/amdgpu contains polaris10_k_mc.bin.zst and polaris10_mc.bin.zst; the untruncated probe window has no "Failed to load" line. init_microcode passed. /sys/module/amdgpu/parameters/rebar reads 0 — the modprobe.d options line took effect.
- vramtest.py then exposed it: sysfs resource line 0 reads start=0. Full resource dump: BAR0, BAR2, BAR4 all 0x0 (unassigned); BAR5 0x88b00000 and ROM 0x88b60000 fine; lspci shows ONLY Region 5. Boot dmesg: FOUR realloc rounds, every BAR0 (256M) and BAR2 (2M) assignment "failed to assign", NO success line; /proc/iomem has no 0x4020000000/0x4030000000 window this boot. **BAR0/BAR2 were never assigned in the baked boot** — unlike every typed-GRUB trio boot on 2026-11-08, where BAR0 landed at 0x4020000000.
- Reinterpretation of the probe: it ran with NO framebuffer aperture (aper_base/aper_size = 0 at mc_init) and no doorbell BAR. The VRAM: 8192M / GART prints are computed from FB_LOCATION and are BAR-independent, so they printed normally; the silent -22 in gmc_v8_0 sw_init is the downstream zero-aperture failure in the bo/gart stage. The plant, the SMC firmware, and the two patches are all CLEARED for this failure — it is a PCI resource-allocation failure of this particular boot. Nobody had verified BAR0 in this boot before the probe (all pre-probe tooling is BAR5-only) — process gap, now gated.
- Why realloc lost this boot is not yet established (identical cmdline verified in run.sh's log). Candidates: bridge-window layout nondeterminism, enclosure link timing at kernel init. Next: bridge-window forensics, then re-roll the cycle with a BAR0 gate added to run.sh (abort loudly on a dud boot before the dance). If assignment fails deterministically under the baked config, compare against a typed-line boot and consider a larger hpmemsize.

## Addendum 2026-11-09 ~11:53 (his clock) — with all preconditions true, modprobe hard-locks the machine instantly (twice)

- barfix2 (top-down remove+rescan from the root port) rehabilitates dud boots: the kernel ignores the setpci-programmed windows on rescan of existing bridges, but removing 00:1c.4 and rescanning forces a full subtree re-derivation — with pci=realloc armed it assigned everything itself (root bus resource [mem 0x4000000000-0x7fffffffff] confirmed present): BAR0 0x4010000000 [256M], BAR2 0x4020000000 [2M], BAR5 0x80400000, ROM 0x80440000, audio BAR0 0x80460000, BAR4 io 0x7000. All five bridges' config registers DID hold the manual window values (setpci readback 2001/3ff1/40/40 everywhere) — hardware accepted them; only the kernel's boot-time resource structs were stale. SMC virgin state SURVIVED both remove+rescan rounds and the chain rebuild (peek after wake: RESP=1, PC 0x2b50/0x28dc ROM, SMU_STATUS=0x0) — config-space surgery does not touch the ASIC. Note: peeks before the wake steps (setpci COMMAND=0002 + power/control on) read all-0xFFFFFFFF (device asleep, decode off) — void reads, not state.
- Rehabilitated boot (journal -1, 11:23:39-11:35:26 his clock): run.sh v3 (BAR5 read from sysfs) ran flawlessly — dance byte-clean PASS, plant 14/14 OK, gate verified. Then go.sh (old, terminal-only) -> black screen, fans normal, machine silent; forced power-off. The probe's outcome appears NOWHERE: journal -1 ends at 11:35:26 (during run.sh), kern.log's last 44-lines are the 11:32 rescan, pstore is EMPTY (no panic dump). 
- Repeat in the next boot with go.sh rewritten to tee+sync to ~/egpu/probe.log: barfix2.log and attempt.log complete again (dance PASS, plant OK), **probe.log exists but is 0 bytes** — go.sh launched, tee created the file, and the machine died before even the header line flushed. modprobe was issued and the system hard-locked within a fraction of a second.
- Pattern across all four patched-module probes: WITHOUT BAR0/BAR2 (unassigned), the probe completes and fails cleanly (gmc sw_init -22, modprobe returns). WITH BAR0+BAR2 assigned (barfix2 map), modprobe hard-locks instantly — twice. The delta correlates with the aperture/doorbell existing, i.e. the probe surviving into the stages that actually touch the card (bo/gart init with a live aperture, hw_init: mc_program, MC microcode load + training wait, SMU rendezvous with the danced firmware). Naked modprobe is now a known-instant-lock action under full preconditions; do not repeat it uninstrumented.
- Next instrument design: big-font tty (setfont ter-v32b — console text hurts his eyes at native size), staged bind: set eGPU driver_override="none", modprobe (twin binds, module resident, stable), clear override to "amdgpu", raise console loglevel, then sysfs-bind 44:00.0 alone while kernel printks spray onto tty1 — a lock then freezes the probe's last lines on the panel for a photo, converting the invisible failure into a readable one.

## Addendum 2026-11-09 ~12:17 (his clock) — isolated bind freezes a healthy system; untrained-DRAM theory fits every data point

- Staged bind executed from a working desktop (module pre-loaded via driver_override="none" on 44:00.0; twin bound and driving the panel; gfx.sh had recovered the panel after the twin's modeset darkened it — machine verified alive, caps-lock LED toggling). The moment 44:00.0 was bound to amdgpu via sysfs: TOTAL freeze — frozen screen, no mouse, caps-lock LED dead. Power-hold recovery. The eGPU's own probe, alone, with all preconditions true, hard-locks the machine.
- Full pattern, four patched probes: (1) no plant, aperture present -> VRAM 0M, clean -22, no freeze (driver believes no VRAM, never touches it). (2) full plant, NO aperture -> VRAM 8192M printed, clean gmc sw_init -22, no freeze (cannot touch what has no address). (3) 2-register plant, aperture present -> freeze (2026-11-08 22:58). (4) full plant + aperture -> freeze (x3: two modprobes, one isolated bind).
- Working theory: the plant makes the driver believe 8 GB of VRAM sits behind a real aperture, but the GDDR5 was never TRAINED — training is part of VBIOS POST (AtomBIOS memory-init), which the no-POST patch deliberately skips (POST also starts the parked Apple-channel SMC). The probe's first real aperture touch (bo/gart setup or early VRAM access) hangs the ASIC's memory fabric; PCIe completions stop; the probing CPU stalls mid-load; IRQs die (caps-lock LED is the discriminator — every PPSMC/driver wait is timeout-bounded and could not kill the LED). Nothing is ever logged because printk never gets flushed — matches journal amputation and empty pstore in every freeze.
- Consequence: POST's two effects are separable in time — DRAM training vs starting the wrong SMC. New experiment ladder for next session: (a) virgin state; (b) one STOCK modprobe (POST runs, probe fails at the SMC timeout as always, device left POSTed — the 2026-11-08 harvest proved the register panel survives that failure); (c) vramtest.py read/write through BAR0 (does training persist post-failure?) + smcpeek (state A?); (d) if VRAM is alive, the open question is whether the dance can seize a RUNNING SMC (halt via SYSCON reset/clock-gate, then blast — a v6 dance) and whether a no-POST-patch is even needed if POST already happened in the same boot (bind with only the no-reset guard). Do NOT run another naked full-precondition probe before this ladder.

## Addendum 2026-11-09 ~12:42 (his clock) — today's stock probe classified: dud boot; POST does not appear to start the SMC

- This boot's resource file: BAR0/BAR2 all-zero (UNASSIGNED — barfix2 was not run in this boot), BAR5 0x88b00000, ROM 0x88b60000. Dud map.
- The stock probe in this boot is therefore fully explained without new physics: POST ran ("GPU posting now...", CONFIG_MEMSIZE left at POST's 0xff801000, driver fell back to VRAM 4096M), then gmc_v8_0 sw_init failed -22 in ~21 ms — the known no-aperture bo_init failure (same family as patched probe B). NO SMC-timeout hole: the probe died upstream of the SMU stage, so it says nothing about the SMU rendezvous.
- BUT the post-probe smcpeek measured the SMC in the VIRGIN ROM download-wait state (PC 0x28e8/0x2a2c, RESP=1, SMU_STATUS=0) minutes after ASIC_Init ran. Two readings: (a) ASIC_Init does not start this card's SMC at all; (b) the failed probe's fini reset the SMC back to ROM. (b) is weakened by the 2026-11-08 harvest: after a failed stock probe in the same boot, the POSTed MC register panel survived intact for harvesting — a fini ASIC reset would have wiped it. Working hypothesis: (a) — POST on this card does board/memory init but leaves the SMC in ROM wait. If true, the no-POST patch (guard #2) protects against nothing and only ever cost us DRAM training; the correct module is guard #1 (no-reset) ALONE, with dance+plant before the bind and POST running inside the probe.
- Still open: the guard greps (exact text of the need_post guard in the tree's amdgpu_device.c, and confirmation of the vi.c guard) — required before writing the removal script. Then: rebuild one-guard via the headers venue, install behind the modinfo gate, fresh cycle (enclosure cycle + reboot, barfix2 if dud, dance, plant, staged bind).

## Addendum 2026-11-09 ~13:11 (his clock) — BREAKTHROUGH: one-guard probe runs POST, trains DRAM, enables GART, no freeze; dies silently at fw_loading after an ~11 s hole

- One-guard module (no-reset guard ONLY; need_post restored to stock) + dance + plant + staged bind. Probe log (683.211-694.256): POST ran ("GPU posting now...", ~16 ms), POST overwrote the plant's CONFIG_MEMSIZE with its own 0xff801000 -> driver fell back to VRAM 4096M (the 8G plant does not survive POST; full-VRAM is now a driver-side polish item). Then, for the first time ever on this card under Linux: "Detected VRAM RAM=4096M, BAR=256M", "RAM width 256bits GDDR5", "4096M of VRAM memory ready", "15984M of GTT memory ready", **"PCIE GART of 256M enabled (table at 0x000000F400000000)"** — gmc sw_init AND hw_init PASSED, VRAM was written, **NO FREEZE**. The untrained-DRAM freeze theory is CONFIRMED: guard #2 (no-POST) was the freeze's cause; POST's training is the cure.
- Failure: after "Found UVD firmware" (683.234812) an **10.975 s silent hole**, then "amdgpu_device_ip_init failed" / probe -22 with NO block-failure line of any kind.
- Source-verified against mainline v7.0 amdgpu_device.c (read 2026-11-09): in 7.0, ip_init's loop runs sw_init per block with COMMON and GMC hw_init inlined (matches the log exactly), then ib_pool_init, ucode_create_bo, hw_init_phase1 (COMMON/IH only — both already hw, a no-op here), then **amdgpu_device_fw_loading**, whose failure does `goto init_failed` with NO dev_err at the call site — the only silent -22 in the sequence at that depth. For pre-Vega ASICs fw_loading is a single call: **amdgpu_pm_load_smu_firmware** (the PPSMC/SMU rendezvous). phase2 never ran. The hole is therefore the SMU firmware-load wait — the same ~11 s signature as the 2026-11-08 stock timeout, now precisely located.
- Open fork (decided by one peek): did the danced firmware survive POST to reach fw_loading? If PC is in SRAM + SMU_STATUS=0x3 in a post-failure peek, the firmware lived and the driver's start_smu handshake rejected the state (prime suspect: the dance v5 ownership tail CLEARS FIRMWARE_FLAGS — plausibly the very flag the driver checks to decide the SMC is already loaded; the tail was tuned for the no-POST probe that never reached fw_loading, so its effect here was never measured). If PC is back in ROM range, ASIC_Init resets the SMC and the dance cannot precede POST — the fix then lives in the driver (start_smu path) instead.
- Module state note: updates/amdgpu.ko is now the ONE-GUARD build (parked two-guard build at /root/egpu-park/amdgpu.ko; record copy of one-guard at ~/egpu/amdgpu-oneguard.ko). Stock module remains in its kernel/ path.

## Addendum 2026-11-09 ~13:16 (his clock) — the hole is PPSMC_MSG_LoadUcodes: the SMC took the order and never reported back

- Post-failure peek (same boot, BAR5 0x80400000): **SMU_STATUS=0x3**, PC=0x2df5c (inside the SRAM window, STATIC across both samples — parked, not roaming), RCU=0x000e0084, clock running. DIRECT mailbox holds a leftover: **MESSAGE=0x254, ARG=0x47e, RESP=0x0 (never answered)**.
- Header-verified (mainline v7.0 smu7_ppsmc.h): **0x254 = PPSMC_MSG_LoadUcodes**; its predecessors in smu7_request_smu_load_fw are DRV_DRAM_ADDR_HI 0x250, DRV_DRAM_ADDR_LO 0x251, SMU_DRAM_ADDR_HI 0x252, SMU_DRAM_ADDR_LO 0x253. ARG 0x47e has exactly 7 bits set = the 7-ucode mask for >TOPAZ (RLC_G, SDMA0, SDMA1, CP_CE, CP_ME, CP_PFP, CP_MEC).
- Source-verified gate (smu7_smumgr.c): smu7_is_smc_ram_running = (ck_disable==0 && SMC_PC_C >= 0x20100) — both true at peek time, so start_smu accepted the danced firmware as running and skipped its own start sequence; the dance SURVIVED POST. smu7_send_msg_to_smc always returns 0 (its RESP waits are swallowed), so an unanswered message does not fail there; the ~10.975 s hole is the LoadUcodes rendezvous — the send's RESP wait plus smu7_check_fw_load_finish polling the UcodeLoadStatus soft register — ending in -EINVAL, which fw_loading returns silently. Every line of the probe log is now accounted for.
- Interpretation: the four address messages were answered (the flow advanced past them), then LoadUcodes was issued and the SMC never answered and never set load-finish. LoadUcodes is the first time this SMC has been asked to DMA-fetch from DRAM (the TOC + ucode images the driver staged). PC parked static suggests it wedged or waits inside that fetch. Sub-mechanism (SMC's DRAM window / GTT-vs-VRAM buffer placement / IOMMU path over the TB chain) not yet distinguished.
- Next diagnostic (no fresh cycle needed — firmware live, DRAM trained in this boot): clear the stale mailbox from userspace (regpoke BAR5 offsets 0x250/0x254/0x290 = 0), run a background smcpeek loop logging PC/RESP/SMU_STATUS to ~/egpu/watch.log, re-bind, and read the SMC's trajectory through the rendezvous — separates "never started the load" from "started and wedged on the fetch". Do NOT clear the mailbox blindly in future boots without noting it: the stale 0x254/RESP=0 state could poison a later probe's first send pre-wait.

## Addendum 2026-11-09 ~13:22 (his clock) — watch-log transitions: the SMC has answered NOTHING since probe #1

- Watch-log baseline (13:18:00, before probe #2's bind, after the manual mailbox clear): MESSAGE=0, RESP=0, and the PC was ALREADY wobbling in the 3-address loop 0x2df5c/0x2df60/0x2df64. That loop therefore predates probe #2 — it is the SMC's resting state since probe #1 failed (contrast: after a dance, PC samples roam widely across SRAM).
- Probe #2's flow: the first load message caught in flight is 0x252 (SMU_DRAM_ADDR_HI, ARG=0xf4 — upper half of an 0xF4xxxxxx FB address; the smu_buffer is in VRAM). It sat UNANSWERED (RESP=0, PC never leaving the 3-address loop) across ~8-14 s of samples. So probe #2 died at its FIRST load message, not at LoadUcodes; the driver simply walked its timeouts forward. By extension, probe #1's leftover 0x254 proves only that the driver REACHED LoadUcodes in its own sequence (smu7_send_msg_to_smc returns 0 regardless of answers) — it does NOT prove any message was ever answered in probe #1 either.
- Unified candidate theory for the stock failure (revises the 2026-11-08 model): the SMC sits in ROM download-wait in stock boots (measured today), and the driver's own smu7_upload_smu_firmware_image blasts the image ONCE with no verify/repair — while the dance measured ~30,490 dword write-losses on pass 1 through this card's indirect SRAM path. A stock upload is therefore corrupt, the SMC never runs, and start_smu's waits time out. The dance exists precisely because it verifies and repairs to byte-clean.
- Remaining fork for the danced case: (i) the dance's ownership tail (FIRMWARE_FLAGS clear + reset pulse, mirroring the driver's own tail) leaves the re-booted firmware in a state whose PPSMC service never arms, and start_smu's "already running" gate skips the sequence that would arm it — silence from probe #1's first message onward; or (ii) probe #1's LoadUcodes DRAM fetch wedged the firmware mid-attempt and the 3-address loop is that wedge persisting. Discriminator: send PPSMC_MSG_Test (0x100, arg 0x20000) from userspace right now via regpoke (ARG 0x290=0x20000, RESP 0x254=0, MESSAGE 0x250=0x100) and peek RESP — an answer kills (i)/(ii) as stated and refocuses on the fetch path; silence confirms the message service is dead post-probe-#1 and sets up a fresh-boot A/B (dance with vs without the tail).

## Addendum 2026-11-09 ~14:09 (his clock) — force-start TEST: gate fired, start proceeded, failure isolated to check_fw_load_finish

- Test setup: fresh cycle, dud boot -> barfix2, NO dance, NO plant, staged bind with the force-start module. Probe log: POST ran (VRAM fallback 4096M), GART enabled, UVD found 343.281, hole 9.79 s, ip_init failed -22.
- Unfiltered dmesg (the earlier `grep amdgpu 0000:44` hid non-device-prefixed lines — standing lesson: powerplay prints carry no device prefix): **[343.292] "amdgpu: Blackmagic eGPU: forcing full SMC start" — THE PATCH FIRED.** Then [353.066] "amdgpu: SMU load firmware failed" and "amdgpu: smu firmware loading failed". Source-confirmed (smu7_smumgr.c v7.0): "SMU load firmware failed" is the pr_err in smu7_request_smu_load_fw when smu7_check_fw_load_finish returns error; check polls the UcodeLoadStatus soft register (soft_regs_start + offsetof(SMU_SoftRegisters, UcodeLoadStatus)) for equality with the fw mask 0x47e. NEITHER start-failure assert appeared ("Failed to load SMU ucode." / "SMU Firmware start failed!").
- Residue peek (peek-first rule observed): MESSAGE=0x254, RESP=0, ARG=0x47e; PC wobbling 0x2df5c/0x2df64; SMU_STATUS=0x3. Same shape as the danced probes' corpse.
- CAVEAT (source): the start has two paths. polaris10_start_smu_in_protection_mode verifies the upload (Test -> DONE -> PASS check -> tail -> INTERRUPTS_ENABLED). polaris10_start_smu_in_non_protection_mode has NO integrity check — it returns the upload's result, and the blast upload cannot detect data corruption. Which path ran depends on SMU_FIRMWARE.SM_MODE read from the virgin card before the start. So "start succeeded" is PROVEN only if the protection path ran; otherwise a corrupt driver upload remains possible. Path discriminator: SMU_FIRMWARE (SMC 0xE00030A4) SM_MODE bit. Integrity discriminator independent of path: userspace Test ping now (residue already captured), and if needed a userspace SRAM readback diff vs polaris10_smc.bin / polaris10_k_smc.bin (the dance's verify machinery proves this card's SRAM is readable; smu7_upload_smu_firmware_image picks SMU vs SMU_SK (kicker) by security_hard_key = SMU_FIRMWARE.SM_SEL).
- Current model: even with a by-the-book driver start, the LoadUcodes rendezvous does not complete — UcodeLoadStatus never fills. Remaining suspects: the SMC's DRAM fetch of the TOC/images (addresses are VRAM mc_addr values: smu_buffer 800 KiB BO, header_buffer 4 KiB BO, per-ucode BOs from amdgpu_ucode_init_bo), the soft-regs status path, or (if non-protection ran) the upload itself.

## Addendum 2026-11-09 ~14:15 (his clock) — SMU_FIRMWARE decoded: PROTECTION start ran and PASSED; driver upload EXONERATED; death is inside the load window

- Userspace Test after the force-start probe: UNANSWERED (RESP=0, PC 0x2df5c/64 loop) — the service is dead again post-probe.
- SMU_FIRMWARE (SMC 0xE00030A4) read 0x0003001e. Field positions from smu_7_1_3_sh_mask.h (verified, correcting an earlier guess): SMU_MODE = bit 16 (mask 0x10000), SMU_SEL = bit 17 (mask 0x20000). Value has BOTH set: **SMU_MODE=1 (protection mode), SMU_SEL=1 (security_hard_key=1 -> the upload used UCODE_ID_SMU = polaris10_smc.bin, the same image the dance uses)**.
- Logic chain proving the forced start genuinely succeeded: protection-mode start verifies the image (Test -> wait SMU_DONE -> require SMU_PASS==1, else "SMU Firmware start failed!" + return -1, which would also print "Failed to load SMU ucode." and prevent request_smu_load_fw from running at all). Neither assert appeared, and "SMU load firmware failed" (which only prints from inside request_smu_load_fw, after the start returned 0) DID appear. Therefore: the driver's auto-increment blast upload is CLEAN when performed under reset (the dance's pass-1 loss is specific to uploading while the boot ROM is live), the uploaded image booted, answered the driver's Test, set DONE+PASS, and took the tail. The service was alive at the end of the start and dead after the load window — the killer is inside request_smu_load_fw's rendezvous (the LoadUcodes fetch/status completion) or the subsequent fini. POST-era and upload theories are closed.
- Force-start side effect that changes iteration economics: EVERY bind now re-runs the full verified SMC start, so a same-boot RE-BIND is a complete fresh attempt — no enclosure power-cycle needed for SMC experiments. Next diagnostic: watched re-bind (script-file watcher, watch.sh) capturing the message/ARG trajectory (SMU_DRAM_ADDR and DRV_DRAM_ADDR values = smu_buffer and header_buffer mc_addr) and the PC collapse timing relative to LoadUcodes; the captured header_buffer address then allows a userspace VRAM read of the TOC the SMC was told to fetch (num_entries=9, structure_version=1 expected) to test whether the memcpy_toio staging landed over Thunderbolt.

## Addendum 2026-11-09 ~14:23 (his clock) — the re-bind's firmware is ALIVE; ARG+1 is the firmware's Test ack; fini is innocent

## Addendum 2026-11-09 ~14:26 (his clock) — source facts for the hand-run load tool

- TOC type is `struct SMU_DRAMData_TOC` (smu7_smumgr.c smu7_request_smu_load_fw): fields num_entries (=0 init), structure_version (=1), entry[]; entries populated in fixed order RLC_G, CP_CE, CP_PFP, CP_ME, CP_MEC, CP_MEC_JT1, CP_MEC_JT2, SDMA0, SDMA1 (+MEC_STORAGE only if vf). Whole struct memcpy_toio'd into header_buffer, then DRV_DRAM_ADDR_HI sent.
- UcodeLoadStatus is a field of the SMU_SoftRegisters structs (variants in inc/smu71.h, smu72.h, smu73.h, smu75.h) — polaris10's exact struct + offsetof still to pin down from smu7_check_fw_load_finish. There is also a query message: PPSMC_MSG_UcodeLoadStatus = 0x246 (smu7_ppsmc.h).
- The UCODE_ID grep hit the Carrizo header (smu_ucode_xfer_cz.h: RLC_G=8, mask 0x100) — WRONG island; must use smu_ucode_xfer_vi.h for Polaris. (Probe #1's mask 0x47e has bit 10 set, consistent with VI RLC_G mask 0x400, not CZ's 0x100.)

## Addendum 2026-11-09 ~14:30 (his clock) — load-flow ABI fully pinned from source

- struct SMU_Entry (LE): uint16 id, uint16 version, uint32 image_addr_high, image_addr_low, meta_data_addr_high, meta_data_addr_low, data_size_byte, uint16 flags, uint16 num_register_entries = 28 bytes. SMU_DRAMData_TOC = {uint32 structure_version=1, uint32 num_entries, entry[12]} = 344 bytes.
- SMU73_SoftRegisters (polaris10's): offsetof(UcodeLoadStatus) = 108 (counted: 13 uint32 to HandshakeDisables @48, 8 uint8 phy configs @52-59, 3 uint32 activity @60-68, 8 uint8 DPM levels @72-79, DRAM_LOG_* 5x uint32 @80-96, UlvEnterCount @100, UlvTime @104, UcodeLoadStatus @108).
- soft_regs_start discovery: SMU73_Firmware_Header sits at SRAM 0x20000 (start of the uploaded image); its SoftRegisters field is at header offset 48 (Digest[5] 20B + Version + HeaderSize + Flags + EntryPoint + CodeSize + ImageSize = 44 -> Rtos @44, SoftRegisters @48), so soft_regs_start = SRAM dword @ 0x20030; UcodeLoadStatus lives at soft_regs_start + 108.
- smcload.py design (staged for delivery): TOC at FB 0x100000, SMU scratch 1 MiB zeroed at 0x200000, real /lib/firmware/amdgpu/polaris10_rlc.bin staged verbatim at 0x300000, single RLC_G entry (version = ucode_version dword at file offset 12, truncated to uint16; data_size_byte = file size); sends SMU_DRAM_ADDR_HI/LO (0x252/0x253) = 0xF400200000, DRV_DRAM_ADDR_HI/LO (0x250/0x251) = 0xF400100000, LoadUcodes (0x254) arg 0x400; polls status + PC 24x0.25 s, ends with a Test ping (wedge detector). Pre-registered verdicts: status bit sets + PC roams + Test answers = DRAM channel works (driver's data at fault); PC collapses to 0x2df5c-64 + Test dead = channel dead at SMC/VBIOS level; status stays 0 but firmware alive = TOC rejected (iterate format).

## Addendum 2026-11-09 ~14:35 (his clock) — smcload run 1: messages perfect, TOC never landed in VRAM, NEW wedge PC 0x33824

- Run 1 output: RLC image 23,488 bytes, ucode_version 0x8. soft_regs_start = 0x3fa28 (plausible, near image end), UcodeLoadStatus @0x3fa94 = 0 before. All four address messages answered RESP=0x1. LoadUcodes accepted (MESSAGE stuck 0x254, RESP never set).
- **TOC readback from VRAM was ALL ZEROS** — the userspace BAR0 mmap write did not land (payload/scratch writes used the same path, equally suspect). The firmware therefore fetched a non-TOC and wedged inside the LoadUcodes handler at a NEW single static PC **0x33824** (status stayed 0, final Test dead) — distinct from the driver-run corpse loop 0x2df5c/60/64.
- Refined two-stage model (tentative): stage 1 = TOC fetch (our run died here, tooling fault); stage 2 = ucode payload fetch (the driver's runs, whose TOC was really in VRAM via memcpy_toio, died at 0x2df5c). Next: vramprobe.py discriminates mmap-write vs os.pwrite vs pread on the eGPU's BAR0 and checks whether the payload landed at 0x300000. Firmware left wedged at 0x33824; revivable by forced-start re-bind when needed.

## Addendum 2026-11-09 ~14:38 (his clock) — BAR0 writes never reach VRAM; write-combine mapping is the prime suspect

- vramprobe2: pack_into writes to BAR0 offset 0x100000 also read back as zeros (method-independent). pread/pwrite on resource0 both fail EIO (sysfs resource io is a dead end here). Reads DO work: offset 0x300000 returns real nonzero data (59b66a39 59a6100f a64995c6 b649ffe0 - not our payload, POST-era residue), so the aperture reads fine; writes never arrive (consistent with the SMC seeing a zero TOC in smcload run 1).
- Prime suspect: kernel pci_mmap_resource_range maps PREFETCHABLE BARs write-combining on x86. BAR0 (256M, prefetchable) from userspace = WC mapping: small writes sit in CPU WC buffers and may not flush for seconds/ever without pressure or a fencing read; BAR5 (non-prefetchable) maps UC, which is why every BAR5 poke all day worked instantly. Kernel memcpy_toio uses its own UC ioremap - why the driver's TOC landed and ours didn't.
- Test (vramprobe3): pattern write, then (a) dummy BAR5 read as a fence, (b) 4 KiB bulk fill, (c) full 64-byte-line write + fence; check after each step whether DEADBEEF appears. If confirmed, smcload gets a flush ceremony (bulk staging + BAR5 fence read + settle delay) after every VRAM write.

## Addendum 2026-11-09 ~14:40 (his clock) — write-combine CONFIRMED; flush rule = full 64-byte lines

- vramprobe3 ladder: pattern write alone -> zeros; + BAR5 fence read -> zeros; + 4 KiB bulk fill at a different offset -> zeros; **full 64-byte line write covering the pattern + fence read -> deadbeef 12345678 a5a5a5a5 0f0f0f0f READ BACK FROM VRAM.** Userspace BAR0 mapping is write-combining; a WC line commits only when written in full. Partial-line writes (all our staging in smcload run 1) never left the CPU.
- smcload v2 staging rule: every VRAM write in 64-byte-aligned full 64-byte lines (TOC padded 344->384 B; RLC payload 23,488 B is exactly 367 lines; scratch zeroed in zero lines), one BAR5 fence read after staging, hard readback gates (TOC head + decoded entry, payload head/tail) before any message is sent. Revive the wedged firmware first with a forced-start re-bind (fails -22 at the PASS gate, leaves live servicing firmware), then run v2 in the same boot.

## Addendum 2026-11-09 ~14:43 (his clock) — VRAM writes land but individual lines corrupt; adopt verify-and-repair staging

- vramdiag (384 B tagged pattern at FB 0x380000, smcload2's exact full-line loop): lines 1,2,3,5 correct; lines 0 and 4 wrong, STABLE across 0.3 s and an 8 KiB eviction churn. Line 0 contained line 3's leading dwords (0xC0DE0030..33 instead of 0xC0DE0000..03) - duplication of one line's data into another line plus loss of the original, in the write path (reads are stable/trustworthy). Not a flush-timing issue anymore.
- Fix pattern = the dance's own: verify-and-repair. stage(): full-line write of the whole block, fence, read back, rewrite only mismatching lines, repeat (max 8) until byte-clean; gates report pass counts. diag2 validates convergence on the 384 B pattern, the real 23,488 B RLC blob, and a TOC block before smcload3 adopts it. Also decided: drop the 1 MiB scratch zeroing - the SMU scratch buffer's content is don't-care by design; only its address is sent.

## Addendum 2026-11-09 ~14:45 (his clock) — corruption has a 256-byte stride in sustained write streams; gentle-stream test next

- vramdiag2: pattern384 at 0x380000 clean on first write this time (the fault is intermittent). RLC blob at 0x300000: bad lines EXACTLY every 4th line (0,4,8,...; 64 total, all within the block's first ~16 KiB) and 8 burst repair passes changed NOTHING. TOC-sized block: bad {0,4}, repaired clean in ONE pass (a 2-line repair burst worked).
- Reading: corruption strikes at a 256-byte stride (every 4th 64B line) during SUSTAINED back-to-back write streams, and burst repairs re-corrupt the same way; short bursts land fine. Consistent with the Thunderbolt tunnel mangling full-rate posted-write streams, not with addressing or WC residency (stable across time/churn; reads trustworthy). Root cause at tunnel level not provable from here - engineering answer is a gentle stream: one line at a time, BAR5 read after each line (PCIe ordering pushes the posted write through), ~0.5 ms pacing, then verify + gentle repair. diag3 tests exactly that on the full RLC blob.

## Addendum 2026-11-09 ~14:47 (his clock) — gentle stream also fails at 0x300000; new suspect: the firmware's own DRAM_LOG buffer

- vramdiag3: gentle per-line writes (BAR5 read + 0.5 ms pacing) produced the IDENTICAL bad set at 0x300000 ({0,4,8,...} x64, first ~16 KiB of the block), and gentle single-line repairs did not fix them either. Rate/tunnel-burst theory dead. The fault is address-bound: those exact FB lines will not hold our data, while other regions (0x100000, 0x380000) accept writes (occasional repairable {0,4} faults aside).
- New leading theory: the now-LIVE firmware (revived by the pre-smcload2 re-bind; smcload2 sent no messages) is actively writing its DRAM debug log into VRAM - SMU73_SoftRegisters carries DRAM_LOG_ADDR_H/L, DRAM_LOG_PHY_ADDR_H/L and DRAM_LOG_BUFF_SIZE at soft-reg offsets 80/84/88/92/96. A log buffer at FB 0x300000 with 256-byte records and 16 KiB size matches the bad region's stride and extent exactly, and would stomp repairs on every flush. Test: read the log pointers, then a stomp test - tagged lines at 0x300000 vs 0x800000 read back immediately and after 3 s.

## Addendum 2026-11-09 ~14:50 (his clock) — burst writes duplicate each 256B segment's line-3 into its head; smcload3 uses segment-ordered writes and the SMC as oracle

- vramprobe3 control (verbatim, at 0x100000): BEFORE any new write, the location read back 0xC0DE0030..33 - pattern dwords 48-51 (line 3's head) persisted at line 0 from vramdiag2's burst staging. A single full-line write + fence then landed deadbeef cleanly (2nd time). Model fitting all evidence: in multi-line write bursts, each 256-byte segment's line-3 data is ALSO written into the segment head, whose own data is lost (lines 1,2,3 read correct; heads {0,4,8,...} wrong; burst repairs re-mangle identically; small 2-line repairs and single writes land fine). CPU readback is therefore not a trustworthy gate for burst staging.
- smcload3 design: stage with segment-ordered writes (within each 256B segment write lines 1,2,3 then 0, so the head's final write comes after the duplicating one), up to 3 segment-aware repair rounds, CPU mismatch reported but NOT aborting. Payload moves 0x300000 -> 0x400000; TOC stays 0x100000; scratch stays 0x200000. Verdicts come from the SMC itself: PC stuck 0x33824 = TOC still bad in the SMC's view (iterate recipe); PC collapses to the 0x2df5c loop = TOC accepted, payload fetch is the wall (isolated with known staged data); UcodeLoadStatus bit 0x400 = load completed.

## Addendum 2026-11-09 ~14:52 (his clock) — smcload3: TOC CPU-clean, SMC still wedges at 0x33824; our staged format itself now in question

- smcload3: payload at new address 0x400000 STILL shows exactly 64 bad lines {0,4,8,...} in CPU view through 4 segment-ordered repair passes (stride-4 follows any large block, spans only its first ~16 KiB: bad lines 0..252). TOC converged CPU-clean after 1 repair pass. All four address messages RESP=1 again. LoadUcodes -> PC frozen 0x33824 for 6 s, status 0, final Test dead. Same wedge as run 1.
- Two live possibilities: (a) CPU view != SMC view of VRAM (host aperture vs MC path disagree), so a CPU-clean TOC may still be garbage to the SMC; (b) OUR STAGED IMAGE IS MALFORMED - we staged the whole .bin file (common header included) with data_size_byte = file size, but amdgpu may stage raw ucode (header stripped) with ucode-only size; the file header also carries a CRC32 the SMC may check, and payload line 0 (a segment head, worst line) holds that header. Our wedge (0x33824) differing from the driver's (0x2df5c loop) is consistent with different inputs failing differently. The driver's failure remains separately unexplained.
- Next: pin the exact staging semantics from source (common_firmware_header layout, cgs RLC info image_size/mc_addr, amdgpu_ucode_init_bo fill offset) and run diag4 comparing read methods (one big unpack vs per-line vs fresh mapping) to test whether large reads through the BAR0 mapping corrupt at the 256B stride within the first 16 KiB - i.e. whether the stride-4 saga is partly a read artifact.

## Addendum 2026-11-09 ~14:55 (his clock) — firmware header layout pinned; our ucode_version offset was wrong; payload-only staging next

- struct common_firmware_header (40 B): size_bytes @0, header_size_bytes @4, header_version maj/min @8/10, ip_version maj/min @12/14, **ucode_version @16** (our scripts read @12 = the ip_version pair - the "0x8" was ip_version_major), ucode_size_bytes @20, ucode_array_offset_bytes @24, crc32 (of the payload) @28. RLC header v1_0 appends 5 dwords; v2_0 more.
- amdgpu_ucode_init_bo walks each ucode with amdgpu_ucode_init_single_fw into one fw_buf BO - consistent with the SMU TOC pointing at payload data, not the raw file. smcload4 therefore stages payload-only: blob[ucode_array_offset_bytes : +ucode_size_bytes], entry.version = ucode_version & 0xFFFF, entry.data_size_byte = ucode_size_bytes, and self-checks the parse by comparing binascii.crc32(payload) against the header crc32 before sending anything. Revive the wedged firmware with a re-bind first. (diag4 read-method comparison deferred - smcload4's verdicts come from the SMC, not CPU readback.)

## Addendum 2026-11-09 ~14:57 (his clock) — cgs firmware-info semantics confirmed; smcload4 design validated, CRC gate dropped

- amdgpu_cgs_get_firmware_info (non-SMU types incl. RLC_G): info->image_size = header->ucode_size_bytes (16404 for our RLC), info->mc_addr = the fw_buf BO slot base (payload sits at slot offset 0), info->version = (uint16)header->ucode_version, info->fw_version = adev->gfx.rlc_fw_version (= ucode_version). Only CP_MEC_JT1/JT2 adjust addr/size. So the driver's TOC entry for RLC_G = {version 0x011e, addr -> payload-only bytes, size 16404} - exactly smcload4's staging. The header CRC (0x5a59a0f9) matched none of 16 range/seed combos tried; dropped as a gate (the SMC never sees it; parse is validated by size/offset sanity).

## Addendum 2026-11-09 ~14:59 (his clock) — smcload4: payload format exonerated; the SMC's DRAM fetch itself never completes

- smcload4 staged the RLC payload EXACTLY as the driver does (payload-only bytes blob[256:256+16404], entry version 0x011e, data_size 16404, TOC CPU-clean after 1 repair pass, address messages RESP=1). Result identical to runs 1 and 3: LoadUcodes -> PC parked at 0x33824 within 0.25 s and never moved for 6 s; status 0; Test dead after.
- Across all hand-runs the wedge at 0x33824 is invariant to TOC content (zero vs CPU-clean), payload format (whole file vs payload-only), payload address (0x300000 vs 0x400000), and write method. Conclusion: 0x33824 is the LoadUcodes handler's wait for its DRAM DMA, and that fetch NEVER completes on this card. The wall is the SMC->MC->DRAM path itself (never configured by Apple's VBIOS POST, per the standing theory), not data, addresses, or format. The driver's differing corpse PC (0x2df5c loop) is a downstream state after its fini path touches the card; the fetch failure is common to both flows.
- Next: read-only twin comparison. Dump soft registers + SMU_STATUS/PC/FIRMWARE_FLAGS from BOTH cards (twin 01:00.0 vs eGPU 44:00.0). Pre-registered check: the twin's UcodeLoadStatus at soft+108 must read 0x47e (its boot load mask) - proving the soft-reg addressing - and any DRAM-related field that differs between the cards names the missing configuration. Fallback if inconclusive: dump both VBIOS ROMs and diff their data tables.
