## Addendum 2026-11-09 ~15:22 (his clock) — the dance works PERFECTLY on the twin; load blocked by the console framebuffer, not by the card

- Twin test, no-driver boot (photo-verified): the twin's virgin state matched the eGPU's signature exactly (PC=0x2950 ROM range, RESP=0x1, SMU_STATUS=0x0). smcdance_twin.py: pass 1 30,490 mismatches (the usual lossy blast), pass 2 BYTE-CLEAN, Test RESP=0x1 in 0.1 s, SMU_STATUS=0x3 DONE+PASS, ownership tail done, PC roaming (0x20588/0x205c0/0x23fb4...). The userspace dance is card-portable: the standard SMC firmware starts cleanly on a standard-VBIOS Ellesmere with no driver anywhere.
- smcload_twin2.py then died at line 22: PermissionError opening /sys/bus/pci/devices/0000:01:00.0/resource0 O_RDWR. Cause: the twin is the console device - the EFI framebuffer driver (efifb/simplefb) owns its BAR0, so the resource is busy for userspace. The eGPU never hit this because nothing ever claimed its BAR0.
- Fix (standard VFIO move): unbind the platform framebuffer driver, which releases BAR0, then run the load and auto-reboot; results land in twinload.log. The twin's danced firmware is live in the current boot, so the test must run BEFORE any reboot.

## Addendum 2026-11-09 ~15:37 (his clock) — *** THE TWIN LOADED: METHOD FULLY VALIDATED; THE eGPU's SMC DRAM FETCH IS GENUINELY DEAD ***

- The IDENTICAL procedure (same dance, same staging code, same TOC/payload bytes, same message sequence, same addresses) that wedges the eGPU at 0x33824 in <0.25 s completes on the twin in <0.25 s. Method, format, and dance are all exonerated end-to-end. The wall is a property of the Blackmagic card's state: its SMC's DRAM fetch never completes, and the difference from the twin is what a real VBIOS POST (EFI ran the twin's ASIC_Init at power-on) programs before any OS touches the card - the step the eGPU never gets under Linux (Apple POSTs it their own way under macOS).
- Side discovery: the twin's BAR0 staging was clean on pass 0 - the write-combine stride-4 corruption that plagued every eGPU staging is a Thunderbolt-path WC artifact, absent on the internal card's BAR0.
- Immediate next step (same boot): sweepdiff with BOTH cards in virgin pre-driver state - twin = pure EFI-POST state, eGPU = raw - the first comparison with no driver confound. Whatever POST programs for the fetch should be sitting in that diff.

## Addendum 2026-11-09 ~15:45 (his clock) — clean-room sweep: FB_LOCATION is ZERO on the raw eGPU; prime suspect is now plant-AFTER-dance ordering

- sweepdiff in the clean boot (twin = pure EFI POST state, eGPU = raw virgin, no driver anywhere): 259 stable diffs. The bulk are DRAM training (expected). Also 0x2004 MC_SHARED_CHMAP: twin 0x00433210 (= the macOS plant value) vs raw eGPU 0x00430210 - POST adjusts the channel map too; plus aperture regs 0x20b8/0x20bc twin 0x000c0400 vs raw 0x00000400.
- **Ordering theory (new prime suspect): every eGPU attempt so far started the SMC firmware BEFORE the FB window existed.** run.sh dances first and plants second; the dance-era firmware therefore initialized its DRAM client with FB_LOCATION=0, and if the SMC latches its DRAM addressing at start, no later plant can cure it. The twin test had FB_LOCATION set by EFI POST hours before the dance - and loaded instantly. Driver probes may share the flaw if their SMC start precedes GMC's FB programming. Test (this boot, eGPU still virgin): poke 0x2024=0xf4fff400 + 0x2004=0x00433210 FIRST, then dance, then smcload4. Pre-registered: status 0x400 sets = theory confirmed; another 0x33824 wedge = latch theory dies, SMC-space POST pokes (0x80000140, 0x800002b0) are next.

## Addendum 2026-11-09 ~16:07 (his clock) — vramprobe3 on the virgin card reads all zeros: the virgin DRAM is UNTRAINED; new poke-then-bind experiment

- Fresh power-cycle+reboot boot was another BAR0 dud; barfix2 assigned the standard layout (BAR0 0x4010000000, BAR5 0x80400000). vramprobe3's ladder then read ALL ZEROS at every rung, including full-line+fence - on this VIRGIN card the GDDR5 is untrained, so VRAM neither stores nor returns data. This is a virgin-state signature, not a bad BAR. Retroactive reinterpretation: smcload run 1's all-zero TOC readback (2026-11-09 ~14:35, virgin card, no prior bind) was most likely this same untrained-DRAM effect, not only the slice-assignment bug.
- Consequence: userspace staging is impossible until something trains the DRAM, and only a POST does that. New experiment to force every ordering variable at once: poke FB_LOCATION + CHMAP from userspace FIRST (window in place from t=0), then trigger the driver probe (modprobe/bind with the guard#1+#3 module). The probe's POST trains DRAM, the forced start fires with the window already set regardless of the driver's internal start/GMC ordering, and its own load flow then runs with trained DRAM + window. Outcomes: probe passes fw_loading (or fails later at a new stage) = ordering was the wall; same "SMU load firmware failed" -22 = the delta is SMC-space POST config (0x80000140 / 0x800002b0) or ASIC_Init's SMC writes, and the hunt narrows there.

## Addendum 2026-11-09 ~16:33 (his clock) — dance does NOT revive a load-wedged SMC; plant analysis: only FB_LOCATION + CHMAP are true inputs

FB_LOCATION the driver sets itself to the plant value. **CHMAP is a proven free input**: raw 0x00430210, macOS/plant 0x00433210, and a userspace poke of it survived a full driver probe untouched (the Linux driver never writes it). The aperture regs 0x20b8/0x20bc are unclassified. The 0x3a6c8 run - the only wedge that ever moved - had exactly the two proven inputs (FB + CHMAP) set before the dance.
- Frontier test (designed, not yet run): a boot where virgin SMC + trained ASIC + FB/CHMAP pokes + graded-good staging coincide, then dance -> smcload4. Virginity after mere reboots is a lottery (the 0x3a6c8 boot was virgin after two reboots with no recorded power-cycle - TB power behavior during iMac reboots appears inconsistent with the Nov 8 doctrine; unresolved). ## Addendum 2026-11-09 ~16:42 (his clock) — this reboot fully reset the ASIC (lottery lost); new deterministic path: reset-restart the resident firmware

- Boot grading after a plain reboot + barfix2: regpeek 0x2024=0x00000000, 0x2004=0x00430210 (plant WIPED, raw values), vramprobe3 ladder all zeros (DRAM UNTRAINED). This reboot cut ASIC power like a power-cycle: SMC virginity, training, and register state all reset together. The virgin+trained combination cannot be reliably manufactured by reboots - the 0x3a6c8 boot's overlap stays unexplained (likely a shorter power dip that reset the SMC logic but not the MC PHY state).
- New deterministic recipe (designed from today's facts, not yet run): the dance and virginity are only needed to get a standard firmware INTO SRAM and started. A driver bind does the same (forced start uploads polaris10_smc.bin) and its POST trains the ASIC; its load then wedges the SMC. If a SYSCON reset pulse RESTARTS the resident SRAM image (rather than returning to ROM-wait), the firmware would boot fresh on a trained ASIC with FB/CHMAP poked in place - every frontier condition satisfied in one boot, no lottery. Sequence: bind (train + upload + wedge) -> verify/re-poke FB+CHMAP -> reset pulse (exact assert/deassert values to be read from smcdance5.py's tail) -> verify liveness (PC roaming) -> smcload4. Fallback if the reset-restart leaves a dead mailbox: the lottery, or the ROM diff.

## Addendum 2026-11-09 ~16:51 (his clock) — reset pulse on a VIRGIN card: cleanly restarts the boot ROM; gfx.sh never probed the eGPU

- smcrestart.py ran against what turned out to be a virgin card (gfx.sh brought the desktop up but the eGPU was never probed - state at pulse time: PC=0x2a40 ROM range, SMU_STATUS=0, FIRMWARE_FLAGS read garbage 0x426328aa = no image resident in SRAM). FB/CHMAP pokes landed OK before the pulse.
- Pulse result: FIRMWARE_FLAGS cleared, RESET_CNTL bit-0 pulse applied; the boot ROM restarted into a pristine download-wait (PC roaming 0x28e8-0x2c8c ROM range, DIRECT MESSAGE=0 RESP=0x1 ARG=0, SMU_STATUS=0, SYSCON_CLK=0x01000000). The pulse is a safe, non-destructive SMC core reset - but with no SRAM image resident it can only restart the ROM. The reset-restart recipe remains untested against a wedged resident firmware; the immediate gap is that this boot still needs its training bind.

## Addendum 2026-11-09 ~16:57 (his clock) — bare-pulse restart FAILS (incomplete start); new door: corrupt the SRAM image to force ROM-wait on a trained card

- Reset-restart verdict: with the driver-wedged firmware resident (PC 0x2df5c, FIRMWARE_FLAGS=0x1), the bare pulse (clear FIRMWARE_FLAGS + RESET_CNTL bit-0 pulse) made the core roam SRAM briefly (0x20188-0x2e004), then settle into the same wait family at 0x2df24-0x2df2c with the mailbox dead (all messages RESP=0, Test dead). FIRMWARE_FLAGS never returned; UcodeLoadStatus read garbage 0x91203800 = the soft-register block was never initialized by this boot instance. **A bare reset pulse is not a start: the driver's/dance's full sequence (SMU_STATUS clear, clock/reset sequencing, likely a ROM-mediated verified boot) is load-bearing. Recipe retired.**
- Boot-target gating (from today's two pulses): pulse on a virgin card -> boot ROM download-wait (RESP=1); pulse with a valid SRAM image resident -> boots the image. The target appears gated on image validity. **New deterministic frontier path: on a trained card (post-bind), corrupt the SRAM image header via smcpoke, pulse -> ROM should reject the image and fall back to download-wait (re-virginized WITHOUT an ASIC power event, so training and the FB/CHMAP pokes survive) -> run the real dance (full correct start) -> smcload4.** Worst case is a lost boot; SRAM is volatile, no flash risk. Not yet run.

## Addendum 2026-11-09 ~17:01 (his clock) — protection mode SEALS a running firmware's image; writes to SRAM 0x20000 ignored while it runs

- Corrupt-image attempt failed at the write step: smcpoke wrote 0x0 to SRAM 0x20000-0x2001c on the wedged firmware; 7 of 8 readbacks MISMATCHED with scrambled-looking values (0xa6e46fc4, 0x6d82d211...). The image never changed; the subsequent pulse just re-sat the core in its 0x2df2c wait. **In protection mode, once a firmware has started, its SRAM code region is sealed against host writes (and reads return scrambled data).** This is itself the deep reason the dance requires the virgin state - the image area is only writable before the first start seals it.
- Corollary from the driver source flow: the driver's own upload runs while the core is HELD in reset (assert -> upload -> clear status -> clock on -> deassert). Next test (designed): assert-and-HOLD reset via a new smchold.py, corrupt the header under the hold, release via smcrestart.py, check for the virgin signature, then dance + smcload4 on the still-trained ASIC.

## Addendum 2026-11-09 ~17:05 (his clock) — corrupt-image trick DEAD (sealed writes never land, release test confirms); pivot to the ROM ASIC_Init diff

- Hold test: smchold.py asserted RESET_CNTL bit 0 (0x40000000 -> 0x40000001, PC read 0x0 while held - the hold genuinely stopped the core). The 8 header writes under the hold read back the IDENTICAL scrambled constants as the running attempt - the seal is protection-unit state that persists across a core reset; host writes to the code region do not land. Release test: smcrestart pulse -> PC straight back to the 0x2df24/0x2df2c wait, RESP=0, no virgin signature. **In-boot re-virginization is exhausted: bare pulse, held pulse, and image corruption all fail. Virginity comes only from power events, which also kill training - the frontier (dance start on a trained ASIC) remains untested except the one lottery boot (0x3a6c8, ruined staging).**
- State: eGPU = half-restarted firmware instance wedged at 0x2df2c, mailbox dead, ASIC trained, FB/CHMAP pokes in place, desktop up. Next main road: the offline ROM diff - dump both VBIOS ROMs (the working internal twin is the reference ASIC_Init) and compare the AtomBIOS command-table write lists. Analysis labour is offline; the dumps are the only machine-side step.

## Addendum 2026-11-09 ~17:20 (his clock) — ROM teardown verdict: no missing POST write; the delta is the start procedure (Apple EFI)

- Both VBIOS images dumped (60,928 B each, checksums valid; eGPU via 4K-block sysfs ROM reads after /dev/mem and debugfs routes failed; twin via debugfs amdgpu_vbios) and fully torn down offline (kernel atombios.h/atom.c as reference; report: blackmagic-egpu-rom-diff.md; ROMs + tools in files/roms/).
- Result: the two init trees are functionally the same program. ASIC_RegistersInit's MC writes identical; MemoryControllerInit byte-identical; MC_InitParameter's 117 (register,value) pairs 100% identical; SMU_Info identical; FirmwareInfo differs by one voltage byte; SMC_Init is a zero-size stub in BOTH. VRAM_Info differs correctly per board (8 Gbit modules vs 4 Gbit). The only structural omission: MemoryTrainingInfo (data[29]) absent on the eGPU - DRAM pattern training data, while the eGPU's DRAM works for all other clients post-POST.
- **The "Apple's POST omits a register write" theory is dead.** Reframe: the twin's standard SMC firmware runs from machine POST (loaded, 0x5FE) because Apple's UEFI starts it on the internal card; nothing ever starts the standard firmware on the external card under Linux (virgin ROM-wait). The working/dead delta lives in the start procedure. Remaining roads: (1) retail Ellesmere VBIOS teardown to see what a normal card's SMC_Init does (both Apple images stub it); (2) the still-untested frontier - dance-start on a trained ASIC with FB/CHMAP poked.

## Addendum 2026-11-09 (late aft.) — userspace AtomBIOS executor BUILT and dry-validated
- the author asked whether MemoryTrainingInfo could be pulled from macOS. Answer: no (macOS contains no AtomBIOS-format data — Apple's stack never uses AtomBIOS), and it wouldn't matter: the eGPU's MemoryTraining bytecode never selects data block 29 while the twin's does — the two VBIOSes are authored as matched sets (table present-and-used vs absent-and-unused), so this is not a deletion. His follow-up named the pattern: every road routes through the AtomBIOS layer, the one thing nobody runs on this card (macOS never, Apple's EFI only on the internal card, Linux only inside the probe fused with the SMC start).
- New tool `atomrun.py` (roms/atomrun.py): a faithful userspace port of the kernel Atom parser (opcode_table + get/put operand semantics extracted from torvalds/linux atom.c), executing a ROM's command tables with MM registers via BAR5 (live) or a shadow dict (dry). Key semantics nailed during the build: arg classes REG0 PS1 WS2 FB3 ID4 IMM5 PLL6 MC7 (no PCI class; IMM=5); EOT=91, opcode 0 ends a table; SETREGBLOCK operand is a U16; the REG[0] write quirk (value<<2) is the MC index/data window at MM dwords 0/1 (the MC_InitParameter pair loop is an MC-space programmer); IIO programs come in read/write pairs (ids 0x02/0x82, 0x03/0x83, 0x05/0x85) which is why kernel get uses iio[mode&0x7F] and put uses iio[mode&0xFF]; root PS for ASIC_Init is NOT zeros — amdgpu_atom_asic_init sets ps[0]=FirmwareInfo default SCLK, ps[1]=default MCLK (both read 30000 = 300 MHz here), ps_size 16.
- Dry-run validation (shadow registers): both ROMs execute ASIC_Init identically (213 MM writes) up to a genuine hardware poll in table 77 (TEST REG[0x48e0].BYTE0,1 + JUMP EQUAL loop — a wait-for-bit the shadow can never satisfy). SetMemoryClock (34) completes cleanly after the setregblock fix; MemoryTraining (64) and table 33 complete standalone; MemoryControllerInit (5) executes 119k MM writes standalone before looping in callee table 18 (training-sweep-shaped; real hardware resolves). A 5 s wall-clock stuck-jump guard mirrors the kernel's stuck-table abort for live runs.
- Live test queued with the author at the machine: power-cycle (virgin SMC) -> reboot (blacklist keeps amdgpu out) -> wake card -> atomrun --live (ASIC_Init) -> grade with vramprobe3/regpeek -> plant pokes -> dance -> smcload4, all in one boot. This manufactures the frontier state (POSTed + trained + virgin SMC) deterministically instead of relying on the reboot lottery.

## Addendum 2026-11-09 (evening) — FIRST LIVE atomrun: 211 writes deep, stuck poll, two causes found
- the author ran atomrun --live on the virgin card (after barfix2 fixed a dud BAR0 to the standard layout). Result: root PS correct (30000/30000), 1,887,746 instructions, 211 MM writes, ABORTED on the 5 s stuck-poll guard at a jump target +0x180, notes = 6 MM reads + 6 MM writes "OOB".
- Cause 1 (tool gap, FIXED): six register accesses target byte offsets >= 0x40000 (0x48018/1c/20 and 0x5132c/30/34 — PLL config triples, values 0x3200/0x21c00/0x123d1110), beyond BAR5's 256 KiB. The driver reaches those via the PCIE index/data window (PCIE_INDEX dword 0xE / PCIE_DATA dword 0xF; write the BYTE address, read back to flush, then access DATA — amdgpu_mm_rreg/wreg -> pcie path, verified in v6.12 amdgpu_device.c). atomrun.py patched to do the same in live mode. If the stuck poll waits on a PLL whose config writes were being dropped, this alone explains the stick.
- Cause 2 (procedure, to rule out on re-run): the wake steps (setpci COMMAND=0002 + power/control=on) were run BEFORE barfix2; a PCI remove+rescan does not preserve them, so the run may also have talked to a decode-off/suspended card. Re-run sequence now re-wakes AFTER any barfix2 and verifies COMMAND + a regpeek sanity read first.
- The card was left half-POSTed (clock/display-gating writes only; ASIC_Init aborts before MemoryControllerInit, so MC/DRAM untouched; SMC still virgin). Re-running the full ASIC_Init from the top is the clean retry; power-cycle is the fallback reset.

## Addendum 2026-11-09 (evening) — live run #2: same poll; trace shows the SMU block powered down; ORDER HYPOTHESIS INVERTED
- Run #2 (patched atomrun, COMMAND=0002 verified, card awake, virgin ASIC after the power-cycle): no OOB notes (PCIE-indirect path works), 211 writes, stuck at the same poll (jump target +0x180) as run #1. The dropped PLL writes and the wake state are thereby EXONERATED as causes.
- Trace tail (post-trace2.txt) is decisive: in the 0x120xx block (SMU host registers, BAR5 byte 0x12000+), every read-modify-write writes back all-ones-derived values (0x1225c: 0xffffffff / 0xffffffbf alternating; 0x120ec/0x120f0: 0xffffffff -> 0xffff0000; 0x1207c: real IMM write 0x7d then 0xffffff00). Live reads of that block return 0xFFFFFFFF — the block is powered/clock-gated down. The dry run reads back shadow values there, which is why dry and live take the same path into the same wait. The poll chain sits in table 20 (called by 77, under EnableDispPowerGating cmd[13]); the whole ASIC_Init trace contains ZERO SMC mailbox writes (0x250/0x254/0x290), so userspace POST never touches the SMC.
- Inference: the Atom POST tables assume the SMU block is awake, i.e. in every environment where this POST completes (Apple EFI on the twin; the Linux driver), the SMC firmware is already RUNNING before ASIC_Init executes. The frontier design had the order backwards. New sequence, same end state: DANCE FIRST (start the standard firmware from the virgin state), then atomrun POST (trains the ASIC under a live SMU), then plant-if-needed + smcload4 — all one boot. Test in progress with the author at the machine (SMC still virgin in the current boot; run #2 left only clock writes, MC untouched).

- Full clean boot chain executed by the author: power-cycle -> reboot (tty) -> wake -> smcdance5 (textbook: pass 2 byte-clean, Test RESP=1, SMU_STATUS=0x3 DONE+PASS, ownership tail, PC roaming SRAM) -> atomrun --live. ## Addendum 2026-11-09 (evening) — the stuck poll NAMED: display ComboPHY TX clock status, not the SMU
- Offline decode (linear-sweep disassembly of cmd[77] DIG2TransmitterControl + AMD dce_11_2_d.h): the live run's abort target +0x180 is the case-PS=0x00 (transmitter disable) loop: TEST dword 0x48d1 bit 2 / JUMP_NOTEQUAL back — it waits for bit 2 of mmDPCSTX_TX_CLOCK_CNTL (byte 0x12344) to CLEAR. Neighbouring case (+0x170) waits for the same bit to SET: a clock-running handshake bit.
- Register geography corrected: dword 0x48xx (byte 0x120xx-0x123xx) is the DCIO/UNIPHY ComboPHY block, NOT the SMU. Header names: 0x48e0 = mmCMD_BUS_TX_CONTROL_LANE0 / UNIPHY_MACRO_CNTL_RESERVED32; 0x48e2 = mmCMD_BUS_GLOBAL_FOR_TX_LANE0; 0x4897 = mmPHY_AUX_CNTL (the register whose live RMW values were all-ones-derived in the run #2 trace tail); 0x48d1 = mmDPCSTX_TX_CLOCK_CNTL.
- So the userspace POST dies in EnableDispPowerGating's DIG control because the eGPU's display-PHY register block reads 0xFFFFFFFF (not decoding / powered down) in the virgin state, and the PHY clock-status bit can never clear. The SMC/SMU was never the poll's subject.
- Deciding test queued for the author (tool built: regpeek5.py, resource5-based, PCIE-indirect for >256K): peek 0x12344/0x12380/0x1225c on BOTH cards in the virgin boot (eGPU woken first). Twin sane + eGPU FF = PHY block powered on the twin (EFI drives the internal panel through it), held down on the eGPU. Twin FF too = the block is dark on any unposted card and a driver-environment step wakes it.

## Addendum 2026-11-09 (evening) — CORRECTION: the PHY block reads FINE on the virgin eGPU; FF inference retracted
- Direct peeks (new regpeek5.py, resource5 path) in the virgin boot, eGPU woken, BOTH cards: dword 0x48d1 (TX_CLOCK_CNTL) = 0x000000f1, 0x48e0 = 0x00000106, 0x4897 (PHY_AUX_CNTL) = 0x00010001 — IDENTICAL on eGPU and twin, all sane. The DCIO/PHY block was never dead or held down on the eGPU.
- RETRACTED: the earlier claims that the 0x48xx block "reads 0xFFFFFFFF / is powered down" (and the SMU framing before it). That inference came from all-ones WRITE values in the atomrun trace tail; writes alone cannot prove read values (write-1-to-clear and computed all-ones patterns produce the same lines). The block-aliveness question is settled by direct measurement: alive.
- Consequence: TX_CLOCK_CNTL bit 2 is ALREADY 0 on the virgin card, so the +0x180 poll should pass instantly on a true read. The stuck poll therefore means one of: (a) the run's own earlier writes start the TX clock and the hardware never reports it stopped, or (b) the executor's read path diverges from hardware during the run (e.g. after a SETPORT/IIO switch — the dry shadow runs cannot catch this class of bug; atomrun has never been validated against a known-good card).
- Discriminator queued: re-run atomrun on the eGPU, then immediately re-peek 0x12344. Bit 2 set post-abort = the run changed real state (case a). Still 0xf1 = the executor mis-reads during the run (case b) and the executor gets debugged offline.

## Addendum 2026-11-09 (evening) — the run KILLS THE BUS; the poll was a symptom
- Discriminator result: pre-run peek TX_CLOCK_CNTL = 0xf1 (sane, both cards); atomrun run #4 stuck at +0x180 again; IMMEDIATE post-run peek = 0xFFFFFFFF. Combined with vramprobe3's all-FF BAR0 reads after the dance-first run, the pattern is consistent: an atomrun run leaves the eGPU dead on the bus. The poll spins because every read after the kill returns FF (bit 2 "set" forever), not because the PHY clock misbehaves. Some write in ASIC_Init's early tables (71/2/13/78/51, before table 77's first poll) kills BAR decode; prime suspects are the six high writes (byte 0x5132c/0x51330/0x51334 and 0x48018/0x4801c/0x48020, same value triple 0x3200/0x21c00/0x123d1110, executed inside table 78) which reach past BAR5 via the PCIE indirect window. Run #1 does not exonerate them (its wake was botched). Dry runs cannot reproduce (shadow state has no bus to kill; dry aborts at the LATER +0x190 poll instead).
- Built atomrun_probe.py (atomrun + a probe_live hook: raw direct read of dword 0x48d1 after every table entry and every calltable return; aborts with "BUS DIED at <tag>" on an FF read). Dry-verified identical (213 writes). Awaiting a live bisect run; revive after each death = enclosure power-cycle + rescan.

## Addendum 2026-11-09 (evening) — bus death confirmed total, inside ClockSource (table 71)
- Post-probe-run peeks: CONFIG_MEMSIZE (byte 0x5428) AND TX_CLOCK_CNTL both 0xFFFFFFFF — the whole device dies, not just the PHY block. Probe bisect (atomrun_probe.py): bus alive entering table 71, dead after it returned; 56 instructions, 96 writes, 0.2 ms delays.
- Table 71's write list (dry trace, lines 2-98): ~90 of 96 writes go through an SMC indirect port (MM byte 0x208/0x20C = SMC IND INDEX/DATA) into SMC-space clock registers (index values 0xC05xxxxx = SMU clock/PLL file, plus 0xC0000010/0xC0000014); the table's final write sets the port index to 0x80000370 = SMC_PC_C. Two plain MM writes zero dwords 0x1A2/0x1A7. The kernel survives this exact table on this card (post-probe residue peeks always answered), so the kill is a delta in the executor's execution of it — per-write bisect is the tool.
- Built atomrun_probe3.py: tracks the current table and, while inside table 71, probes CONFIG_MEMSIZE after EVERY register write, printing write #, address, value, io_mode and reg_block; aborts naming the killing write. Dry-verified identical (213 writes).

## Addendum 2026-11-09 (evening) — KILLING WRITE FOUND: table 71, write #71
- Per-write bisect (atomrun_probe3.py) output: writes #1-70 fine, then W#68 INDEX(0x208)=0xC0000014, W#69 DATA(0x20C)=0x00010001, W#70 INDEX=0xC0000000, W#71 DATA=0x1f058531 -> bus FF immediately after. Probe caveat: its CONFIG_MEMSIZE referee reads 0x00000000 in the virgin state (live zero, not the macOS-state 0x2000) — the 0->FF transition is still the death marker.
- Context: most of table 71's writes run under io_mode 0x85 (SETPORT_ATI port 5, IIO-mediated PLL/SMC-space accesses via the port at MM 0x208/0x20C); writes #68-71 run under io=0x00 as manual port pokes. W#71's value is an OR-bit-0 on a read that returned 0x1f058530 — a garbage-looking value for an SMC/RCU-region register (0xC0000014/0xC0000000 are neighbours of RCU_UC_EVENTS 0xC0000004). Open question: is 0x1f058530 the register's true content (then the kernel writes the same and survives -> the delta is later, likely an IIO-computed PLL value from W#3-67 leaving the SMC clock dead so the reset at W#71 never releases), or is the executor's read of that transaction corrupted (port settle / IIO read program)?
- Ground-truth test built: smcport.py — manual port transaction with 1 ms settle and a double read that flags disagreement. To run on the revived virgin card for 0xC0000000, 0xC0000014, 0xC0500174.

## Addendum 2026-11-09 (evening) — driver source verdict: NO hidden pre-POST step on a virgin card
- Twin atomrun test: hard-froze the whole iMac (grey screen, caps-lock dead, fans spiralling after). Forced power-off discarded the run's unsynced output — twinrun.txt is EMPTY; no data recovered. Not to be repeated casually.
- Read torvalds/linux v6.12 amdgpu_device.c + vi.c for the exact POST environment: amdgpu_device_init order = ip_early_init -> reset-on-init check -> need_post -> amdgpu_device_asic_init -> ip_init. Inside asic_init: amdgpu_asic_pre_asic_init(adev) then amdgpu_atom_asic_init. For VI: vi_pre_asic_init is an EMPTY function. vi_need_reset_on_init reads SMC SYSCON_CLOCK_CNTL_0 + SMC_PC_C via RREG32_SMC and returns true ONLY if the SMC clock is enabled AND PC >= 0x20100 (running from SRAM); on a virgin card (PC ~0x2b4c, ROM) it returns FALSE — so the stock driver does NOT reset before POST on a virgin card either (consistent with guard #1 being a no-op in virgin state). No hidden preparation step exists in the driver flow; the POST stream is the stream.
- Remaining environmental delta on the table: the driver's card arrives via a full PCI probe (device enabled by the PCI core), ours via a manual wake with COMMAND=0002 (memory decode only). Test queued: wake with COMMAND=0006 (adds bus-master) and run plain atomrun on a fresh power-cycle.

## Addendum 2026-11-09 (evening) — COMMAND=0006 exonerated; executor diffs come back CLEAN; probe2 still never run
- Run #5 (wake COMMAND=0006, bus-master on): identical death — stuck poll +0x180, 211 writes. Bus-master joins the exonerated list (dropped PLL writes, wake/decode state, dance-first order, PHY-block virgin death).
- Line-by-line diffs of atomrun.py against torvalds/linux atom.c (the promised microscope): IIO index parse (atom_index_iio) identical; atom_iio_execute op semantics identical; ps_shift = ps/4 identical; amdgpu_atom_asic_init builds root PS from FirmwareInfo DEFSCLK/DEFMCLK identically (30000/30000); the W#71 op (OR DATA-port BYTE0 with 0x01, merge on the read) faithful. No divergence found in any diffed path.
- Re-examination of the probe evidence: probe1's "death" referee was TX_CLOCK_CNTL alone — a register ClockSource may legitimately gate — and probe3's CONFIG_MEMSIZE referee reads a live 0 in virgin state, so its 0->FF flip marks A transition, not necessarily THE kill. The two-referee boundary build (atomrun_probe2.py) was built but never run; it is now the decisive instrument: it shows whether the bus survives table 71, which table it dies in, and whether the PHY block ever relights.

## Addendum 2026-11-09 (evening) — probe2 verdict: TOTAL bus death inside table 71, both referees
- Clean two-referee bisect on a verified-live virgin card: PROBE enter table 0: CONFIG_MEMSIZE=0x00000000 (live zero), TX_CLOCK_CNTL=0xf1. Enter table 71: same. After calltable 71: BOTH 0xFFFFFFFF. 56 instructions, 96 writes. The death is total (GMC space dies too — not PHY clock gating) and happens inside ClockSource, consistent with probe3's transition at write #71 (OR bit 0 into SMC[0xC0000000] via window 0, on a read of 0x1f058530).
- New hypothesis (state, not stream): the driver's flow touches the SMC through window 11 immediately before POST — vi_need_reset_on_init reads SYSCON_CLOCK_CNTL_0 (0x80000004; ck_disable = bit 0) and SMC_PC_C (0x80000370) via RREG32_SMC. No userspace atomrun has ever been preceded by a window-11 touch on a still-virgin (boot-ROM, firmware-not-started) SMC; the one dance-primed run had the firmware RUNNING, a different state. If a window-11 touch wakes/primes the SMC clock-interface, every unprimed run dying in the SMC-clock table fits.
- Test built: smcprime.py — replicates the driver's two reads (twice each, printing ck_disable and PC) on the revived virgin card, immediately followed by plain atomrun. Readings themselves are diagnostic: ck_disable=1 in virgin state would give the hypothesis its mechanism; a value change between pass 1 and pass 2 would show the prime happening.

## Addendum 2026-11-09 (evening) — prime hypothesis DEAD; need_post gate found: BIOS_SCRATCH_7 bit 9
- smcprime on the virgin card: SYSCON_CLOCK_CNTL_0 = 0x01000000 (ck_disable = 0 — the virgin SMC's clock is ENABLED, cken bit 24 set), PC roaming 0x2c8c/0x28dc, identical across two passes. The window-11 prime immediately before atomrun (post-trace10) changed nothing: same death at the table-77 poll, 211 writes. Prime hypothesis and the ck_disable mechanism are both dead.
- Driver-source find (amdgpu_device.c + amdgpu_atombios.c, v6.12): for asic_type >= CHIP_BONAIRE, amdgpu_device_need_post() = amdgpu_atombios_scratch_need_asic_init(), which reads BIOS_SCRATCH_7 (mmBIOS_SCRATCH_7 = dword 0x5D0, byte 0x1740; bif_5_0_d.h) and returns FALSE (skip ASIC_Init entirely) if ATOM_S7_ASIC_INIT_COMPLETE_MASK (0x00000200, bit 9) is set. ASIC_Init normally sets that bit itself at completion.
- Consequence if the virgin card's scratch bit reads SET: the kernel has never executed ClockSource against the virgin boot-ROM SMC state on this card — the "driver survives the identical stream" premise collapses, and the virgin-state probes that "POSTed" need re-reading (the one-guard probe's trained DRAM may trace to its own boot's earlier attempts, and the poke-then-bind probe's forced start fires in ip_init regardless of whether ASIC_Init ran). Measurement queued: full wake, verify 0xf1, peek byte 0x1740 (+ 0x2024 FB_LOCATION) BEFORE any atomrun.

## Addendum 2026-11-09 (evening) — scratch bit CLEAR: skip theory dead; plan: kernel self-narration diff
- Virgin-state measurement: BIOS_SCRATCH_7 (byte 0x1740) = 0x00000000 — ATOM_S7_ASIC_INIT_COMPLETE bit CLEAR; FB_LOCATION = 0 as expected. So need_post returns TRUE on the virgin card: the kernel DOES run ASIC_Init (incl. ClockSource) from the virgin state and survives. The executor-vs-kernel framing is restored in full.
- Executor diff completed for the remaining operand classes (ID = U32 at data_block+idx, kernel parity; PLL/MC dispatch via IIO programs 2/0x82 and 3/0x83, kernel parity; SETDATABLOCK via data_table). No divergence found anywhere table 71 exercises.
- Definitive instrument chosen: the kernel parser ships with full debug narration compiled in (#define ATOM_DEBUG at atom.c:34; runtime gate = the global amdgpu_atom_debug, atom.c:73). Setting it to 1 makes a driver probe print every operand and value of its own ASIC_Init to the ring buffer. Plan: one-line sed in the build tree, rebuild the guard#1+#3 module, install, then ONE virgin-state probe with dmesg -w streamed to ~/egpu/atomdbg.log (atomprobe.sh; console loglevel dropped to 1 first so the flood skips the tty). Diff the kernel's table-71 stream against the executor's value by value. Side benefit: if the kernel's POST dies under printk latency, the SMC port protocol is latency-sensitive and Python's microsecond gaps were the bug all along.

## Addendum 2026-11-09 (evening) — ROOT CAUSE FOUND: atomrun.py's DST2SRC table was wrong
- The kernel narration log (atomdbg.log) shows ClockSource's first SMC op as AND_REG on REG[0x0083] with field .[23:16]: the kernel reads SMC[0xC0200000] = 0x00030004 and writes back 0x00000004 (AND 0xFC applied to byte 2). atomrun.py resolved the same attr (0xa5) to field [15:0] and wrote 0x00030004, leaving bits 16-17 set.
- Cause: atomrun.py's DST2SRC constant was not the kernel's atom_dst_to_src. Kernel: rows {0,0,0,0},{1,2,3,0}x3,{4,5,6,7}x4. Shipped executor had a fabricated table ([[0,4,2,3],...]) — every partial-field destination write in every userspace POST run was potentially written to the wrong field of the register. Dry runs could not show it (shadow reads are self-consistent); code diffs missed it because the table was assumed, never compared.
- Fix applied to atomrun.py: DST2SRC replaced with the kernel table verbatim; shift_left/shift_right/clear now resolve the destination through the original attr (the DEF_DST attr2 shortcut removed, matching atom_op_shift_*/atom_op_clear). ARG_MASK/ARG_SHIFT verified identical to the kernel. py_compile + table spot-check pass.
- Side findings from the same log: (1) amdgpu probes asynchronously — the twin's and eGPU's init interleave in dmesg. (2) This boot's eGPU never ran ASIC_Init under the kernel: the enclosure was powered BEFORE the host reboot, so the iMac's UEFI POSTed the eGPU itself (FB_LOCATION read 0xF4..., VRAM 4096M reported, scratch bit set, driver skipped the POST) and the probe then failed at gmc sw_init -22 with BAR0 unassigned at boot (no barfix2 run). The poke-then-bind probe of the afternoon is reclassified the same way (UEFI-posted, POST skipped) — consistent with all evidence. Every genuine virgin-state kernel probe (rescan-revive boots) did run and survive ASIC_Init.
- Next: live run of the FIXED executor on the virgin card. Pre-registered: if ASIC_Init completes (or at least passes table 71), the userspace-POST frontier (POST -> dance -> hand-load) is open again.

## Addendum 2026-11-09 (evening) — fixed-executor run VOID (state trap identified); two assistant inferences corrected
- the author corrects the record (twice, firmly): he pulled BOTH Thunderbolt and AC, for longer than specified, on this cycle as on every cycle. The assistant's "skipped power-cycle" and "which plug" inferences were both wrong; the empirical datum stands on its own: at peek time FB_LOCATION read 0xf4fff400.
- Mechanism that fits all facts: the narration probe (atomprobe.sh) left the debug amdgpu module LOADED for the rest of that boot. When the enclosure's TB link returned after its AC cycle, the resident driver bound the (genuinely virgin) card instantly on hot-plug and ran its own probe: need_post TRUE -> full ASIC_Init (FB_LOCATION set, kernel survives again) -> forced start -> SMC load fails as usual -> device left POSTed-but-failed. The author's manual rescan/wake/peek then found the already-posted card, and the fixed executor ran SECOND against a live MC/SMC-corpse state — re-POSTing a live card is the plausible host-freeze cause. The run is VOID as a test of the DST2SRC fix.
- Standing hazard named: once amdgpu is loaded in a boot, every eGPU hot-plug auto-probes. Any userspace-POST test must run in a boot where amdgpu has never loaded (fresh boot; blacklist keeps it out), with the enclosure cycled AFTER the boot, then rescan -> barfix2 if needed -> wake -> verify FB_LOCATION == 0 (hard gate) -> run.
- Awaiting: recovery from the freeze and the tail of post-trace11.txt.

## Addendum 2026-11-09 (evening) — fixed executor run: wall MOVED out of table 71; W#71 acquitted by the narration
- Kernel narration (twin ClockSource) on the two suspect ops: OR into SMC[0xC0000014] (@+0xbb) uses field [15:8]; the famous OR into SMC[0xC0000000] (@+0xca) uses field [7:0] with the twin's running SMC reading low byte 0x30 -> 0x31 — the same field and low byte the buggy executor produced from its 0x1f058530 read. W#71 was field-correct all along; the "RCU strobe" interpretation is retired. A 250 us DELAY sits between the two ORs in the kernel stream (the executor's table-71 delays total ~0.25 ms — consistent).
- Fixed-executor virgin run (post-trace12; gate verified f1 + FB_LOCATION 0): ABORTED at a stuck poll (jump target +0x1c) after 237 MM writes (buggy stream: 211). Table path tail: 77, 20, 77, 20, 13, 20, 35, 20 — the run cleared ClockSource (71), ASIC_RegistersInit (2), the display chain (13/77/20) and entered table 35 -> 20, where it stopped. Post-run peeks: BOTH referees 0xFFFFFFFF — the bus still died, ~26 writes later than the buggy stream's death.
- Conclusion: fixing DST2SRC moved the kill out of table 71 — the AND field bug was real but (at most) the first of the stream's problems. Whether the new death is another misresolved field or a distinct state issue is unknown; probe3's per-write method must be re-run on the FIXED code. Built atomrun_probe4.py from atomrun.py by single-source copy with a per-write two-referee hook in reg_write (CONFIG_MEMSIZE + TX_CLOCK_CNTL checked after every live write; prints the flipping write). No more diverged executor copies.

## Addendum 2026-11-09 (evening) — probe4 flips at W#71 again (hook implicated); STRATEGIC PIVOT: POST-and-stop driver patch
- probe4 (fixed executor + per-write two-referee hook), clean virgin gate: REFEREE FLIP at W#71 — byte 0x20C = 0x1f058531, both referees FF, 36 instructions. Identical write to the historic bisect.
- But the hook is now a prime suspect: post-trace12 ran the SAME fixed code with no hook and passed table 71, dying ~237. The hook inserts two foreign MMIO reads after EVERY write, including inside the SMC port's index->data transactions and inside every IIO program step. Working model: the boot-ROM-serviced SMC port tolerates only fast, back-to-back transactions; host-side dwell inside a transaction (Python overhead, hook reads) can wedge it — which would also explain why the kernel (ns gaps) never dies and why death sites look write-specific while actually being gap-sensitive. The userspace-POST road is therefore not just buggy but structurally fragile at Python speeds.
- PIVOT (supersedes the userspace executor as the POST vehicle): patch the driver to POST-and-stop. amdgpu_device_init already runs the full VBIOS POST faithfully at kernel speed on the virgin card; insert a BDBD:A14C guard immediately after the need_post block so device_init returns -ENODEV before clock-info/IP init — the SMC is never started, the unwind skips never-initialized IP blocks, and the card is left POSTed + trained with the SMC in download-wait: exactly the frontier state the dance + hand-load need. Cut point verified in v6.12 amdgpu_device.c (POST block -> clock-info -> fence -> ip_init). Tool: poststop_patch.py (anchor-verified, backs up to .pre-poststop, aborts without writing on mismatch against the 7.0.0 tree).
- After the probe with this module: grade via the dance's start-state checks (RESP=1 / SMU_STATUS=0 / PC in ROM) and vramprobe3's ladder; then smcdance5 -> smcload4 is the frontier test (hand-load against a kernel-posted, trained ASIC).

## Addendum 2026-11-09 (night) — FRONTIER TEST RAN: load still wedges at 0x33824; start-conditions theory DEAD
- The full frontier sequence ran clean: post-and-stop state live -> (the author's call) barfix2 first (the probe unwind had indeed left BAR0 unassigned; fixed to 4010000000) -> re-wake -> gate 12344=0xf1, 2024=0xf4fff400 (kernel POST's FB_LOCATION, live) -> smcdance5 start state PC=0x28e8 RESP=0x1 SMU_STATUS=0x0 (virgin download-wait ON A POSTED CARD — first time ever combined), upload byte-clean pass 2, Test 0.1 s, SMU_STATUS=0x3 DONE+PASS, PC roaming SRAM. The dance on a kernel-posted card is flawless.
- smcload4 on that state: address messages RESP=1/1, TOC readback clean by pass 1, LoadUcodes 0x400 -> RESP never came, PC parked at 0x33824 from t+0.25 s through 6 s, status 0, final Test dead. THE CLASSIC WEDGE, on a fully kernel-posted, trained ASIC with the standard firmware running. Pre-registered verdict: start-conditions theory is DEAD. The SMC's DRAM fetch does not complete on this card under Linux in any state we can manufacture; everything else about the SMC works.

Retry the frontier with COMMAND=0006. The recipe is now mostly automated: power-cycle the card -> the resident post-and-stop module re-probes on hot-plug (verify the journal line) -> barfix2 -> wake 0006 -> dance -> smcload4.

## Addendum 2026-11-09 (night) — 0006 run HIJACKED by the v2 guard's hole; BME still untested
- Recipe automation otherwise worked: fresh power-cycle -> hot-plug re-probe fired the stop (21:10:20) -> barfix2 -> wake 0006 -> gate f1/f4fff400 all correct. But the dance ABORTED on its start-state grade: PC=0x2df64 RESP=0 SMU_STATUS=0x3 — the SMC was already running a corpse loop, and smcload4 (run anyway by the block) read UcodeLoadStatus=0x6 residue and the handler loop at 0x2df5c — the exact fingerprint of the driver's own force-started SMC failure (cf. force-start probe #1). The dance's abort did its job: this run tested nothing about bus-mastering.
- Root cause (mine): poststop_patch v2 placed the guard INSIDE the need_post block, so it only fires for unposted cards. barfix2's remove+rescan re-probed the now-POSTED card: need_post false -> guard skipped -> probe sailed into IP init -> guard #3 force-started the SMC -> the usual failed load -> corpse at 0x2df5c/0x2df64. (Run 1 escaped this only because its barfix2 happened while the failed-probe device state did not re-trigger a full probe the same way.)
- Fix (poststop_patch.py v3): guard placed AFTER the need_post block, firing on every probe of BDBD:A14C, posted or not. Placement self-tested against a mock of the tree's shape. Same build also retires two liabilities: restore atom.c from .pre-debug (atom_debug back to 0 — end the log flood) and restore polaris10_smumgr.c from .pre-forcestart (remove guard #3, now unreachable for this card and a proven hazard if reached). Guard #1 (vi.c) stays. Then reboot (module reload), virgin cycle, modprobe, barfix2 (now harmless), wake 0006, dance-grade, smcload4 = the actual BME test.
