## 2026-11-16 — Clean bus-master test: NEGATIVE (bus-master exonerated)

First fully-clean LoadUcodes test ever run on this card — every known precondition true at once:
- v3 poststop module built/installed this day (stock atom.c + stock polaris10_smumgr.c restored; guard placed after the need_post block; modinfo gate = updates/amdgpu.ko). Guard fired: "posted, stopping before IP init" — card kernel-POSTed + trained, SMC never driver-started.
- Good PCI map (barfix2 run BEFORE the probe this time): root >4G resource present, BAR0 0x4010000000 (256M, above 4G), BAR2 0x4020000000, BAR5 0x80400000, all bridge prefetch windows open down the chain.
- Wake gate f1/0; dance byte-clean, SMU_STATUS=0x3 DONE+PASS, PC roaming 0x205xx before the load.
- COMMAND=0006 set and verified by readback immediately before smcload4.
Result: SMU_DRAM_ADDR/DRV_DRAM_ADDR handshakes answered RESP 0x1, LoadUcodes issued, then UcodeLoadStatus stayed 0x0 with PC parked at 0x33824 for the full 6 s poll — byte-for-byte the Nov 9 wedge. CONCLUSION: bus-master enable is NOT the missing ingredient; the COMMAND=0006 variable is permanently exonerated. The SMC takes the command and the addresses and waits at 0x33824 for something that never arrives.
Side findings this day: (1) barfix2 run AFTER a successful dance disturbs a running SMC (PC parked static at 0x2df64, RESP 1->0) even though its rescan never re-probes the card (journal showed exactly one guard line) — map surgery is not state-neutral; barfix2 belongs before the probe, always. (2) v3 guard placement verified working as designed.
Open doors after this result: (a) inspect what PC 0x33824 polls inside polaris10_smc.bin; (b) diff the driver's own SMC-start sequence (IP init between POST and LoadUcodes) against the userspace flow; (c) the parked atomrun two-referee probe + 0x5428 peek (Door 2).

## 2026-11-16 (later) — VRAM staging anomaly: writes that verify, then don't (vramstage_test v1/v2)

Staging the RLC payload (16,404 B = 257 lines of 64 B) at FB+0x400000 through the BAR0 mmap, on a kernel-POSTed card (v3 POST-and-stop), BAR0 above 4G:

- Bulk writes in ANY order (smcload4's segment order 1,2,3,0, or plain sequential) leave exactly **38 bad lines**: the first line of every 256-byte segment for segments 0–36 (payload offsets 0x0000–0x2400), plus the final partial line 256 (offset 0x4000). Segment leaders 37–63 verify clean.
- Per-line write + immediate verify + retry lands every line in the moment (each bad line needs exactly 1 retry) — yet a later bulk read shows the same 38 bad again.
- v2 discriminators: **lone re-reads agree with the bulk read** (38 of 38 still bad alone) — the corruption is real in DRAM, not a bulk-read artifact. Targeted lone rewrites of just the bad lines (3 rounds) **never converge**; the bad set is bit-stable over time (checked again after 1 s idle).
- Standing theory: the immediate verify is satisfied without the write durably landing (write-combining buffer forwarding on the BAR0 mmap), and the eventual flush drops segment-leader lines at those addresses. Not yet known: what the bad lines actually contain, and whether the "clean" leaders 37–63 are all-zero payload lines matching a zero backdrop by luck. v3 test (pattern fill at scratch offsets + content dump + BAR5-fence-after-each-write variant) designed 2026-11-16, pending run.
- Consequence for smcload4: its staging verify has been reading this same mirage since Nov 9 — the payload the SMC was asked to load may never have been intact in DRAM. The 0x33824 LoadUcodes wedge cannot be interpreted until staging is proven durable.

## 2026-11-16 (later) — v3 verdict: EVERY segment leader fails; the leader ends up holding the segment's LAST line

- Pattern fill (line i filled with byte i) at scratch 0x200000: **all 65 segment leaders bad**, not 38. The payload's "clean" leaders 37–63 were all-zero lines matching a zero backdrop by luck — v3's content map shows Z (all-zero) at exactly segments 37–63. The anomaly is universal: the first 64-byte line of every 256-byte segment never durably lands.
- What the bad leaders contain: leader line 4k holds line 4k+3's data — the segment's LAST line (line 0 reads back 0x03 fill, line 4 reads 0x07, line 8 reads 0x0b, ...). Lines 4k+1..4k+3 hold their own correct data. So the last line's data lands on the leader's address as well as its own (or replaces the leader's).
- A BAR5 fence read after every write (pattern+fence at 0x300000) changed **nothing** — identical bad set, identical contents. The Nov 9 fence trick does not rescue burst staging.
- Open (v4, designed 2026-11-16): do leader writes land when written alone (strided, no segment-mates written)? Do segment-mates' writes clobber an already-good leader? Does a temporally isolated leader rewrite after a full stage stick? Do dword-granularity writes stick where line writes don't?

## 2026-11-16 (later) — v4 verdict: burst theory dead — boundary writes fail even alone

- Leaders written **alone** (strided, segment-mates never written, fresh base 0x280000): all 65 still bad. Filling the mates afterward changes nothing. Isolated leader rewrites after a full stage (with BAR5 fence) never stick; dword-granularity leader rewrites never stick.
- So it is not write-burst assembly: a write to an address sitting on a 256-byte boundary does not durably land, by any userspace method tried (burst, sequential, per-line, strided-alone, dword).
- The question is now pure routing: where does a boundary write GO, and where does a boundary read COME FROM (v3: after a full stage, the boundary address reads back the segment's last line's data)? This matters beyond staging: the SMC fetches VRAM through the memory controller, not the BAR0 path — if the aliasing lives in the PCIe/BAR0 path only, DRAM may be intact and staging innocent in the LoadUcodes wedge. v5 (single-write routing table: one tagged write per fresh segment, read all four lines back, plus a cross-process write/read independence check) designed 2026-11-16.

## 2026-11-16 (later) — v5 routing table: position-0 writes never reach DRAM; position-3 writes double-land

Single tagged writes, one per fresh segment at 0x600000, read all four lines back:

- Write at position 1 → visible only at position 1. Position 2 → only at position 2. **Normal.**
- Write at position 3 → visible at position 3 **and at position 0**. (Isolated proof of the v3 observation: the segment's last line's data lands on the leader's address too.)
- Write at position 0 → reads back fine in the writing process (write-combining buffer forwarding — a mirage), but a position-0 write from a **separate process is invisible** afterward: it never reached DRAM.
- Clincher from v1: in segment order (1,2,3,0) the leader is written AFTER its segment's position-3 write, with nothing left to clobber it — leaders still bad. So position-0 writes are **dropped**, not overwritten.

**Model (POST-and-stop state, BAR0 userspace path):** writes to offsets ≡0 (mod 256) never reach DRAM; writes to offsets ≡192 (mod 256) land at both 192 and 0; offsets 64/128 are normal; reads honestly reflect DRAM. Consequence: with 64-byte line writes, a correct 256-byte segment is **impossible** — address k·256 can only ever receive line 4k+3's data. smcload4's staged payload has therefore been corrupt at every segment leader since Nov 9, regardless of its verify passes. Next: v6 write-SHAPE variants (one 256B write, 128B writes, 32B halves, u64 pieces for the leader). If shapes fail, diff what the driver's gmc_v8_0 MC programming sets that AtomBIOS POST leaves different, and poke it via BAR5.

## 2026-11-16 (later) — v6 verdict: write shape is irrelevant; the anomaly is in the path itself

Five write shapes, five fresh segments, identical outcome every time — position 0 reads back position 3's tag, positions 1/2/3 correct:

- S0 four 64B writes, S1 one single 256B write, S2 two 128B writes, S3 leader written last as two 32B halves, S4 leader written last as eight u64 writes — all give tags [3, 1, 2, 3].

So the corruption is not produced by our write granularity or ordering; it lives below that, in the BAR0 path's handling of each 256B block. Two models remain, and BAR0 alone cannot separate them (every BAR0 observation is identical under both): (i) write-side — boundary writes dropped, position-3 writes duplicated onto position 0, DRAM really is corrupt; (ii) read-side — writes are perfectly linear, but a read of position 0 fetches position 3's cell, so DRAM is intact and only our verify reads lie. Under (ii), smcload4's payload has been fine all along and the LoadUcodes wedge is a separate problem. Under (i), staging is truly broken.

**The untested variable: card state.** Every staging test on 2026-11-16 ran in the POST-and-stop state with the SMC never started — but the real smcload flow stages *after* the dance, with the SMC running. Next experiment: dance → COMMAND=0006 → re-run the v5 routing table in the post-dance state. If routing normalizes, the anomaly is a pre-SMC aperture artifact and staging in the real flow is trustworthy. If it persists, the road is the gmc_v8_0 source diff (what the driver's MC programming changes vs POST) poked via BAR5.

## 2026-11-16 (later) — post-dance routing: the read path was the liar (mostly)

Re-ran the v5 routing table after a clean dance (DONE+PASS, PC roaming 0x205xx, COMMAND=0006):

- **Seg E is the breakthrough:** a position-0 write by a separate process (tag 200) is now plainly visible — pre-dance it was invisible. Position-0 writes now land in DRAM and read back honestly.
- Seg A's backdrop line already read tag 100 — the value written by the *pre-dance* v5 run. It was in DRAM all along; the pre-SMC read path simply couldn't show it. The pre-dance "corruption" was substantially a **read-path mirage**: reads of position 0 fetched position 3's cell.
- Seg D still shows tag 103 at both position 3 and position 0 — but that segment is contaminated: the pre-dance run wrote the same tag to the same segment, so this cannot distinguish "position-3 writes still duplicate post-dance" from "leftover state now honestly read." v7 (fresh segment, fresh tags 777/888, then a full payload stage + bulk verify post-dance) designed to settle it; if the payload verifies 0-bad, smcload4 runs immediately on the live frontier.

## 2026-11-16 (later) — CORRECTION to the v5 model, and smcload wedges again post-dance

**The v5 "position-0 writes never reach DRAM" claim is WRONG.** Post-dance evidence overturns it: v5's seg-A tag 100, written by a lone position-0 write in the *pre-dance* state, was sitting in DRAM when read post-dance. The writes landed all along; it was the pre-dance **read** of position 0 that fetched position 3's cell. Corrected model: pre-dance, reads of offset ≡0 (mod 256) are aliased to offset 192; writes are linear. Post-dance, lone reads and writes are both honest (v7 seg F, fresh segment: tag 777 at position 3 did NOT duplicate; tag 888 at position 0 stuck, position 3 untouched).

**Still open:** the full-payload burst stage. Post-dance, v7 staged the payload in one tight loop and a bulk read still showed the same 38 bad leaders — but every "38 bad" verdict so far rests on a bulk read or on the aliased pre-dance state. Nobody has lone-read a burst-staged payload in the honest post-dance state. (Noted: smcload4's TOC, only ~8 lines, converged to 0 bad on its second pass — short bursts behave differently, or small bulk reads are honest.)

**smcload4 run, post-dance frontier (2026-11-16):** TOC converged (pass 0: 2 bad [0, 4]; pass 1: 0 bad). UcodeLoadStatus 0 before. Both DRAM address handshakes accepted (SMU_DRAM_ADDR 0xf400200000 RESP 1/1; DRV_DRAM_ADDR 0xf400100000 RESP 1/1). LoadUcodes (mask 0x400) returned RESP 0 and the SMC wedged at **PC 0x33824** for the full 6 s poll, status 0, and no longer answers even a test ping. Byte-for-byte the Nov 9 / Nov 16 wedge. If the lone-read check (v8) shows the staged payload is actually clean in DRAM, staging is fully exonerated and the wedge is a protocol/precondition problem: the SMC takes the message and both addresses, then spins at 0x33824 waiting for something that never comes.

## 2026-11-16 (later) — v8: burst-write corruption is REAL in DRAM; two separate anomalies, now disentangled

Lone reads (the granularity seg F proved honest post-dance) of the payload smcload4 just staged: **the same 38 leaders are bad in DRAM.** So there are TWO distinct anomalies, and the day's confusion came from them overlapping:

1. **Pre-dance read alias** (gone post-dance): reads of offset ≡0 (mod 256) fetched offset 192's cell. Made good staging look bad and bad staging look confusing; poisoned the v1–v5 verdicts.
2. **Burst-write leader corruption** (both states, DRAM-real): staging many lines back-to-back leaves the first line of every 256B segment holding the segment's last line's data. Lone writes with a BAR5 fence land perfectly (seg F). The scratch dump corroborates: v3's pattern fill is still sitting at 0x200000 with its line 0 holding 0x03 (line 3's fill).

TOC anomaly: smcload4 verified its TOC clean on pass 1 (small bulk read), but v8's lone reads now show the TOC region as zeros mixed with untouched backdrop pattern — either the SMC consumed/scrubbed it during the wedged LoadUcodes, or the small-region verify was buffer-fed and the TOC's DRAM leaders (its pass 0 bad lines were exactly [0, 4] — the leaders) never got fixed. Either way, at send time the SMC was very likely reading a TOC and a payload whose segment leaders were corrupt. The 0x33824 wedge is NOT yet exonerated from staging — it is fully consistent with the SMC parsing a leader-corrupt TOC/payload.

Next: v9 — stage the full payload with the seg-F method (per-line write + BAR5 fence + lone verify) and lone-verify the result, plus an 8-line burst control. If clean, patch ONLY smcload4's staging (source needed from the author) and re-run the load on a fresh frontier.

## 2026-11-16 (later) — v9: leaders verify clean mid-stage, bad at final verify — the segment-mates are the suspects

- Control: even an **8-line burst** loses its leaders — 2 bad [0, 4]. (So smcload4's TOC-sized bursts are not exempt.)
- Main: payload staged per-line + BAR5 fence + lone verify — **0 stubborn**, every line verified clean at its own moment (each of the 38 leaders needed exactly 1 retry). Then the independent lone-read verify: **the same 38 leaders bad again.** Between a leader's clean verify and the final check, the only writes are its own segment-mates. Pattern: **a leader survives only if written after its segment's position-3 line; a later position-3 write overwrites the leader with its own data** (the v3 observation, now with trustworthy reads).
- The one misfit: smcload4 already stages leader-last (segment order 1,2,3,0), yet v8 found its payload leaders bad in DRAM. But v8 read the payload only *after* the SMC had processed LoadUcodes and wedged — and the TOC region demonstrably changed in that same window (clean verify → zeros/backdrop). The wedged SMC trampling pointed-at VRAM is a live confound for the v8 payload reading.
- Next: v10 — controlled write-order comparison in a no-load window, post-dance honest lone reads: A sequential vs B segment-order (leader last) vs D sequential + all leaders rewritten at the very end. Whichever pass verifies 0-bad is the smcload5 staging algorithm.

## 2026-11-16 (later) — v10: write order changes NOTHING

Three passes of the real payload, per-line write + BAR5 fence, lone-read verify: **A sequential = 38 bad, B leader-last (1,2,3,0) = 38 bad, D sequential + all leaders rewritten at the very end = 38 bad** — identical leader sets. So the simple clobber theory is also dead as a complete explanation: in pass D the leader rewrites were the last writes to touch those cells, with only other segments' leader rewrites and reads afterward, and the leaders still read bad. Yet seg F's two-write segment read perfectly clean, and v9's 8-line control lost its leaders. The corrupting event is still unobserved directly — every test so far counts casualties after a battle instead of watching one segment while individual writes land. Next: v11 micro-test — one tagged segment, full content dump after EVERY single write, then foreign-segment writes, then a volume ramp (64 more segments) with the first segment re-dumped after every 8, to catch the exact write that changes a leader's cell and what it changes to.

## 2026-11-16 (later) — v11: the watched segment never blinked

One tagged segment at 0x780000, dumped after every write: L0–L3 land one by one and stay **[1000, 1001, 1002, 1003] through everything** — a stability re-read, writes in foreign segments, a foreign leader write, 1 s idle, and a 256-write volume ramp (segments 1–64 staged around it, seg0 re-dumped after every 8). No corrupting event observed. So the failure needs a condition the micro-test lacked. Remaining differences from the failing samples: (i) seg0 was staged under light traffic with a full-segment dump (reads of all 4 lines) after each of its writes — the ramp's own segments, staged without babysitting, were never verified; (ii) tag content vs real payload bytes; (iii) base address 0x780000 vs 0x400000/0x480000/0x500000. Also noted: the payload's "clean" segments 37–63 may be an all-zero stretch of the ucode array where any corruption is invisible — the anomaly may hit every segment uniformly and only show where content is nonzero. Next: v12 crosses the variables — tags ramp-style (no babysitting) at 0x780000 and at 0x400000, payload at 0x700000, all fully lone-verified.

## 2026-11-16 (later) — v12: address matters — line 0 at 0x780000 lands, at 0x400000 it never does

Three ramp-style cells (write + fence per line, no reads during staging, full lone verify):

- **T1 tags @0x780000: 64 bad — every leader EXCEPT line 0.** Line 0 landed and held.
- **P1 payload @0x700000: 38 bad** (the usual visible set).
- **T2 tags @0x400000: 65 bad — all leaders including line 0.**

Line 0 at 0x780000 has now landed cleanly in two independent runs (v11 micro, T1); line 0 at 0x400000 has failed in three (v9, v10 A, T2). So the quirk has an address geometry, not just a traffic pattern — though T1's lines 4+ still failed, so no tested address is simply "clean." (Confound noted: T2 ran third in its process; v10 A ran first in its process at 0x400000 and still failed line 0, so run order alone doesn't explain it.) Next: v13 address sweep — 8 tagged segments staged and verified at every 256 KiB base across the 8 MiB BAR0 window, mapping exactly which (base, leader) cells fail, plus a repeat-pass check at 0x400000. Practical stake: the TOC/scratch/payload addresses are ours to choose — if a clean neighborhood exists, smcload5 stages there.

## 2026-11-16 (later) — v13 sweep: no clean neighborhood — but pass 2 at 0x400000 is FULLY CLEAN

The address sweep killed the geometry theory: **all 32 bases fail identically on pass 1** (8 bad leaders each, 0x000000 through 0x7c0000 — no clean neighborhood anywhere in the window). But the sweep's one repeat — **base 0x400000, pass 2: 0 bad** — is the first fully clean staging ever recorded. The variable that matters is not address, order, shape, or content: it is **repetition**. Staging the same lines a second time (after a lone-read verify) makes them stick. This also retro-explains T1's line 0 at 0x780000 (that cell had been staged before, by v11's babysat micro writes) while its lines 4+ (v11 ramp writes, never read back) still failed. Open: whether the intervening verify reads are part of the recipe, and whether the recipe scales from 32 lines to the full 257-line payload. Also unexplained: smcload4 stages four passes and never converges — its stage() must differ from this protocol in some way (write order within the pass, fencing, or its bulk verify); its source is needed to see it. Next: v14 — payload double-pass at 0x400000 with verify between, and at 0x700000 back-to-back without.

## 2026-11-16 (later) — v14: the double-pass recipe is scale-limited — window between 2 KB and 16 KB

Full payload, staged twice: **A @0x400000 (verify between passes) = 38 bad on pass 2; B @0x700000 (back-to-back) = 38 bad.** So repetition alone doesn't fix a 16 KB region, while v13's 2 KB region went fully clean on pass 2. The recipe has a **locality window somewhere between 2 KB and 16 KB**: whatever the first pass warms (per-256B-block state in the path) has gone cold again by the time a 16 KB pass comes back around, but a 2 KB region is still warm for its second pass. Next: v15 — chunked staging: work the payload in 32-line (2 KB) chunks, each chunk staged+verified twice before moving on (v13's exact protocol at chunk granularity), plus a 64-line-chunk cell to bracket the window. If the chunked pass verifies clean end-to-end, that is the smcload5 staging algorithm.

## 2026-11-16 (later) — v15: every chunk verifies clean, the full verify still says 38 — the READ pattern is now the prime suspect

Chunked double-staging worked locally at both scales tried: **all 9 chunks (32-line) at 0x400000 and all 5 chunks (64-line) at 0x700000 verified 0 bad right after their second pass.** Then the final full-region verify of the same untouched data: **38 bad, the same leaders, both regions.** So the verdict depends on when and how the data is read, not only on what's stored: small local verifies (≤64 lines, right after staging) say clean; a 257-line sequential verify says the leaders are wrong. Combined with v13 (32-line region: pass-1 small verify bad, pass-2 small verify clean — write history matters to small reads) and v11 (a babysat segment re-read cleanly many times, always in 4-line reads), the standing suspicion flips to the read path: long sequential read streams may re-trigger the leader alias (a prefetcher-like effect), while short reads are honest. If so, DRAM may have been correct since the chunked staging — and the SMC, whose fetch is not our read stream, may have been looking at a good payload all along. Next: v16 — read-pattern forensics on v15's untouched regions, no writes at all: small re-verify of chunk 0, reverse-order verify, leaders-only strided verify, double-read of each leader, and fenced/delayed reads. Any disagreement among them is the read path confessing.

## 2026-11-16 (later) — v16: the read path is innocent (post-dance); the data really does decay after later writes

Six read patterns on v15's untouched payload — small chunk verify, reversed full verify, leaders-only strided, double-reads, fenced+delayed reads, small verify again — **all agree: 38 bad.** R1/R6 read chunk 0's leaders bad NOW, though v15's identical verify read them clean minutes earlier, with no writes to chunk 0 in between — only writes to *other* chunks and regions. So post-dance, reads are honest and consistent; **a leader that verifies clean is later corrupted by subsequent write traffic elsewhere.** Every "final verify bad" result fits this rule (the staging writes that follow a leader's own write corrupt it), and the only durable-clean samples remain v11's seg0 (written with a full-segment read after *every* line, then re-read constantly) and T1's line 0 — both at 0x780000, both possibly special. Black-box probing has hit diminishing returns: one last empirical shot (v17: stage the full payload with v11's exact protocol — read the whole segment after every line — at 0x400000, plus tags the same way at 0x780000). If that fails, the road changes: stop probing the path and fix it — diff what the driver's gmc_v8_0 / nbio programming sets in the MC and aperture registers against what AtomBIOS POST leaves, and poke the difference via BAR5.

## 2026-11-16 (later) — v17: v11 protocol at full scale FAILS — black-box probing closed; the pivot is GPU state

Payload with v11's exact protocol (whole-segment read after every line): **38 bad.** Tags the same way: **65 bad (every leader).** So v11's seg0 survival was not a protocol — it was first-touch luck at one address. Seventeen versions in, no userspace write protocol through the BAR0 mmap produces a durable payload. **Black-box probing is closed, as promised.**

The reframing that matters: **the desktop runs fine on this card's VRAM under the driver** (scanout, compositor traffic — no visible corruption), and **the stock driver's own SMC firmware load also fails on this card through the same VRAM-staging mechanism** (the Nov 8/9 −22) — our 0x33824 wedge is very likely the driver's wedge. The standing difference between "VRAM works" and "our staging corrupts" is GPU *state*: the frontier deliberately keeps the card at AtomBIOS POST's minimal memory-controller/clock configuration (driver stopped before IP init), while every known-good VRAM user runs under the driver's own GMC programming and DPM clocks. Bisect next (v18): run the plain v1 staging test in the *current* boot, where the driver is bound and the desktop is up. Clean → POST state is the culprit, and the fix is a register census diff (POST state vs driver state) poked in via BAR5 before staging. Still bad → the aperture path itself (BIF/PCIe/bridge) is implicated and the census narrows there.

## 2026-11-16 (later) — v18: driver state changes nothing — state theory dead; last untested variable is the write transaction itself

Plain staging in the driver-bound boot (desktop up, driver GMC/DPM programming in place): **payload 38 bad, tags 65 bad — identical.** So POST-vs-driver state is not the discriminator. Correction to the previous entry's framing: "the desktop runs fine" was weak evidence — amdgpu's bulk VRAM traffic is DMA (GPU-initiated), and the one workload that does use sustained CPU posted writes through BAR0, firmware staging, fails for the stock driver too. What has never been cleanly varied post-dance across all 18 tests: the write transaction itself — every test pushed 64B Python mmap stores back-to-back, so posted writes were always pipelined. Next (v19): write-with-acknowledge — each dword stored singly, read back from BAR0, retried until it matches. If per-dword serialization converges, concurrency on the posted-write path (bridge/BIF) is the culprit and the loader gets a staging method with per-dword proof. If even acknowledged writes can't converge, the remaining road is DMA staging (GPU-initiated copies), not CPU writes at all.

## 2026-11-16 (later) — v19: writes LAND (ack-proven); the corruption is post-write decay — time or traffic, one fork left

Write-with-acknowledge: every dword of the payload and the tags matched its BAR0 read-back after **at most one retry** (595 and 1,040 retries total, zero stuck). The write path is exonerated at last — each store lands. Yet the final verify still shows **38/65 bad**: dwords that were provably correct at write time are wrong after the remaining thousands of writes. Combined with v16 (clean chunk decays with no writes to it), the corruption is something that happens *to stored data afterwards*. Two candidate agents remain, and they imply opposite loader designs: **time** (a refresh/retention pathology at these addresses — then the fix is staging fast and firing LoadUcodes within the decay window, and smcload4's four verify passes may be exactly what dooms it) or **traffic** (later writes clobber earlier leaders — then the fix is write ordering: leaders last, nothing after, fire instantly). v20 separates them: one segment left alone with sleeps, one segment subjected to far-away write traffic, one to far-away read traffic.

## 2026-11-16 (later) — v20 fork VOID: segments fail their IMMEDIATE verify in driver state — dump the bytes before judging (v21)

v20's three segments (payload's first 256B at 0x500000 / 0x580000 / 0x600000) read **False at the very first verify** and stayed False through idle, write traffic, and read traffic alike. No previous test ever failed a freshly staged single segment instantly — so v20 cannot separate time from traffic; something about this run differs. Two live suspects: (1) the addresses collide with live driver allocations in the 5–6.5 MB band (a compositor-owned buffer would overwrite staged bytes within a frame — instant, permanent False), or (2) in driver state, with the GPU busy (scanout, DMA, DPM), the leader alias is now instant rather than traffic-developed. v20's boolean verdict hid the bytes; v21 dumps them: payload segment at the proven 0x400000 and at 0x500000, tag segment at 0x680000, first dwords of every line plus line 0 in full, immediately and after 5 s idle. Pixel-like data or zeros = allocation collision; line 0 holding line 3's data = the classic alias, now instant.

## 2026-11-16 (later) — v21: the alias in its purest form, instantly — and the loader question is now the only one that matters

The byte dump settles the phenomenology: a freshly staged segment, read immediately, has **line 0 = line 3's exact bytes** (payload: c0100009 cd030022…; tags: line 0 holds tag 3003, not 3000), lines 1–3 perfect, identical at 0x400000 and 0x500000, unchanged after 5 s idle. No allocation collision, no decay curve — in the current (driver-bound, SMC-wedged) state a single staging aliases its leaders at once. Reconciling all 21 versions: every CPU-view "clean leader" reading followed a *second* staging of that segment (v13 pass 2, v15's per-chunk second pass); every first-staging reading aliases. What no CPU read can ever answer is whether the alias lives in DRAM or only in the BAR0 read path — the SMC fetches through the memory controller internally, not through the aperture. So the staging investigation closes with a loader experiment, not another probe: **smcload5** = smcload4's exact protocol and TOC (ABI recovered from the 2026-11-09 records — TOC struct, entry layout, mailbox registers 0x250/0x254/0x290, message IDs — no source paste needed after all), with staging replaced by the chunked double-pass protocol and the messages fired immediately after, on a fresh frontier. The outcome matrix is decisive either way: load succeeds → the alias was a CPU-read ghost and DRAM was fine; wedges at 0x33824 with per-chunk-clean staging → staging is exonerated and the wedge is protocol/SMC-side.

## 2026-11-16 (afternoon) — the blind chain: dance PASSED; the load was killed by our own Ctrl-C, not by a typo

The author ran the frontier steps manually, then blind-typed the chained line (dance ; setpci 0006 ; smcload5 | tee ; gfx.sh) at the grey screen. dance.log proves the dance **completed byte-clean, DONE+PASS, PC roaming 0x205xx** — his typing was perfect. But smcload5.log does not exist: the load never started. Cause: the dance alone takes ~7 minutes (blast + two full 32,490-dword verify passes over the register-indirect path), which exceeded the assistant's 3–6 minute estimate for the *whole chain*; at ~7 minutes the assistant called it "parked" and told the author to Ctrl-C — and **Ctrl-C in an interactive shell discards the remainder of a `;` list**, so setpci/smcload5/gfx.sh were cancelled, seconds after the dance finished. He then ran gfx.sh separately and recovered the desktop. Lessons now standing: (1) dance runtime ≈ 7 min; a full frontier.sh run should be given 10–12 minutes of grey before anyone touches anything; (2) never advise Ctrl-C on a chained line you actually want to finish; (3) frontier.sh (placed in ~/egpu/ Nov 16) is the only sanctioned way to run this now — one visible command, internal gates, full log, GUI returns by itself. The smcload5 experiment remains unrun.

## 2026-11-16 (afternoon) — smcload5 RAN (frontier.sh, one command): wedge at 0x33824 with chunk-clean staging — STAGING IS EXONERATED

frontier.sh ran end-to-end unattended (13:13–13:23 CST): barfix2 applied the best map in step 1 (BAR0 0x4010000000, BAR2 0x4020000000, BAR5 0x80400000), regpeek pair f1→0, module gate passed (7.0.0-42 updates/), POST-and-stop guard confirmed, dance byte-clean DONE+PASS, COMMAND readback 0006. Then smcload5's first-ever run: **every payload chunk and the TOC verified 0-bad after the 2nd pass** (the CPU full-view at send time still showed the usual 38 aliased leaders — the known read-path ghost). DRAM/SMU addr messages accepted (RESP 0x1/0x1). LoadUcodes 0x400 → RESP 0x0, **PC pinned at 0x33824 for the full 6 s poll, UcodeLoadStatus never set, final Test ping unanswered** — byte-for-byte the Nov 9 wedge, third time, now under the cleanest measurable preconditions. **Verdict: VRAM staging is exonerated; the v1–v21 investigation is closed. The wedge is protocol/SMC-side** — something the real driver's LoadUcodes flow does that ours does not (prime suspect: our TOC has a single entry with num_register_entries=0; the driver's TOC carries the full ucode set), or the loader at 0x33824 waits on a condition this enclosure only satisfies in the Apple/Blackmagic host path. Next door (desk work, no hardware runs): diff the kernel's own flow in smu7_smumgr.c / polaris10_smumgr.c (tree at ~/egpu-build/linux-source-7.0.0 on the iMac) against smcload5's TOC construction and message sequence.

## 2026-11-16 (afternoon) — driver-source diff vs smcload5: three certain defects found; smcload6 built

Diffed smcload5 against mainline `smu7_smumgr.c` (`smu7_request_smu_load_fw`, `smu7_populate_single_firmware_entry`), `polaris10_smumgr.c` (`polaris10_start_smu`), `smu_ucode_xfer_vi.h`, and `smu7_ppsmc.h`. Confirmed NOT defects: PPSMC IDs match exactly (Test 0x100, DRV 0x250/0x251, SMU 0x252/0x253, LoadUcodes 0x254); message order matches (SMU_DRAM → DRV_DRAM → LoadUcodes); TOC struct/size (344 B) and RLC version truncation (0x11e) match; RESP 0x0 on LoadUcodes is normal (the SMC answers via UcodeLoadStatus, not RESP). The three certain defects in smcload5: (1) **RLC_G entry flags = 0; the driver sets flags = 1 (UCODE_FLAG_UNHALT_MASK) for RLC_G and CP_MEC.** (2) **data_size_byte = 16404; the driver subtracts the 20-byte digest on bare metal ("digest need be excluded out") → 16384** — exactly 16 KiB, plausibly the RLC instruction-window size, so the oversize may make the SMC copy 20 bytes past the window. (3) The driver zeroes UcodeLoadStatus before the address messages; smcload5 only read it (was already 0 — parity fix, unlikely causal). Held constant for the experiment: single-entry TOC + mask 0x400 (driver uses 9 entries + mask 0x47E; a coherent single-entry request should be legal), and the 0xF400000000 FB base (driver hands the SMC BO mc_addrs in aperture space; the base question stays open as the next variable if smcload6 wedges identically). **smcload6.py** = smcload5 + fixes 1–3 only, staged payload unchanged (full 16404 B, as the driver stages). **frontier-v32.sh** = pinned-path frontier calling smcload6. Both delivered to the author Nov 16; run pending his file placement.

## 2026-11-16 (afternoon) — smcload6 run: wedge INVARIANT at 0x33824; the FB_LOCATION discovery; smcload7 built

smcload6 ran via frontier v3.2 (13:37): all gates green, staging chunk-clean, TOC advertised 16384 with flags=1, status zeroed — **LoadUcodes wedged at PC 0x33824, identical in every particular.** The three parity fixes are exonerated as wedge causes (kept in smcload7 anyway — they are what the driver does). New prime suspect, found in the logs we already had: regpeek's second read on every frontier run is BAR5 byte **0x2024 = MC_VM_FB_LOCATION** (confirmed: radeon cikd.h `#define MC_VM_FB_LOCATION 0x2024`; gmc_v8_0.c programs BASE = vram_start>>24, TOP in the high half) — and it reads **0x00000000**. In a normal boot the driver's gmc init sets FB_LOCATION to the aperture base before powerplay loads firmware, and the SMC is handed aperture-based mc_addrs. In our POST-and-stop boot gmc init never runs, FB_LOCATION stays virgin-zero, so the SMC's fetch window decodes only raw FB offsets (addr>>24 == BASE == 0; our 1/2/4 MB offsets qualify). **Every load attempt since Nov 9 handed the SMC 0xF400000000-based addresses outside that window — an SMC DMA to nowhere, which never completes: a fixed-PC wedge with no error and no Test response, exactly the observed signature.** smcload7 = smcload6 + all SMC addresses built on the live-read FB_LOCATION base (base = (reg & 0xFFFF) << 24; = 0 in the frontier, so addresses become the raw offsets). frontier v3.3 calls it. Run pending.

## 2026-11-16 (afternoon) — smcload7 run: address theory DIES (FB_LOCATION is 0xF4-based at load time); smcload8 = full driver replication

smcload7 ran the full frontier (13:46). Its live read of MC_VM_FB_LOCATION **at load time** returned **0xf4fff400 (BASE 0xf400, TOP 0xf4ff → window [0xF400000000, +256 MB))** — the module's early init programs it before the POST-and-stop guard fires. The 0x00000000 in every regpeek pair is the *pre-modprobe* value and does not describe load time. So smcload7's computed addresses were identical to smcload5/6 (0xF400100000 / 0xF400200000 / image 0xF400400000) — **the 0xF4 base was correct all along; the address space is definitively exonerated** — and the wedge at 0x33824 held again. One new datum: at t+3.0 s the poll caught PC = 0x20714 (normal roaming range) for exactly one sample before returning to 0x33824 — read glitch or the SMC briefly surfacing; unresolved. Exonerated so far: staging (21 versions + chunk-verified), flags, digest size, status zeroing, message IDs/order, bus-master, address base, SMC virginity/dance. The largest remaining protocol difference: the driver ALWAYS sends a 9-entry TOC with mask 0x47E (RLC_G, CE, PFP, ME, MEC, MEC_JT1, MEC_JT2, SDMA0, SDMA1 — JT entries per amdgpu_cgs_get_firmware_info: MEC size = jt_offset*4−20, JT addr = MEC slot + ALIGN(size, PAGE), JT size = jt_size*4−20, JT2 = JT1 on VI); our 1-entry/0x400 request is a combination the driver never produces. **smcload8** replicates the full request (firmware read from /lib/firmware/amdgpu/ on the iMac, page-aligned slots, 120 s poll). frontier v3.4 calls it. If smcload8 wedges identically, the LoadUcodes path needs machine state the frontier cannot provide (gfx/CP block state or enclosure handshake), and the remaining door is reading the SMC firmware's code at the wedge PC.

## 2026-11-16 (afternoon) — smcload8/9/10: the full TOC LOADS FIRMWARE — SDMA0+SDMA1 in; the wall is now the CP block

smcload8 (full 9-entry driver replication) never ran: it died on its first open() — on this system the firmware lives at /lib/firmware/amdgpu/*.bin.zst (Ubuntu compresses it); the RLC copy in ~/egpu/ is the only plain one. Lesson banked: verify on disk every file a loader opens BEFORE spending a virgin cycle. smcload9 added a resolver (plain in ~/egpu/, plain or .zst in /lib/firmware/amdgpu/) and a desktop `--check` pre-flight that parses everything and prints the TOC it would send, no device access. The pre-flight immediately caught a real bug: the MEC jump-table fields are at header bytes 36/40, not 44/48 (gfx_firmware_header_v1_0 = 32-byte common header + feature_version). smcload10 fixed that plus a tripwire guard; pre-flight then cross-checked perfectly (jt_offset 65541 dwords + jt_size 101 dwords = the 262,568-byte MEC array exactly; MEC entry size 262,144, JT entries 384 at MEC slot + ALIGN(ucode_size,4096)).

smcload10 RAN (frontier v3.6): all nine images staged chunk-clean, TOC staged clean, addresses accepted — and **UcodeLoadStatus hit 0x6 within 0.5 s: SDMA0 (bit 1) and SDMA1 (bit 2) LOADED — the first firmware this SMC has ever accepted under Linux.** The 0x33824 wedge is gone with the full TOC. The SMC then sat in a NEW live loop, PC flickering 0x2df5c↔0x2df64, for the full 120 s poll, and the final Test ping went unanswered (SMC busy inside the LoadUcodes handler). Read: the SMC loads in mask-ascending order and is waiting on the next block — the CP family (CE is bit 3). The wall moved from "the protocol" to one block family. Next: mask bisect (smcload11 takes the mask as argv) — 0xE (SDMA pair + CE) first, then 0x406 (SDMA pair + RLC). Timing correction: a full frontier run takes ~3 minutes total; the dance itself is seconds (the old ~7-minute dance figure came from the blind-chain run and was wrong).

## frontier.sh — what the run script is and why (the author asked for this in the notes, 2026-11-16)

frontier.sh is the entire frontier procedure — everything that used to be typed by hand at the console — written once into a script that runs itself. It exists because of the NO BLIND TYPING rule: after the modprobe step the eGPU displays go grey and there is no visible console, so any flow needing typed input at that point is a design failure. The operator types exactly one command while the screen is still visible (`sudo bash ~/egpu/frontier.sh`); the script then drives, checks its own gates, logs everything to ~/egpu/frontier.log, and restores the GUI itself at the end on BOTH success and abort. No interactive prompts, ever.

Steps, in order:
1. **Wake block / BAR fix.** Apple firmware leaves the GPU's BARs unassigned; if BAR0/BAR5 read zero, barfix2 assigns the known-good map (BAR0 0x4010000000 256M, BAR2 0x4020000000, BAR5 0x80400000 — BAR5 can vary per boot) and programs the bridge windows. This MUST precede any regpeek (the all-zeros trap reads garbage).
2. **Register peek (informational).** BAR5 0x12344 should read 0xf1, 0x2024 (MC_VM_FB_LOCATION) reads 0 pre-modprobe. Not a hard gate — the dance is.
3. **Module gate (hard).** modinfo must show the poststop-patched amdgpu.ko under /lib/modules/7.0.0-42-generic/updates/ — the frontier boots the -42 kernel from GRUB's Advanced options because GRUB's default is -43 (stock module). Wrong module → abort before touching hardware state.
4. **POST-and-stop.** modprobe the patched module; the guard in amdgpu_device.c stops it after POST, before IP init. The grey screen starts here and is expected. The script greps dmesg for the guard line; absent → abort.
5. **The dance (hard gate).** smcdance5 uploads polaris10_smc.bin (129,960 B, v0x171a00) to SMC SRAM at 0x20000 over the register-indirect path and verifies it: pass 1 shows ~30,490 mismatched dwords (normal — the read-back aliasing pattern), pass 2 must be byte-clean, ending DONE+PASS (SMU_STATUS 0x3, PC roaming 0x205xx/0x23fxx). Anything less → abort.
6. **Bus mastering.** setpci COMMAND=0006 with readback — the card may not DMA until memory + bus-master are both on (0002 comes from the dance step; the loader needs 0006).
7. **The loader.** The variable step — currently smcload11.py (full 9-entry TOC builder + LoadUcodes sender; mask selectable via argv for bisects). It stages every firmware image into VRAM with the chunked double-pass recipe (each 32-line chunk staged and verified twice; per-chunk 0-bad is the criterion — full-view read-backs still show the leader-alias artifact and are not trusted), builds the TOC at VRAM 0x100000, zeroes UcodeLoadStatus, sends SMU_DRAM_ADDR → DRV_DRAM_ADDR → LoadUcodes, and polls status/PC for 120 s, logging every sample.
8. **GUI restore.** gfx.sh runs unconditionally at the end (and on every abort path) so the desktop always comes back on its own.

Design invariants for the finalized version: paths pinned to EGPU=/home/wednesday/egpu (under `sudo bash`, `~` expands to /root — that bug aborted frontier v2 instantly); every loader must pass a desktop `--check` pre-flight before a cycle is spent on it; the log is the ground truth for what the SMC did, never the operator's or the assistant's time estimates.

## 2026-11-16 (afternoon) — bisect run 1 (mask 0xE): CE is the wall, same loop

frontier v3.7 / smcload11 with mask 0xE (SDMA0+SDMA1+CP_CE): status hit 0x6 immediately and never gained bit 3 in 120 s; PC pinned at the same 0x2df5c↔0x2df64 loop. So the stall is at CE itself — the first CP image — not further down the line. The SDMA blocks accept their ucode; the CP (inside the GFX block) never accepts its first. Note this also explains the stock driver's -22 from day one: smu7_check_fw_load_finish polls this same status with a timeout, so a stock full boot hits the identical wall (SDMA loads, CE never completes, timeout, -EINVAL). The driver was never broken; the CP block genuinely does not accept ucode on this enclosure in any environment tried. Next bisect: mask 0x406 (SDMA pair + RLC_G) — RLC is the other GFX sub-block and historically wedged at a different PC (0x33824); whether it completes or stalls at its own wait separates "CP-path wall" from "whole-GFX-block precondition missing" (power/clock/a POST this card may never have had).

smcload12 (frontier v3.9, mask 0xE) dumped CP state before and during the CE stall: GRBM_STATUS 0x3028 (CP_BUSY=0, GUI_ACTIVE=0; set bits are just FIFO-avail counts and DB/CB cache-clean flags), GRBM_STATUS2 0x8, CP_ME_CNTL 0x15000000 (CE_HALT=PFP_HALT=ME_HALT=1) — and none of it changed across the full 120 s stall. ## 2026-11-16 (afternoon) — THE WAIT LOOP DECODED (LM32 disassembly): the SMC writes command byte 0x40 to its own register 0x80000398 and polls for the hardware to clear it

SMU7's core is a LatticeMico32 (LM32) — documented, with a Ghidra processor module (jevinskie/ghidra-lm32) whose Sleigh spec gives the opcode map. The firmware image sits at file offset 256 (array_offset) and executes at SRAM 0x20000, so PC 0x2df5c = file 0xE05C (mapping proven: image head matches the dance's payload head byte for byte). Decoded with a hand-built LM32 disassembler (62 opcodes parsed from lm32.sinc; RI format dest=reg1, src=reg0; branches target = PC + 4*simm16):

The stall loop (0x2df5c): `lw r1, (r4+0)` / `andi r1, r1, 0xff` / `bne r1, r0, loop` — poll the low byte of a word until it is 0. Immediately before it (0x2df3c block, entered when state value r5 == 4): build 0x80000398 via mvhi/ori, read-modify-write that register clearing the low byte and setting it to **0x40**, store it back, then poll. Sibling state blocks do the identical dance with command bytes 0x30 (r5 == 2) and 0x20 (r5 == 5). The blocks are entries in a work-list walker (cursor r28, +8 per completed item, exit at 0x2e14c = advance + ret). Only this walker uses SMC register 0x80000398 in the whole firmware.

So: during a firmware load the SMC walks a work list; certain items order a hardware engine by writing a command byte to SMC register 0x80000398 and waiting for the hardware to clear the byte. The SDMA/RLC items' orders complete instantly; the CP-path item's order (byte 0x40) is NEVER cleared on this card. The SMC is not waiting on the CP's MMIO state, the driver, or the TOC — it is waiting on a hardware completion that never arrives for the graphics-core load step. Note the SMC register space (0x80000370 = PC, 0x80000398) is the SAME space our host tools already reach via SMC_IND_INDEX_11/DATA_11 (BAR5 bytes 0x6B0/0x6B4) — so 0x80000398 is host-readable: during a stall it should show the stuck 0x40; on the healthy internal GPU it should read 0. Cheap confirmation on any future run. Bonus: the OLD wedge PC 0x33824 decodes as `bi 0` (op 0x38, Rel26 = 0) — a deliberate branch-to-self panic trap, a different failure from the live poll.

## 2026-11-16 (afternoon) — digging further, at the author's direction: the 0x398 neighbourhood is the SMC's CPU block; the decisive test is a completion ASSIST (smcload13 / frontier v4.1)

More disassembly: the only other builder of a 0x800003xx address is a context save/restore pair at 0x202c0/0x20318, which stores the task frame pointer to 0x80000394 — so 0x800003xx is the SMC CPU's own register block (PC 0x370, context pointer 0x394), and 0x80000398 is an event/completion flag register in that block: the load task sets a command byte and spins until the byte clears (cleared by hardware/interrupt when the transfer into the target block completes). The walker's search loop also consults a dword table at SMC 0x80000338. The LM32 disassembler is saved at files/lm32/lm32dis.py (+ lm32.sinc) for future firmware work.

THE ASSIST EXPERIMENT (built, awaiting a run): two hypotheses remain — (H1) the transfer into the CP actually happens but the completion signal never reaches the SMC in this enclosure; (H2) the transfer never happens. smcload13 = smcload12 + an assist: while polling, it watches SMC reg 0x80000398; if a command byte sits unchanged for 5 s, the host clears the byte (max 8 assists, each logged), and it dumps SMC regs 0x80000380–0x800003B0 once. frontier v4.1 runs it at mask 0xE (minimal repro). Outcomes: status gains the CE bit / load completes after an assist → H1, the signal path is the whole problem and an assisted full load becomes a real (if inelegant) route; SMC advances but the CE bit never sets → H2, the transfer itself is dead and the wall is purely the GFX domain's power/clock state.

## 2026-11-16 (afternoon) — assist run 2 (frontier v4.2): H2 CONFIRMED — the transfer never happens; the SMC re-issues the order and keeps verifying

smcload14's assist fired 8 times. Every time the host cleared the byte in SMC reg 0x80000398, the SMC immediately re-issued command 0x40 and resumed polling; UcodeLoadStatus never moved off 0x6 in 120 s; final PC still in the loop, ping unanswered. ## 2026-11-16 (afternoon) — the author's idea, adopted: the WARM HANDOFF — harvest and load against the macOS-initialized SMC state (frontier v4.4)

The author's reasoning: pull the live boot state from macOS, where the card is fully functional. macOS userspace gives no raw SMC access (SIP/kext walls), but the state itself can be carried across: boot macOS with the eGPU attached and working, then Restart (no unplugging, no virgin cycle) into Kubuntu -42. The enclosure's standby power keeps the SMC running across a warm reboot; the -42 poststop module guarantees the Linux driver never touches the SMC, so the card reaches the TTY exactly as macOS left it. frontier v4.4 replaces the dance with a warm check (smustat must show SMU_STATUS 0x3) and then runs smcload15 unchanged — which first harvests the macOS-initialized SRAM header + SMC register block (directly comparable to a virgin-state harvest), then attempts the load against the warmed SMC. If the missing ingredient is the SMC's boot-time platform state, the load completes here; and if it does, the sequel experiment is a warm boot on the STOCK -43 kernel, letting the real driver make its own attempt. The virgin-state harvest (v4.3) remains available afterward for the diff.

## 2026-11-16 (afternoon) — virgin-state harvest (frontier v4.3, frontier-7.log): the SMC's own house is in order

SRAMHDR (live, post-dance): the SMU74 firmware header is fully populated — table pointers at 0x20020+ (0x20100, 0x1d80c, image size 0x1fba8, 0x3d7dc, soft_regs 0x3fa28, 0x3f294, 0x3fa04...) all present and plausible; the 0x200a8+ span is uniform aa55/aaaa filler. So after the dance the SMC boots with its tables formed — "SMC boots half-blind" is NOT supported at the SMC-memory level. SMCREG block is mostly CPU context: PRE shows idle-task PCs (0x205xx/0x23fxx); STALL shows the task stack parked inside the walker (0x2df60/64, 0x2df00, 0x2df34, 0x2dea0, 0x2def8), two context slots holding the pointer 0x80000398 itself (the register being waited on), and one slot at 0x80000308 holding 0x33804 — inside the panic-trap function. Conclusion: whatever macOS initialization adds does NOT live in the SMC's own memory — it must be ASIC-side state (GFX/clock/power registers) or extra host-to-SMC configuration — precisely what the warm handoff (v4.4) preserves. Baseline values are in frontier-7.log (workspace/user/files).

## 2026-11-16 (afternoon) — warm handoff run (frontier v4.4): the SMC instance does NOT survive a warm reboot; BAR0 was also cold

After macOS (card working) → Restart → Kubuntu -42: the warm check read SMU_STATUS 0x0, UcodeLoadStatus 0x0, ping unanswered — the SMC was not running any firmware. The decisive test needs no new script: frontier v4.3 (dance + smcload15 harvest) run after a macOS warm restart instead of a virgin cycle — if the ASIC state survived, the load completes; if it stalls identically, the warm route is closed on evidence and the remaining road is ASIC-side pre-programming from Linux (candidate registers from gfx_v8_0/vi clock-power programming, replayed by the host before the load).

## 2026-11-16 (late) — Apple init diff mined: the missing table is the CGTT clock-gating (MGCG) table amdgpu skips for Polaris

Desk phase complete (full report: hidden_files/apple-init-diff.md). Apple's HWLibs SMC load path is a 1:1 port of Linux polaris10_smumgr.c — same messages, order, waits (the RCU_UC_EVENTS accesses are waits, not writes); no ASIC registers are written anywhere in it, and no amdgpu-style golden table exists in any carved binary. The one real diff: HWLibs carries a 75-entry (reg, mask, value) MGCG clock-gating table for the Polaris10 backend (CGTT_CP_CLK_CTRL 0x3C2C0=0x100, CGTT_CPC_CLK_CTRL 0x3C2C8=0x100, CGTT_CPF_CLK_CTRL 0x3C2C4=0x40000100, RLC_CGTT_MGCG_OVERRIDE 0x3B120=0xFFFFFFFF, RLC_CGCG_CGLS_CTRL 0x3B124=0x0020003C) — and vi.c's vi_enable_mgcg() explicitly falls through for CHIP_POLARIS10/11/12, so amdgpu never writes these registers on this card. Next test: read those five registers on the cold eGPU vs the internal GPU after barfix2 (read-first A/B), then a frontier variant replaying the CP subset before the dance+load.

## 2026-11-16 (evening) — frontier v4.6: the MGCG clock-gating theory is DEAD — the eGPU already held Apple's values

The A/B read killed it before the writes even mattered: the cold, unPOSTed eGPU already carried the table values on all seven registers (CGTT_CP/CPF/CPC/RLC_CLK_CTRL all 0x100 — CPF at the table-C variant 0x100, matching the internal GPU exactly; RLC_CGCG_CGLS_CTRL 0x0001003F identical; RLC_CGTT_MGCG_OVERRIDE 0xFFFFFFFF, MORE permissive than the iGPU's 0xFFFFFFE0; CP_RB_WPTR_POLL_CNTL identical). The card programs these itself at power-on. The ranked writes were applied anyway (all readbacks OK), held through the dance, and the load stalled at 0x6 in the same 0x40 loop — zero change. Clock-gating branch CLOSED. ## 2026-11-16 (late) — phase 2 desk: start sequence is a faithful port (clean negative); bypass tracks mapped

Full report: hidden_files/apple-start-sequence.md. (1) The macOS start path is fully traced (controller → X4000 setupCail/initCail → HWLibs CAIL initializeAdapter: EarlyASICInit → PostVBIOS → Initialize → queries → ReserveMCAddressRange → F2 = FixChipsetBugs → NoBiosInitializeAdapter → ASICSetup) and contains NO Apple-only step before the SMC load: no extra SMC messages, no SMC-indirect writes outside the five SMU clusters, no PCIe link programming, zero Thunderbolt/enclosure awareness in any binary.
(2) One unproven thread: CAIL has complete soft-reset machinery (mask translator 0x71f5cc, GRBM_SOFT_RESET dword-0x2008 pulse at 0x71f711+, composite 0x71ef11) whose only caller chain runs through init-shaped function 0x712fc8 (also calls PostVBIOS + NoBios + ASICSetup steps) — start vs recovery role unprovable from the carve; amdgpu never pulses soft reset at probe, so it becomes experiment B1, not a conclusion.
(3) LM32 walker fully decoded (both images, Apple's identical at −0xcb0 incl. stall PCs 0x2d2ac/b4): five loader tasks share routine 0x2de78; states 2/4/5 issue bytes 0x30/0x40/0x20 to SMC reg 0x80000398 and poll for hardware clear with NO verification; tasks 1/3 use a CSR channel class (bytes 1–6, channel bases from the 0x80000338 table into CSRs 16–19). CE → state 4 proven; UcodeLoadStatus is never written by this routine — completion bits come from a hardware path, which is exactly why the v4.2 host assist could never work.
(4) Bypass verdicts: poll-skip/status-fake patches are cosmetic (CP RAM contents are the real missing thing; direct CP ports already proven dead). The one patch with a physical path to a working card is re-routing the CP command byte 0x40 → 0x30 (one byte at Linux SRAM 0x2df54), gated by a one-byte patch-viability dance (P0: does the upload path verify the image?). Decisive readout = driver bind + CP ring test, not status bits.
(5) Apple's TOC recovered exactly (populate order RLC, CE, PFP, ME, MEC, JT1, JT2, SDMA0, SDMA1, SMU_SK; mask 0x447E when id 14 present; MEC_STORAGE id 35 added after DRV_HI, no mask bit) — replay assessed LOW prior (SK walks after CE and is refuted as a precondition by the internal GPU; MEC_STORAGE is never walked), worth one free frontier run. Ranked experiments: P0+P2 → B1 soft-reset pulse → TOC replay → B2 partial-mask display-only bind as the bankable fallback win.

The one-byte-patched image (0x40→0x30 at file 0xE054) uploaded byte-clean, but SMU_STATUS came back 0x1 — DONE=yes, PASS=0 — versus 0x3 for every genuine image. The patched image never ran; everything after the dance in frontier-11.log is garbage state (soft_regs read 0xaaaa55c1 = filler pattern) and proves nothing about the 0x30 theory itself — the re-route remains UNTESTED, gated behind defeating the self-check. ## 2026-11-16 (late) — SMC self-check verdict: ROM-side keyed digest — the image-patch road is closed

Frontier v4.7's one-byte-patched image (0x40→0x30) uploaded byte-clean but SMU_STATUS came back 0x1 (DONE, PASS=0) and the PC never left ROM space (0x2950): the verdict is rendered by the SMU's mask-ROM bootloader before any image code runs — the image's own boot path (0x20100 → BSS clear → main) contains no verification loop, and neither genuine image contains any hash/CRC constants. The stored expected value is Digest[5] at image offset 0 (SMU7_Firmware_Header, smu7.h); the header's trailing "Signature" dword is the constant magic 0xaa55aa55 in all 13 genuine images checked. Exhaustive recomputation test — SHA-1/224/256/512, MD5, RIPEMD-160 × 15+ ranges × byte-order transforms, over 13 genuine images (Linux + Apple Polaris10 + 11 siblings extracted from HWLibs) — produced zero matches: Digest[5] is a keyed/authenticated digest (consistent with SMU_FIRMWARE protected=1, hard-key=1), not recomputable without the fused key. No fix-up exists; patch track P2 is closed. Full report: hidden_files/smc-selfcheck.md.

## 2026-11-16 (night) — egpushim v0.4 FIRST HARDWARE RUN: writer died at the root port; EFI test VOID, cause identified

Shim log (egpushim-20261117-021444.log, 387 lines) ends MID-WRITE: WSAVE captured all 25 originals; WWRITE OK through entry 16 (bridges 0A:01.0, 09:00.0, 06:01.0, 05:00.0) and stops exactly before the ROOT PORT (00:1C.4) writes. No WRESTORE, no WRITER_RESULT, no ACPI/CHAINLOAD sections — the app never finished the phase. The author subsequently reached Kubuntu on a virgin state (dmesg: all BARs zero at enumeration, BAR0/BAR2 "can't assign; no space" without hpmemsize; kernel renumbered the TB subtree to buses 42/43/44 — the bus-44 mystery, benign) and frontier v5.0 ran on barfix2's map: Apple dance PASS=1, smcload15 0x5FE, status 0x6 at t+0.5s, identical 0x40 stall. **The EFI-stage variable never entered the boot — the run is void, not a null result.** Leading mechanism: the root port's prefetch-window rewrite transiently breaks decode for the ENTIRE TB subtree; if the shim's boot volume sits downstream of 00:1C.4 (SD card in a USB-C/TB reader or hub), the writer loses its own log volume mid-phase and hangs at the next flush. On 2026-11-08 the v0.3 writer wrote the same root-port registers and APPLIED entries=20 with the log surviving through chainload — so that night the volume was NOT behind the root port (built-in SD slot or rear USB). Fix is physical, not code: boot the shim from the built-in SD slot (or rear USB-A), never a TB-tree reader; protocol otherwise unchanged. CONFIRMED by the author minutes later: tonight the card was in a USB-C adaptor (downstream of the TB root port — mechanism confirmed); on Nov 8 it was directly in the iMac's built-in SD slot. Standing rule for all shim runs: **the boot card lives in the built-in SD slot (or rear USB-A), never in a reader behind the Thunderbolt tree the writer rewrites.** Refinement (the author, same night): GRUB appeared ON ITS OWN — so the writer most likely COMPLETED all 25 writes in memory (log_flush ignores Write errors; the in-memory log kept going, the file froze at entry 16 when the adaptor's volume dropped behind the scrambled root-port window) and chainloaded normally — but the adaptor dropping off the TB tree plausibly triggered a subtree re-enumeration/bus reset that wiped the card's config before Linux enumerated it (hence virgin BARs + kernel bus renumbering). Either way: round 2 with the card in the built-in slot removes the whole failure class, and the log surviving past entry 16 is the first checkpoint.

## 2026-11-16 (night) — JOURNAL VERDICT: the eGPU is not in the kernel's initial scan at all; EFI road CLOSED on evidence

Monotonic journal from the v0.4.2 boot: twin enumerated 7.57 s; thunderbolt driver binds 7.63 s ("device links to tunneled native ports are missing!") and discovers the enclosure as a TB device ("Blackmagic Design eGPU RX580") at 7.64 s; **the eGPU's PCI function appears only at 29.51 s** (0000:44:00.0) — 22 s later, kernel-numbered, virgin. The card is ABSENT from the initial PCI scan: the PCIe tunnel is down at handover, the enclosure power-cycles the card (the fans at "loading initial ramdisk"), and Linux meets a cold, freshly-plugged-equivalent device via TB hotplug enumeration. **Consequences:** (1) No EFI-stage state on the card can survive — it physically reboots before Linux enumerates it; the EFI-placement road is closed completely, on evidence. (2) Every frontier run ever done started from exactly this cold state; the SMC 0x40 wall lives entirely in the cold-card Linux world. (3) The twin-vs-eGPU delta under Linux is precisely warm-EFI-POSTed vs power-cycled-cold — but amdgpu's own driver POST (which the poststop guard sits after) does not lift the wall, so plain POST is not the missing precondition either. **The one remaining warm-card road**: an ICM-claim shim — speak the NHI/ICM mailbox protocol from EFI and claim the controller before the handover so the tunnel never drops and the card reaches the kernel's initial scan warm at 0B (the "device links missing" line marks the adoption metadata Linux lacks; with a live tunnel at scan time the late 29.5 s dance may never touch the GPU). Big build, uncertain prize — the author's call. All other roads (firmware builds, TOC, clocks, soft reset, EFI placement, EFI drivers, CP ports, SMC patch) are closed on evidence as of tonight.

## 2026-11-17 (morning) — egpushim v0.5.0-icmclaim BUILT: the DRIVER_READY claim, ported from the kernel

The author chose to push ("lets push"). Desk research (torvalds/linux sources read in full): the ICM claim is ONE control-channel exchange — icm_ar_driver_ready sends a header-only ICM packet (code 0x3) over NHI ring 0 and matches an ICM_RESP with the same code (icm_match). Wire framing (ctl.c tb_ctl_tx): packet dwords big-endian + CRC-32C trailer (~crc32c(~0,...), Castagnoli). Ring mechanics (nhi.c tb_ring_start/stop): desc/options register layout, ENABLE|RAW = 0xC0000000, RX sof/eof mask 0xFFFFFFFF, frame size 256, doorbells at desc+8 (TX prod<<16, RX cons). icm_firmware_init mirrors: mode from OUTMAIL opmode; CM mode → mailbox ALLOW_ALL_DEVS (0x23) first; ICM not running (FW_STS ICM_EN) or SAFE mode → driver gives up. NHI at EFI stage: 07:00.0, CMD=0006, BAR0 0x81A00000 (from the Nov 8 log) — MMIO-ready. **v0.5.0-icmclaim built** (BOOTX64.EFI 123,717 B, sha256 0150341738ae6dc47755d7656d897a9717a9f3cc8be42074cafa3ab401c25255): pure wire core icm_pure.c host-tested (CRC reproduces the universal CRC-32C check value 0xE3069283 for "123456789"; frame/parse/descriptor asserts pass; plan suite still green); do_icm() does recon always (CAPS/FW_STS/mode/ring-0 state) and the claim only under ICM_CLAIM=1, with EFI ring-0 registers saved/restored, tb_ring_stop teardown, DMA pages freed, all waits bounded, every failure falling through to the writer. Config-space write audit intact: exactly one Pci.Write call site. TB probe now gated behind TB_PROBE=1 (default off). **QEMU/OVMF smoke PASSED** (unarmed cfg): booted, logged, ICM phase skipped cleanly (no NHI in VM), new cfg keys parsed. Kit: files/Shim_v050_card.zip (BOOTX64.EFI + armed cfg with ICM_CLAIM=1), extraction-verified. Awaiting the hardware run: readings = ICMCLAIM RESULT line, fans at the ramdisk line, and the journal — card in the initial scan at 0b with placed BARs = the handover is won. Side note from the author (Nov 17): Kubuntu's Thunderbolt devices panel shows the feature toggled off at boot — boltd/KCM state; irrelevant to this work (frontier tools talk straight to the PCI device; the ICM does authorization on this firmware-managed controller; enumeration already proven with it off), and the EFI claim is upstream of Linux userspace entirely.

## 2026-11-17 (morning) — v0.5.0 RUN: there is NO ICM on this machine — the claim road is void, and the recon rewrote the handover story

Log egpushim-20261117-144600.log. Writer APPLIED 25, RECHECK intact (as always). The ICM recon is the story: NHI at 07:00.0, BAR0 0x81A00000, COMMAND 0x0006, CAPS hops=12. **FW_STS = 0x000001A0 — ICM_EN = 0, OUTMAIL opmode = 0 (SAFE): the ICM firmware is NOT RUNNING at EFI stage.** The claim correctly SKIPPED (nothing to claim; DRIVER_READY would have had no firmware to answer). Instead, **ring 0 is live with APPLE's own control channel**: TX descbase 0x78D5C800, count 8, opts ENABLE|RAW, doorbell 0x00030003 (3 frames flowed); RX descbase 0x78D56800, count (256<<16)|8, mask 0xFFFFFFFF, doorbell 0x00060004. **Conclusion: Apple's EFI Thunderbolt stack is a NATIVE connection manager — it programs the host router's config space directly over ring 0 and never starts Intel's ICM.** The tunnels that exist in EFI are Apple's own programming; the ExitBootServices drop is the disappearance of that boot-services driver and its state, and no firmware manager persists to honour a claim. The ICM road is closed on evidence — and the discovery also corrects the frame for the Linux side: with no ICM running anywhere, Linux's 29.5 s enumeration is its native/ICM-start path rebuilding the PCIe path from nothing, and the enclosure power-cycles the card in the gap. Every EFI-stage road is now closed (placement, disconnects, claim). The warm-card quest's only remaining lever would be preventing Apple's firmware from dropping its own tunnels at the handover, which lives inside Apple's ExitBootServices handling — outside this project's reach. The cold-card SMC wall (the original problem) stands exactly where the frontier left it; the untested desk lever remains the ASIC-state diff (cold-Linux vs warm-macOS register harvest around SMU/CP).

Reads chunked at 32 dwords, INVALID_CONFIG_SPACE ends a space cleanly, 2 attempts × 2000 ms per chunk, stray/bad-CRC frames counted. All output buffered in memory during the window; emitted to the log after teardown/TPL restore, plus LOGS\SNAPSHOT.BIN (EGPUSNAP stream: per-block route/port/space/count/status + dwords). Config READ packets only — no config-write builder exists in the build; single-Pci.Write audit intact (pci_write called only from pci_write_entry ← do_write). Plan + ICM host suites still pass; only warning is the pre-existing benign scan_bus one. QEMU/OVMF smoke PASSED (unarmed + SNAPSHOT=1: version/cfg parsed, phase skipped cleanly with no NHI, full run completed). Kit files/Shim_v060_card.zip extraction-verified (EFI hash matches; cfg PAUSE=1, WRITE_ENABLE=1, ICM_CLAIM=1, SNAPSHOT=1). Awaiting the hardware run + upload of the log AND SNAPSHOT.BIN; then the desk work: judge the captured PCIe path programming for completeness against the tb_regs.h layout before designing the ExitBootServices-hook replay.

## egpushim v0.6.0 HARDWARE RUN + v0.6.1 doorbell fix (2026-11-17)
v0.6.0 ran on hardware (log egpushim-20261117-151806.log): the capture FAILED at the first read - "SNAP-END route=0 port=0 space=SWITCH got=0 status=TIMEOUT tx_completed=0", exchanges=2, strays=0, bad_crc=0, SNAPSHOT.BIN 52 bytes (header + one empty block). tx_completed=0 is the decisive datum: the NHI never consumed the TX descriptor - the frame never left, so this was ring mechanics, not protocol. Desk diagnosis against fresh torvalds/linux nhi.c/nhi_regs.h/linux-thunderbolt.h: descriptor format, RING_DESC flags (POSTED 0x4 / INTERRUPT 0x8 / COMPLETED 0x2), register bases, tb_ring_start write order, CRC/framing all check out - EXCEPT the doorbell. Apple's ring-0 TX doorbell read 0x00020002 at recon: the +8 register's low half is the NHI's TAIL index, and it does NOT reset when the descriptor base is reprogrammed. The shim rang prod=1 against a hardware tail of 2, so the NHI saw nothing new. The kernel never hits this because tb_ring_stop zeroes +8, leaving tail=0 for the next start. v0.6.1 (BOOTX64.EFI 140,610 B, sha256 5c6373ad5f130ad1f715a7d99c2795ce82cf7d3228b648a45e9f58f3fc084686): both ring-programming sites (snapshot + the latent same flaw in do_icm) now zero +8 right after the descriptor-base write; the snapshot phase also logs a full ring readback after programming and the TX doorbell on any timeout. Host suites all pass; single Pci.Write audit intact. QEMU smoke SKIPPED for v0.6.1 (the build VM was redeployed mid-morning and its apt replay stalled; gnu-efi 3.0.15 was source-built under ~/workspace/toolchain to route around it - the smoke only exercises the skip path, unchanged from v0.6.0). Kit: files/Shim_v061_card.zip, extraction-verified, cfg identical to v0.6.0 (only BOOTX64.EFI changes on the card).

## egpushim v0.6.1 HARDWARE RUN + v0.6.2 disable-first fix (2026-11-17)
v0.6.1 ran (log egpushim-20261117-153740.log): the doorbell zeroing LANDED (programmed readback TX doorbell=0x00000000, RX=0x10) but the capture still timed out with tx_completed=0 - and the timeout footer showed tx_doorbell=0x00020000: prod advanced 0->2 across the two attempts while the hardware tail stayed 0 and the descriptor was never read. Verdict: the NHI was still working from Apple's descriptor array - a new descriptor base written into a LIVE (enabled) ring is ignored; the kernel's flow always starts from a stopped ring (tb_ring_stop leaves options=0). v0.6.2 (BOOTX64.EFI 141,122 B, sha256 f90544d7d3f8f74c5b91cd83f1c5e74b9a1e0bb77c1f60887300c367762b2272): both ring-programming sites write options=0 + Stall(2000us) BEFORE the descriptor-base write; the snapshot readback now covers bases, doorbells, counts and options for both rings. Host suites pass; kit files/Shim_v062_card.zip extraction-verified, cfg unchanged. QEMU smoke skipped again (toolchain still source-built; smoke covers only the unchanged skip path).

## egpushim v0.6.2 HARDWARE RUN + v0.7.0 borrow design (2026-11-17)
v0.6.2 ran (log egpushim-20261117-155500.log): full readbacks PERFECT (TX base=0x7A4E6000 count=0x10 opts=0xC0000000/0, RX likewise, doorbells as programmed) and STILL tx_completed=0, tail frozen at 0. Three own-ring builds have now failed identically with kernel-exact programming - the register theory space is exhausted. Leading suspect: DMA reachability of the shim's own pages (IOMMU/VT-d domain mapping only Apple's buffers; this is a T2 Mac with kernel DMA protection), unprovable from the shim side. v0.7.0-borrow (BOOTX64.EFI 140,071 B, sha256 efd40572b1d16723715819fbe29aa76c1579ad94ae017b5dec5212019d66ec0f) removes the variable: the snapshot posts each config READ into Apple's OWN idle ring 0 (frame written into the slot's existing Apple buffer, descriptor phys untouched, only the TX doorbell written) and reads replies from Apple's RX ring by watching for new completions; no ring register is reprogrammed, nothing to restore, RX doorbell/consumer accounting untouched. Gates: ring geometry plausible + TX prod==tail (idle), else SKIPPED with reason. Apple's full TX/RX descriptor tables are logged for the desk. Host suites pass; kit files/Shim_v070_card.zip extraction-verified, cfg unchanged. Decisive either way: if the borrowed post completes, the channel works and the capture proceeds; if even Apple's own ring won't carry our post, the blocker is the posting act itself under raised TPL, not memory or programming.

## egpushim v0.7.0 HARDWARE RUN - BORROWED CHANNEL WORKS; v0.7.1 kernel-sized reads (2026-11-17)
v0.7.0 ran (log egpushim-20261117-160754.log): borrow gates passed (TX prod=3 tail=3 idle, RX prod=6 cons=4, Apple's descriptor tables logged) and the FIRST config exchange in project history completed end-to-end - exchanges=1, strays=0, bad_crc=0. The host switch ANSWERED, refusing the 32-dword SWITCH-space read with INVALID_CONFIG_SPACE (SNAP-END status=END_OF_SPACE). The channel, framing, CRC and borrow mechanics are all proven; the refusal is semantic: the kernel reads the switch header 5 dwords at a time (switch.c tb_cfg_read route,0,TB_CFG_SWITCH,0,5), port headers 8, hop entries 2 - the responder rejects oversized requests. (Also corrected on the record this morning, at the author's catch: the iMac19,1 has NO T2 chip - that arrived with the 2020 iMac20,x. The DMA-reachability suspicion for the own-ring failures stands only as an unproven possibility, without the T2 motivation.) v0.7.1 (140,071 B, sha256 e5bd387a81b014b8a7913dd662f2db11c1cd4a7e1128a9cd1484ad1d3df0d4bd): snap_read_space chunks at kernel sizes (8 SWITCH/PORT, 2 HOPS); kit files/Shim_v071_card.zip extraction-verified, cfg unchanged.

## egpushim v0.7.1 HARDWARE RUN - FIRST CAPTURE; v0.7.2 drain fix (2026-11-17)
v0.7.1 ran (log egpushim-20261117-162053.log): SNAPSHOT RESULT: CAPTURED - the host router's SWITCH space read in full (24 dwords until END_OF_SPACE): vendor/device 8086:15EA, rev 6, max_port 13, upstream_port 7, depth 0, ENABLED=1 (dword3 0x80000000). Apple's descriptor tables logged (TX dw2 pattern 0x00611010 = completed config reads from Apple's own boot traffic). The walk then stalled permanently: every PORT read timed out (tx_doorbell prod climbing 0x0C->0x26, tail frozen) - because the shim inspected replies without CONSUMING them: Apple's RX ring (8 slots, 2 already pending) filled with the walk's replies and the channel wedged. v0.7.2 (this build): the exchange drains FIFO from Apple's consumer index, advancing the RX doorbell cons after every inspected frame (Apple's 2 pending frames count as strays; harmless - Apple's EFI driver never resumes in this boot), uses a global seq counter, and the router walk reuses the first read's header instead of re-reading. The PCIe-path judgment (PORT/HOPS of the host TB port, the PCIe adapters, the enclosure router) is still outstanding - it is exactly what the stalled walk never reached.

## egpushim v0.7.2 HARDWARE RUN - scan deadlock; v0.7.3 fill-index scan + cons jump (2026-11-17)
v0.7.2 ran (log egpushim-20261117-163509.log): FAILED at the first read, but informatively - SNAP-END shows tx_completed=1, tx_doorbell=0x00050005 (both posts consumed, tails following), zero frames inspected. The v0.7.2 scan started at Apple's consumer index (slot 4, an old reposted buffer that will never complete again) and FIFO-waited there forever while the replies landed at the hardware fill index (RX doorbell high half, slot 6). v0.7.3 (140,089 B class, kit files/Shim_v073_card.zip): scan starts at the fill index again (v0.7.0/0.7.1 behaviour); the per-frame cons write jumps the doorbell past the inspected frame, freeing all slots up to it - the drain v0.7.1 lacked, without the FIFO trap. Timeout footers now log both doorbells.

## egpushim v0.7.3 HARDWARE RUN (USB stick debut) + v0.7.4 probe-all (2026-11-17)
v0.7.3 ran off the new USB-A stick (log egpushim-20261117-165353.log) - the stick workflow worked end to end (boot, chainload, log read in Kubuntu, no SD shuffle). Transport is now SOLID: 21 exchanges, ZERO timeouts, every request answered. The capture: host SWITCH space in full (24 dw, 8086:15EA, enabled, max_port 13, upstream 7); port 0 PORT space (8 dw, type PORT, max hops 7/7) - and port 0 is the switch itself per the kernel (tb_port_init skips its header read: "Port 0 is the switch itself and has no PHY"). Every real port 1..13 refused its PORT space with TB_CFG_ERROR_INVALID_CONFIG_SPACE, which ctl.c's tb_cfg_get_error() documents verbatim as the "port not implemented" answer (-ENODEV). Port 0's HOPS space refused the same way. Desk verdict: in the EFI environment the host router's port config spaces are dark; requests are kernel-identical (verified against ctl.c/switch.c/path.c), so this is the hardware's answer, not a shim defect. v0.7.4-probeall (kit files/Shim_v074_card.zip, BOOTX64.EFI sha256 3b6bed936e23585f...) probes every host port 1..13 as a route to a downstream router and walks any that answer - the untested question is whether the config channel still forwards over the live link to the enclosure (Linux's journal places the enclosure at host port 3). If nothing answers downstream either, the EFI config universe is the host switch header alone and freeze-by-replay has no path state to work with.

## egpushim v0.7.4 HARDWARE RUN - phantom ports; EFI ROAD CLOSED at the config layer (2026-11-17)
v0.7.4 ran (log egpushim-20261117-170408.log; SNAPSHOT-6.BIN 960 B). Transport held (45 exchanges, no timeouts) but the RX ring flooded: strays=224, bad_crc=7 (vs 47/0 in v0.7.3) - a stream of event/stale frames sloshing in the borrowed ring. The headline pattern is a mirage and the data proves it: ports 0,2,4,6,8,10,12 "answered" with BYTE-IDENTICAL headers whose dword3 port_number field reads 0 in every one - they are recycled copies of port 0's reply matching later requests because (kernel parity) the reply matcher does not compare the echoed port field and seq is only 2 bits. Odd ports and all HOPS spaces refused INVALID_CONFIG_SPACE as before, and all 13 route probes (incl. port 3, where Linux's journal places the enclosure) were refused with the same error by the host switch itself. Verdict: in the EFI environment the ONLY genuine config-space content is the host switch header (24 dw, stable across four runs); the tunnel's path state (hop tables, PCIe adapters) is not held in host-readable config space at all - it lives inside the DSL6540's internal firmware while Apple's native CM supervises. Freeze-by-snapshot/replay therefore has nothing to snapshot. With this, every EFI-stage road is closed on evidence: placement (handover reset), driver disconnect (no effect), ICM claim (no ICM in Apple's EFI), config snapshot (state not host-readable). The SMC 0x40 wall remains a cold-card Linux-world problem, exactly where the frontier left it. A strict-matching reader (full echoed-address compare + pre-purge) could confirm the darkness cleanly but cannot create state that is not there.

## EFI ROAD CLOSED BY THE AUTHOR'S DECISION (2026-11-17)
After the v0.7.4 verdict, the author chose "Close the EFI road - back to the cold-card problem" (options widget, 2026-11-17 11:11). The egpushim line (v0.1-v0.7.4) is retired: placement, TB-driver disconnect, ICM claim and config snapshot/freeze are all closed on evidence (see the entries above). Do not re-pitch EFI-stage work. The problem is the cold card under Linux: SMC loads SDMA0/1 + RLC_G instantly and waits forever on the CP-path transfer (command byte 0x40 at SMC reg 0x80000398, never cleared by hardware). Remaining untested lever: a wide ASIC register diff, cold eGPU vs the warm internal twin (1002:67DF at 01:00.0), same stock boot, read-only - the comparison the hunt never ran wide.
