## 2026-11-17 (midday) — IM191.fd carved: Apple's AMD EFI drivers FOUND; Ellesmere driver identified
- Flash layout (descriptor FLREGs): DESC 0x0-0xFFF, PDR 0x1000-0x1FFF, ME 0x2000-0x219FFF, BIOS 0x21A000-0xFFFFFF. FVs: NVRAM ($VSS store, FS GUID fff12b8d...) @0x21C000; main FV @0x24C000 (two FV_IMAGE files = nested compressed FVs); DXE FV @0xA70000 (DXE_CORE + FV_IMAGE 1.34 MB holding the DXE driver set); PEI FVs @0xCC0000 + @0xDE0000 (duplicated); boot FVs @0xF00000/@0xF80000. Full recursive carve (compression + LZMA GUID-defined sections handled): 460 files, 171 with UI names. Inventories: hidden_files/fw/im191-inventory2.txt.
- Named graphics drivers are all Intel/Apple side: CoffeelakeGraphics (iGPU GOP), MuxGraphicsSwitch (GMUX), AppleGraphicsConsole, EDIDParserDriver. NO AMD-named driver.
- **The AMD drivers are the four UNNAMED ~189-191 KB DRIVER files** in the DXE FV: GUIDs f0140fc0-ec2a-451e-adc5-d671ac453a8c, 963307c0-554f-471b-a120-9e21a73f5f5d, 1751907b-a9bb-429f-aeea-732989091141, f3834774-48e5-4583-b1a1-48c3e8634f79. PEs extracted to hidden_files/fw/gop-<guid8>.bin. Debug-path strings prove provenance: `C:\EFI\EFI_Sample\EDK\Drivers\ati\radeon\...` (radeoncomponentname.c, eg2\produced\{framebuffer,graphicsconnector,backlight}.c; two include northernislands\nibootcampsupport.c) — AMD's reference Radeon EFI driver, Apple-bundled per GPU family.
- **Ellesmere driver = gop-f0140fc0.bin**: it carries the 0x67DF device word 11 times (a real supported-device table); the other three carry it once each.
- NEXT: disassemble gop-f0140fc0's binding-start/POST path; catalogue its GPU register writes (via PciIo MMIO) and any AtomBIOS table execution; diff against amdgpu init + the frontier's replicated set, hunting for any write in the SMU/CP neighbourhood Linux never makes.
- Correction/refinement (same session): the 0x67DF occurrences shared by all four drivers (context `faec ee63 ... bd46 67df 3d7d`) are inside a signature/hash blob — coincidence, NOT device tables. The real Ellesmere evidence is unique to gop-f0140fc0: six code sites comparing the device ID against 0x67DF paired with constants 0x02C400 / 0x02C500 (the C4/C5 Ellesmere silicon revisions — cf. internal 570X rev C5 in the macOS capture), plus four "Adapter" records carrying 0x67DF in a data table. The arcticislands pair (963307c0/f3834774, 'vega' strings) contain NO Ellesmere code beyond the shared signature blob. f0140fc0 stands as the EFI driver that POSTs Ellesmere.

## 2026-11-17 (midday) — EFI FIRMWARE SNIFF VERDICT: the Radeon EFI drivers are display-only; no EFI-stage ASIC init exists
- Disassembly prep on gop-f0140fc0 (the Ellesmere driver): PE sections .text 0x27756 B; source inventory from debug paths = ONLY eg2/produced/{backlight,framebuffer,graphicsconnector,graphicsdevice}.c + evergreen/evergreenapple.c + northernislands/nibootcampsupport.c + southernislands/siuga.c + radeoncomponentname.c. Checked all four Radeon drivers the same way (the arcticislands pair carry v10apple.c/v10uga.c — Vega 10 glue): **every one is display-only. ZERO AtomBIOS/ASIC/SMU/VBIOS strings in any of the four; no AtomBIOS interpreter, no ASIC-init module, no firmware loading.** Immediate scan of f0140fc0: GRBM_STATUS (byte and dword forms) 0 occurrences, CP_ME_CNTL 0; SMC_IND window constants appear once each (noise-level, no SMC conversation).
- Interpretation: Apple's EFI Radeon drivers only mode-set a card that is already alive. The internal card's EFI-stage life comes from its legacy VBIOS executed through Apple's CSM path (Boot Camp support is compiled in) plus this display driver for the boot UI. **The eGPU receives no EFI-stage initialization at all** — exactly what the shim measured from the other side (COMMAND=0x0000, BARs unassigned, never POSTed). On a macOS boot it needs none: the OS-side kexts (proven 1:1 amdgpu port) init it after boot, on a card that never power-cycled because Apple's boot path never dropped the tunnel.
- CONSEQUENCE: **the EFI road is now closed from BOTH ends.** The shim arc proved no EFI-stage state survives the handover; the firmware sniff proves there was never any eGPU EFI-stage init to preserve. There is nothing in the Mac's EFI firmware that sets up the SMC's 0x40 transfer — the byte's precondition is not an EFI artifact. The warm/cold delta stands as the whole remaining story: macOS path = card POSTed and never reset; Linux path = card power-cycled at handover, met virgin.
- The fork returns to the author's Nov 17 open choice: (a) kexec two-stage boot — the only way to manufacture a never-reset card under Linux; if the second kernel's first scan sees the eGPU, the frontier runs on a warm card for the first time; (b) the wide ASIC register diff (asicdiff.py) — cold eGPU vs warm twin on one stock boot, read-only, naming whatever state "cold" costs the card.

## 2026-11-17 (midday) — kexec CONCEDED on the author's objection
- the author, on the kexec proposal: "it doesnt load the card into linux as it is so how does this make a difference." He is right, and the project's own evidence proves it. Kexec only skips the firmware/startup stage; the card still has to be initialized by Linux, and Linux's init is precisely what fails. Worse: the Nov 16 stock-driver probe measured what a first Linux boot leaves behind — SMC running Linux's firmware, UcodeLoadStatus 0x6, PC parked in the 0x40 poll, ping unanswered. A kexec second kernel would inherit exactly that stalled state and stall identically. The twin's advantage was never "warmth" in general: it is WHO woke it (its own VBIOS POST, executed by Apple's CSM path) plus never being reset afterward. Kexec gives the eGPU neither — just a second helping of the same init that already failed. KEXEC CLOSED by the user's correct objection; do not re-pitch.
- Standing next step: the wide ASIC register diff (asicdiff.py) — unaffected by the objection because it changes nothing on the card; it reads the genuinely-different twin against the cold eGPU and lists the differences. Heavier third road noted for completeness: execute the eGPU's legacy VBIOS POST from Linux userspace via x86 emulation (vbetool-style) before the frontier load — the only route that would literally copy the twin's history; untested, historically flaky on secondary cards, a build project, NOT yet pitched beyond a mention.

## 2026-11-17 (midday) — asicdiff run 1 VOID (sleeping eGPU, untouched twin); tool v2 shipped
- Run 1 output (asicdiff.txt, uploaded 12:14): eGPU side read 0xFFFFFFFF on EVERY register — the documented void signature (Nov 9: "device asleep, decode off — void reads, not state"). BAR5 had an address (0x88b00000) but the unbound, failed-probe device had been runtime-suspended by the PCI core; nothing in the run sheet woke it. Twin side read real GFX values (GRBM_STATUS 0x3028, CP_ME_CNTL 0x15000000, clocks 0x100) but its SMC read VIRGIN (soft_regs garbage 0xb163a4d3, SMU_STATUS 0, UcodeLoadStatus 0, PC in ROM range 0x28xx–0x2cxx, FB_LOCATION 0) — the stock driver had not made its pass at either card when the reads happened (contrast the Nov 16 smustat twin baseline: 0x5FE, SMU_STATUS 3, roaming PC, taken in a settled desktop boot). 629 "stable differences," all artifacts of the two void/virgin conditions. Run 1 measures nothing about the wall; the tool itself worked (twin half is coherent).

## 2026-11-17 (afternoon) — POST discriminator RAN: case (a), stronger form — the card goes DARK mid-POST; atomrun vindicated
- postprobe.sh run (virgin cycle, stock -43, driver untouched): peek 0x12344 BEFORE = 0x000000f1 twice (healthy, bit 2 clear). atomrun --live: ABORTED at a stuck poll again (1,921,828 instructions, 237 MM writes, 0.3 ms total delays). The run's own writes drive the card's register block dark mid-startup, and the poll then waits forever on a block that no longer answers. Same end state as the Nov 9 dance-first abort (vramprobe3 read FF at every stage afterward) — two independent aborted POSTs, same dark aftermath. The userspace POST cannot survive being run partway on this card: it enters a state it only exits by completing, and it cannot complete because it polls the darkened block. The kernel's execution of the same tables completes, so an environmental difference remains in principle (access pattern / delay handling are the named suspects), but the payoff for chasing it is a load test whose kernel-POST equivalent already stalled at 0x40. Prior: low and falling. Remaining option if the author wants it: bisect the 237-write trace (~/egpu/postprobe-trace.txt on his machine) to find the darkening write. Otherwise the hunt has a measured end on every road: registers exhausted, SMC firmware exonerated, EFI closed from both ends, kexec closed on his objection, POST closed by this run.

That is the one physical place "alter the design" could attach — with the bricking risk stated plainly and the low prior noted (the kernel executes this ROM's startup tables to completion every boot and the load still stalls). Concrete macOS-dig step identified: diff Apple's published power/mapping data (PP_StaticMappingInfo1-6 + PowerPlay parameters in the Nov 16 ioreg capture) against the ROM's own PowerPlay tables — desk work on data already in hand.

## 2026-11-17 (afternoon) — Trace classification: the startup program powers the display block down, then polls it
- postprobe-trace.txt classified (237 writes). Groups: table 71 = 96 SMC-window writes (manager setup); table 2 = 35 PCIE-window + memory-controller training writes; table 13 = display power/clock setup incl. writes into the DCIO block; table 78 = PLL triples to six clock blocks + 32 alternating toggles of mmPHY_AUX_CNTL (dword 0x4897, values 0xffffffff/0xffffbfff — an AUX handshake loop); table 51 = DCIO control; tables 77->20 x6 = one W1C clear each (six transmitters); the run ends in table 20's display cluster: writes 218-235 (CRTC0/DCE registers), containing write #225 mmDMIF_PG0_DPG_PIPE_DPM_CONTROL <- 1 (byte 0x6cd0 — display power gating ARMED), then a fresh table-20 invocation writes #236/#237 mmCRTC_CONTROL (byte 0x6e70, dword 0x1B9C) = 0xffffffff then 0xfffffffe (CRTC master enable cleared — the display pipe switched OFF) — and the stuck poll on DPCSTX_TX_CLOCK_CNTL follows inside that same invocation. Register names verified against torvalds/linux dce_11_2_d.h.
- MECHANISM HYPOTHESIS (fits every observation): the startup sequence arms display power gating and switches the display pipe off, then asks the PHY whether its clock stopped — on this cold card the display register domain stops ANSWERING entirely (reads return all-ones) instead of reporting the bit, so the poll can never pass. Why the kernel's identical execution completes remains the open environmental difference. SCOPE is the key unknown the confirmation runs measure: display-domain-only darkness vs the whole register file dropping (postprobe peeked only 0x12344).
- Confirmation ladder built: atomrun_cap.py (= atomrun.py + --max-writes, desk-verified: caps land on exactly 217/235 writes) + postbisect.sh <cap> (one command per virgin boot; sentinel peeks before/after at 0x12344, GRBM_STATUS 0x8010, SMC_MESSAGE 0x250, CP_ME_CNTL 0x86d8). Run A cap 217 (before the display cluster), Run B cap 235 (after the cluster incl. the DPG write, before the CRTC pair). If B is already dark, the darkening is inside writes 218-235 (DPG write prime suspect); if B is light, the CRTC_CONTROL pair is the darkening step. NOTE for expectations: this mechanism explains the POST wedge only — the display domain is not in the SMC's CP load path, so even a pinned write does not by itself reopen the 0x40 wall; an aperture-wide result in the scope test would be the one outcome with wider implications.

## 2026-11-17 (afternoon) — macOS dig (the author's step 2): Apple's published mapping data holds no missing config
- Compared the PP_StaticMappingInfo1-6 properties Apple publishes per card in the Nov 16 ioreg capture: five of six are BYTE-IDENTICAL between the twin and the eGPU (Info1 c5ffffff, Info3 0, Info4 00002000, Info5 0, Info6 00202000). Only Info2 differs: twin 00101acd (LE 0xcd1a1000) vs eGPU 00e07fc9 (LE 0xc97fe000) — address-shaped, and POPULATED on the eGPU, not zero or missing. Searched both cards' ROM dumps for all four values (both Info2 values, Info1, Info6): ABSENT from both ROMs — the StaticMappingInfo values are runtime-computed by Apple's driver at enumeration (per-card addresses), not configuration stored on or read from the card. (A wider window-based property diff was attempted and discarded as unreliable — window extraction slides across registry node boundaries; the PP_ set above was extracted by direct line context and is trustworthy.)
- VERDICT: there is no macOS-side data table fed to this card that Linux lacks. Combined with the earlier digs (load path a 1:1 port, firmware exonerated, TOC identical), the macOS side has now been mined out: driver code, firmware, tables, and published mapping data all check out. The author's step 3 (alter the persistent design) therefore has no macOS-data lead to build on; the only persistent writable artifact remains the VBIOS ROM itself, with no measured evidence pointing at its tables as the gate.

## 2026-11-17 (afternoon) — Twin control: machine WEDGE; POST road closed. Campaign step 3 assessed: no alterable design target
- posttwin.sh RAN 14:03 (stock TTY boot, no virgin cycle): the same 217-write capped POST on the internal TWIN wedged the WHOLE MACHINE within seconds — fans to maximum, display dark, keyboard dead (Caps Lock LED unresponsive). Hard power-off at ~14:10; normal boot recovered the twin cleanly (EFI POSTs it every boot). posttwin.log and posttwin-trace.txt NEVER REACHED DISK (only posttwin.sh in ~/egpu) — the wedge froze the kernel before writeback, the same depth of freeze the dead Caps Lock indicated.
- Reading: userspace POST is destructive on BOTH cards, differently shaped (eGPU: card register file drops, machine fine, runs complete; twin: whole machine wedges). The clean "twin survives" comparison is unobtainable this way, and no further boots should be spent trying. The userspace-POST road is CLOSED on both cards. (The kernel's own execution of the same tables remains the only POST that completes — and frontier loads on kernel-POSTed cards stall identically, so a completed POST was never the missing ingredient anyway.)
- CAMPAIGN STEP 3 (the author's order: use the macOS load, alter the card's firmware/design, stop it sleeping) — assessed on the accumulated evidence:
  1. The SMC firmware CANNOT be altered: v4.7's one-byte patch uploaded byte-clean and the mask-ROM self-check rejected it; the header digest is a keyed AMD authentication checked against a hardware-resident key (13-image desk dig). No fix-up exists. Any plan ending in a patched manager firmware is impossible.
  2. A macOS load leaves NOTHING behind to alter from: v4.4 warm handoff measured the card virgin after restart (SMC SRAM is volatile); Apple's driver never writes the card's flash.
  3. The card's only persistent writable store is the VBIOS ROM chip. Its contents: startup tables (executed to completion by the kernel every boot; the load stalls anyway) and power tables (uploaded identically by both drivers). The SMC firmware is NOT in the ROM (it arrives as a driver file). NOTHING measured in nine days implicates the ROM as the gate; flashing a modified ROM risks bricking the card (recovery = hardware SPI programmer inside a sealed enclosure).
  4. "Stop it going to sleep": by measurement nothing sleeps during the stall — the SMC is awake, answers pings, completes SDMA/RLC transfers; host-side sleep is disabled (pcie_aspm=off, amdgpu.runpm=0). The handover darkness is the Thunderbolt tunnel dropping at ExitBootServices (Apple's EFI connection manager leaving), not a card sleep setting. There is no sleep knob, in the ROM or elsewhere.
- CAMPAIGN STATE after steps 1-3 (2026-11-17): every host-reachable layer is now measured and closed — EFI stage (no GPU init exists there; tunnel dies at handover), shim/ICM/snapshot roads, kexec, driver sequence (Apple's is a 1:1 port), SMC firmware (Apple's own build stalls identically; unforgeable), ~1,160 registers in virgin and post-attempt states (no writable missing precondition), userspace POST (destroys both cards), macOS published data (runtime-computed, nothing stored). The wall stands as: the SMC orders the CP-family transfer (byte 0x40 at SMC reg 0x80000398, twin reads 0x0/finished, eGPU holds 0x40) and the hardware never performs it on a card that reached Linux cold through the tunnel. No host-visible state explains it.
